denote the averaging idempotent and consider the subalgebra
eH_{c}e\subset H_{c}
. By
[
CH98
, Lem 1.1]
,
eH_{c}e
is a filtered quantization of
\mathbb{C}[\Sigma]
.

The quantization parameter (in
\mathfrak{h}^{*}/W
) of
eH_{c}e
can be recovered from the element
c
as follows. Recall that the nontrivial irreducible representations
\{V_{1},...,V_{n}\}
of
\Gamma
are in bijection with the simple roots
\{\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{n}\}
for
\mathfrak{g}
. Write
\{\omega_{1},...,\omega_{n}\}
for the corresponding fundamental weights and consider the parameter

(4.8.1)

\lambda^{c}:=\frac{1}{|\Gamma|}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathrm{tr}_{V_{i}}(c)\omega_{i}\in\mathfrak{h}^{*}.

By
[
Los16
, Prop 3.17]
, there is an isomorphism of filtered quantizations

(4.8.2)

eH_{c}e\simeq\mathcal{A}_{\lambda^{c}}.

It is worth highlighting several special cases of (
4.8.2
). If
c=|\Gamma|e(=\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma}\gamma)
, then
\mathrm{tr}_{V_{i}}(c)=0
for
1\leq i\leq n
. Hence
\lambda^{|\Gamma|e}=0
and

(4.8.3)

eH_{|\Gamma|e}e\simeq\mathcal{A}_{0}.

If
c=1
, then
\mathrm{tr}_{V_{i}}(c)=\dim(V_{i})
for
1\leq i\leq n
. Hence

(4.8.4)

\lambda^{1}=\frac{1}{|\Gamma|}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\dim(V_{i})\omega_{i}.

The parameter
\lambda^{1}
will play a particularly important role in subsequent sections. We call it the ‘weighted barycenter’ parameter for
\Sigma
. If
\Gamma=\mathbb{Z}_{n+1}
(i.e
\Sigma
is of type
A_{n}
), then
V_{i}
are one-dimensional and
\lambda^{1}
is the usual (unweighted) barycenter of the fundamental alcove for
\mathfrak{g}
.

4.9.
Ample cones and
\mathbb{Q}
-terminalizations

We will now recall some facts from
[
Nam15
]
about the classification of
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial terminalizations. Let
X
be a conical symplectic singularity. Recall, see Section

4.5
, that each partial Namikawa space
\mathfrak{P}_{\mathbb{R},k}
is the real span of a root system, namely
\Delta_{k}^{\pi_{1}(\mathfrak{L}_{k})}
. Let
\mathfrak{P}_{\mathbb{R},k}^{\geq 0}\subset\mathfrak{P}_{\mathbb{R},k}
denote the fundamental chamber, and

\mathfrak{P}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\geq 0}:=\mathfrak{P}_{\mathbb{R},0}\oplus\bigoplus\mathfrak{P}_{\mathbb{R},k}^{\geq 0}.

Note that
\mathfrak{P}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\geq 0}
is a fundamental domain for the
W
-action on
\mathfrak{P}_{\mathbb{R}}
. Following Namikawa (
[
Nam15
, Section 1]
), we will construct a finite set of polyhedral cones (indexed by
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial terminalizations of
X
) which partition
\mathfrak{P}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\geq 0}
.

Let
Y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}X
be a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial terminalization. A line bundle
\mathcal{L}\in\Pic(Y)
is said to be
relatively ample
if it is ample with respect to the projective morphism
Y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}X
. Write
\Pic^{a}(Y)\subset\Pic(Y)
for the semigroup of relatively ample line bundles. We can regard
\Pic^{a}(Y)
as a subset of
\mathfrak{P}_{\mathbb{R}}
via the sequence of maps

(4.9.1)

\Pic^{a}(Y)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Pic(Y^{\mathrm{reg}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Pic(Y^{\mathrm{reg}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{R}\simeq H^{2}(Y^{\mathrm{reg}},\mathbb{R})\simeq\mathfrak{P}_{\mathbb{R}}.

The first map is by restriction, and the isomorphism
\Pic(Y^{\mathrm{reg}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{R}\simeq H^{2}(Y^{\mathrm{reg}},\mathbb{R})
is due to Lemma
4.4.6
. Note that the kernel of the map
\Pic(Y^{\mathrm{reg}})\rightarrow\mathfrak{P}_{\mathbb{R}}
consists of torsion line bundles on
Y^{\mathrm{reg}}
, and no torsion line bundle is relatively ample. Thus, (
4.9.1
) is injective.

The
ample cone
of
Y
is defined to be the
\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
-invariant subset

(4.9.2)

\mathrm{Amp}(Y):=\overline{\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\Pic^{a}(Y)}\subset\mathfrak{P}_{\mathbb{R}}

By
[
BLPW16
, Proposition 2.17]
we have
\mathrm{Amp}(Y)\subset\mathfrak{P}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\geq 0}
.

In fact, the cones
\mathrm{Amp}(Y)
are cut out by a collection of rational hyperplanes in
\mathfrak{P}
which admit an alternative characterization, described below. Recall that for
\lambda\in\mathfrak{P}
we let
X_{\lambda}
(resp.
Y_{\lambda}
) denote the fiber of the universal deformation
X_{\mathrm{univ}}
(resp.
Y_{\mathrm{univ}}
) over
W\lambda
(resp.
\lambda
). As was discussed after Proposition
4.7.7
, we have a projective birational morphism
Y_{\lambda}\rightarrow X_{\lambda}
.
Let
\mathfrak{P}^{\mathrm{sing}}\subset\mathfrak{P}
be the subset consisting of all
\lambda\in\mathfrak{P}
for which this morphism fails to be an isomorphism,
(equivalently, for which
Y_{\lambda}
is not affine).

Theorem 4.9.1

(
[
Nam15
]
, Lem. 1, Main Thm.)
.

The following are true:

(i)

There are finitely many isomorphism classes of
\mathbb{Q}
-terminalizations of
X
.

(ii)

Each
\mathbb{Q}
-terminalization is determined up to isomorphism by its ample cone.

(iii)

The ample cone of each
\mathbb{Q}
-terminalization is polyhedral, and of full dimension in
\mathfrak{P}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\geq 0}
.

(iv)

The ample cones of all
\mathbb{Q}
-terminalizations partition
\mathfrak{P}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\geq 0}
.

(v)

The subset
\mathfrak{P}^{\mathrm{sing}}\subset\mathfrak{P}
is the
W
-stable union of rational hyperplanes in
\mathfrak{P}
, including the walls corresponding to the
W
-action. The ample cones
\mathrm{Amp}(Y)
are cut out by these hyperplanes.

4.10.
Automorphisms

We will need some information about the automorphism groups of Poisson deformations and filtered quantizations of conical symplectic singularities. Let
X
be a conical symplectic singularity so that
\mathbb{C}[X]
is a graded Poisson algebra. Consider the group
\overline{\mathcal{G}}
of graded Poisson automorphisms of
\mathbb{C}[X]
. Since
\mathbb{C}[X]
is positively graded,
\overline{\mathcal{G}}
is an algebraic group. Choose a maximal reductive subgroup
\mathcal{G}\subset\overline{\mathcal{G}}
. Note that
\overline{\mathcal{G}}
acts on
\mathrm{PDef}(X)
by

g\cdot(\mathcal{A}^{0},\theta)=(\mathcal{A}^{0},g\circ\theta),\qquad g\in\overline{\mathcal{G}},\ (\mathcal{A}^{0},\theta)\in\mathrm{PDef}(X).

In a similar manner, one can define a
\overline{\mathcal{G}}
-action on
\mathrm{Quant}(X)
. These actions induce two (a priori distinct)
\overline{\mathcal{G}}
-actions on
\mathfrak{P}/W
via Theorems
4.7.5
and
4.7.6
, called the Poisson and the quantum actions. For example, the Poisson action is defined by regarding
\mathfrak{P}/W
as the parameter space for filtered Poisson deformations of
\mathbb{C}[X]
.

Write
\Aut(\mathcal{A}^{0}_{\lambda})
(resp.
\Aut(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda})
) for the group of filtered Poisson algebra automorphisms of
\mathcal{A}^{0}_{\lambda}
(resp. filtered algebra automorphisms of
\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}^{0}
). Both are algebraic groups. The following result, obtained in
[
Los16
, Sec 3.7]
, describes the relationship between
\Aut(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}^{0})
and
\Aut(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda})
.

Proposition 4.10.1
.

The following are true:

(i)

The Poisson and quantum actions of
\overline{\mathcal{G}}
coincide and are trivial on
\overline{\mathcal{G}}^{\circ}
.

(ii)

Write
\mathcal{G}_{\lambda}
for the stabilizer of
W\lambda
in
\mathcal{G}
. There is an epimorphism

\Aut(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda})\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{\lambda}
with unipotent kernel. It is given by taking the associated graded and then projecting onto the maximal reductive subgroup.

(iii)

Similarly, there is an epimorphism

\Aut(\mathcal{A}^{0}_{\lambda})\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{\lambda}
with unipotent kernel.

Proof.

(i) is
[
Los16
, Prop 3.21]
. The claim that the map defined in (ii) is an epimorphism is easy, compare to the proof of
[
Los16
, Lem 3.19]
. The kernel consists of automorphisms of
\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}
such that the assoiciated graded automorphism is unipotent. Such automorphisms are unipotent. So the kernel is unipotent. The proof of (iii) is the same.

∎

Remark 4.10.2
.

Let
G\subset\mathcal{G}
be a connected reductive subgroup. Thanks
to (i) of Proposition
4.10.1
,
G
acts on
\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}
and
\mathcal{A}^{0}_{\lambda}
by filtered (Poisson) algebra automorphisms. There is a
G
-equivariant filtered algebra isomorphism
\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{A}_{\lambda^{\prime}}
if and only if there is a
G
-equivariant filtered Poisson algebra automorphism
\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}^{0}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{A}_{\lambda^{\prime}}^{0}
. So there is a natural bijection between the following two sets:

•

The set of filtered quantizations of
\mathbb{C}[X]
considered up to
G
-equivariant filtered algebra automorphisms.

•

The set of filtered Poisson deformations of
\mathbb{C}[X]
considered up to
G
-equivariant filtered Poisson algebra isomorphisms.

By (ii) and (iii) of Proposition
4.10.1
,
the maximal reductive subgroups in
\Aut_{G}(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}),\Aut_{G}(\mathcal{A}^{0}_{\lambda})
coincide with
Z_{\mathcal{G}}(G)\cap\mathcal{G}_{\lambda}
.

4.11.
Hamiltonian quantizations

Let
A
be a graded Poisson algebra of the type described in Section
4.1
, and let
G
be an algebraic group. Suppose
G
acts rationally on
A
by graded Poisson automorphisms. The
G
-action gives rise to a Lie algebra homomorphism

\mathfrak{g}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathrm{Der}(A),\qquad\xi\mapsto\xi_{A},

where
\mathrm{Der}(A)
is the Lie algebra consisting of derivations of
A
. We say that
A
is
Hamiltonian
if there is a
G
-equivariant map
\varphi:\mathfrak{g}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}A_{d}
(called a
classical co-moment map
) such that

\{\varphi(\xi),a\}=\xi_{A}(a),\qquad\xi\in\mathfrak{g},\quad a\in A.

Now suppose
(\mathcal{A},\theta)
is a filtered quantization of
A
.
We say that
(\mathcal{A},\theta)
is
G
-
equivariant
if
G
acts rationally on
\mathcal{A}
by filtered algebra automorphisms and the isomorphism
\theta:\gr(\mathcal{A})\xrightarrow{\sim}A
is
G
-equivariant. In this setting (as above) we get a Lie algebra homomorphism

\mathfrak{g}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathrm{Der}(\mathcal{A}),\qquad\xi\mapsto\xi_{\mathcal{A}}.

Definition 4.11.1
.

Suppose
A
is a graded Poisson algebra equipped with a Hamiltonian
G
-action.

•

A
Hamiltonian
quantization of
A
is a triple
(\mathcal{A},\theta,\Phi)
consisting of

(i)

a
G
-equivariant filtered quantization
(\mathcal{A},\theta)
of
A
, and

(ii)

a
G
-equivariant map
\Phi:\mathfrak{g}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathcal{A}_{\leq d}
(called a
quantum co-moment map
) such that

[\Phi(\xi),a]=\xi_{\mathcal{A}}(a),\qquad\xi\in\mathfrak{g},\quad a\in\mathcal{A}.

•

An isomorphism
(\mathcal{A}_{1},\theta_{1},\Phi_{1})\xrightarrow{\sim}(\mathcal{A}_{2},\theta_{2},\Phi_{2})
of Hamiltonian quantizations of
A
is a
G
-equivariant isomorphism of filtered algebras
\phi:\mathcal{A}_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathcal{A}_{2}
such that
\theta_{1}=\theta_{2}\circ\gr(\phi)
and
\Phi_{2}=\phi\circ\Phi_{1}
. Denote the set of isomorphism classes of Hamiltonian quantizations of
A
by
\mathrm{Quant}^{G}(A)
.

Note that if
\mathcal{A}
is a
G
-equivariant quantization and
\Phi:\mathfrak{g}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathcal{A}_{\leq d}
is a quantum co-moment map, then
\gr(\Phi):\mathfrak{g}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}A_{d}
is a classical co-moment map. In the other direction, we have the following result.

Lemma 4.11.2
.

Let
G
be a connected reductive algebraic group, and let
X=\Spec(A)
be a conical symplectic singularity with a Hamiltonian
G
-action. Then the following are true:

(i)

There is a unique classical co-moment map
\varphi:\mathfrak{g}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}A_{d}
.

(ii)

For each
G
-equivariant quantization
\mathcal{A}
of
A
and Lie algebra homomorphism
\chi:\mathfrak{g}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathbb{C}
, there is a unique quantum co-moment map

\Phi_{\chi}:\mathfrak{g}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathcal{A}_{\leq d}

such that
\Phi_{\chi}|_{\mathfrak{z}(\mathfrak{g})}=\chi
.

Proof.

Suppose
\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2}:\mathfrak{g}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}A_{d}
are classical co-moment maps. Then
\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{2}:\mathfrak{g}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}A_{d}
takes values in the Poisson center of
A
. Since
\Spec(A)
is generically symplectic, the Poisson center is
\mathbb{C}=A_{0}
, and therefore
\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{2}=0
. This proves (i).

Now suppose
\mathcal{A}
is a
G
-equivariant quantization of
A
. If
a\in\mathcal{A}_{k}
is central in
\mathcal{A}
, then
\overline{a}\in A_{k}
is Poisson-central in
A
. Thus, the center of
\mathcal{A}
is
\mathbb{C}=\mathcal{A}_{\leq 0}
. Suppose
\Phi_{1},\Phi_{2}:\mathfrak{g}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathcal{A}_{\leq d}
are quantum co-moment maps. Then
\Phi_{1}-\Phi_{2}:\mathfrak{g}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathcal{A}_{\leq d}
is a Lie algebra homomorphism with values in
Z(\mathcal{A})=\mathbb{C}
. That is,
\Phi_{1}
and
\Phi_{2}
differ by a character of
\mathfrak{z}(\mathfrak{g})
. It remains to show that the (unique) classical co-moment map
\varphi:\mathfrak{g}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}A_{d}
lifts to a quantum co-moment
\Phi:\mathfrak{g}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathcal{A}_{\leq d}
. We can assume in the proof that
G
acts faithfully on
A
and so
\mathfrak{g}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Der}(\mathcal{A})
.

First, observe that every derivation
\Delta
of
\mathcal{A}
satisfying
\Delta(\mathcal{A}_{\leq i})\subseteq\mathcal{A}_{\leq k+i}
is of the form
[\widetilde{a},\bullet]
for some
\widetilde{a}\in\mathcal{A}_{\leq k+d}
. Indeed, let
\delta=\gr(\Delta)
, a Poisson derivation of degree
k
. By
[
Los16
, Proposition 2.14]
, all such derivations are inner. Thus,
\delta=\{a_{0},\bullet\}
for some element
a_{0}\in A_{k+d}
. Choose a lift
\widetilde{a}_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{\leq k+d}
of
a_{0}
and let
\Delta^{1}=\Delta-[\widetilde{a}_{0},\bullet]
. Note that
\Delta^{1}
is a filtered derivation of degree
k-1
. Iterating this procedure, we obtain an element
\widetilde{a}\in\mathcal{A}_{\leq k+d}
such that
\Delta-[\widetilde{a},\bullet]
is a filtered derivation of degree
-d-1
. Since the grading on
A
is by nonnegative integers and
\gr(\Delta-[\widetilde{a},\bullet])
is inner,
\gr(\Delta-[\widetilde{a},\bullet])=0
. Thus,
\Delta=[\widetilde{a},\bullet]
, as desired.

There is a
G
-equivariant Lie algebra embedding
\mathcal{A}_{\leqslant d}/\mathcal{A}_{\leqslant 0}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Der}(\mathcal{A})
given by
a\mapsto[a,\cdot]
. By the above argument, the image of
\mathfrak{g}
in
\operatorname{Der}(\mathcal{A})
lies in that of
\mathcal{A}_{\leqslant d}/\mathcal{A}_{\leqslant 0}
. So the map
\xi\mapsto\xi_{\mathcal{A}}
can be viewed as a
G
-equivariant Lie algebra embedding
\mathfrak{g}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{A}_{\leqslant d}/\mathcal{A}_{\leqslant 0}
. Since
G
is reductive, this embedding lifts to a
G
-equivariant (and automatically Lie algebra) embedding
\mathfrak{g}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{A}_{\leqslant d}
. This lift is our quantum comoment map.

∎

If
X
is a conical symplectic singularity with a Hamiltonian
G
-action, we will consider the
extended Namikawa space

\overline{\mathfrak{P}}:=\mathfrak{P}\oplus\mathfrak{z}(\mathfrak{g})^{*}.

This space should be viewed as an equivariant version of the Namikawa space for
X
. There is a natural
W
-action on
\overline{\mathfrak{P}}
defined via the decomposition above (the
W
-action on the second factor is trivial). For each
\lambda=(\lambda^{\prime},\chi)\in\mathfrak{P}\oplus\mathfrak{z}(\mathfrak{g})^{*}=\overline{\mathfrak{P}}
, there is an associated Hamiltonian quantization
(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda^{\prime}},\Phi_{\chi})
, where
\mathcal{A}_{\lambda^{\prime}}
is the filtered quantization of
A
considered in Theorem
4.7.6
and
\Phi_{\chi}:\mathfrak{g}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathcal{A}_{\lambda^{\prime}}
is the quantum co-moment map considered in Lemma
4.11.2
(ii). For notational convenience, we will often write
(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda},\Phi_{\lambda}):=(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda^{\prime}},\Phi_{\chi})
. The following is an easy consequence of Theorem
4.7.6
and Lemma
4.11.2
.

Proposition 4.11.3
.

In the setting of Lemma
4.11.2
, there is a bijection

\overline{\mathfrak{P}}/W\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Quant}^{G}(X),\qquad W\cdot\lambda\mapsto(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda},\Phi_{\lambda}).

4.12.
Harish-Chandra bimodules for filtered quantizations

Let
A
be a graded Poisson algebra and let
\mathcal{A}
be a filtered quantization of
A
. Let
\mathcal{B}
be an
\mathcal{A}
-bimodule. A
compatible filtration
on
\mathcal{B}
is an increasing filtration by subspaces

0=\mathcal{B}_{-1}\subseteq\mathcal{B}_{0}\subseteq\mathcal{B}_{1}\subseteq...,\qquad\bigcup_{i=0}^{\infty}\mathcal{B}_{i}=\mathcal{B}

such that

\mathcal{A}_{\leq i}\mathcal{B}_{\leq j}\subseteq\mathcal{B}_{\leq i+j}\quad\text{and}\quad[\mathcal{A}_{\leq i},\mathcal{B}_{\leq j}]\subseteq\mathcal{B}_{\leq i+j-d},\qquad\forall i,j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}.

Under these conditions,
\gr(\mathcal{B})
has the structure of a graded
A
-module. A compatible filtration is
good
if
\gr(\mathcal{B})
is finitely-generated for
A
.

Definition 4.12.1
.

A
Harish-Chandra bimodule
for
\mathcal{A}
is an
\mathcal{A}
-bimodule which admits a good filtration. A morphism of Harish-Chandra bimodules is a homomorphism of
\mathcal{A}
-bimodules. Denote the category of Harish-Chandra bimodules for
\mathcal{A}
by
\HC(\mathcal{A})
.

Note that if
\mathcal{A}=U(\mathfrak{g})
, for
\mathfrak{g}
a reductive Lie algebra, we recover Definition
3.4.1
. For
\mathcal{B}\in\HC(\mathcal{A})
, we can define the associated variety
\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{B})\subset\Spec(A)
and generic multiplicities
m_{Z}(\mathcal{B})\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
as in the Lie-theoretic context (see the remarks following Definition
3.4.1
). We say that
\mathcal{B}
has
full support
if
\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{B})=\Spec(A)
. Note that the regular bimodule
\mathcal{A}
is Harish-Chandra and satisfies this condition. Write
\HC_{\partial}(\mathcal{A})\subset\HC(\mathcal{A})
for the Serre subcategory of Harish-Chandra bimodules which are
not
of full support. Note that
\HC(\mathcal{A})
is a monoidal category (with respect to
\otimes_{\mathcal{A}}
) and
\HC_{\partial}(\mathcal{A})
is a monoidal ideal (see
[
Los21
, Lem 2.13]
). Consider the quotient category

\overline{\HC}(\mathcal{A})=\HC(\mathcal{A})/\HC_{\partial}(\mathcal{A}).

We call
\overline{\HC}(\mathcal{A})
the category of Harish-Chandra bimodules with full support for
\mathcal{A}
. It is a monoidal category because
\HC_{\partial}(\mathcal{A})
is a monoidal ideal.

Lemma 4.12.2
.

If the algebra
\mathcal{A}
is simple, then
\HC_{\partial}(\mathcal{A})=0
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{B}\in\HC_{\partial}(\mathcal{A})
, and consider the algebra
\End_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{B})
of left
\mathcal{A}
-module endomorphisms of
\mathcal{B}
.
There is a natural filtration on
\End_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{B})
defined by

\End_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{B})_{\leq i}:=\{f\in\End_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{B})\mid f(\mathcal{B}_{\leq k})\subseteq\mathcal{B}_{\leq k+i}\quad\forall k\in\mathbb{Z}\}

It is straightfoward to check that the above filtration is good. In particular,
\End_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{B})\in\HC(\mathcal{A})
. There is a natural filtered algebra homomorphism

\varphi:\mathcal{A}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\End_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{B})^{\mathrm{op}},\qquad\varphi(a)(b)=ba.

Note that
\mathcal{V}(\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{B})^{\mathrm{op}})\subseteq\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{B})\subsetneq\Spec(A)=\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{A})
. Thus,
\ker{(\varphi)}=\mathrm{RAnn}(\mathcal{B})
is a nonzero two-sided ideal in
\mathcal{A}
. Since
\mathcal{A}
is simple, this implies that
\mathrm{RAnn}(\mathcal{B})=\mathcal{A}
, and hence that
\mathcal{B}=0
.
∎

Remark 4.12.3
.

Similarly, if
\mathcal{D}
is a sheaf quantization of a Poisson variety
X
(cf. Definition
4.1.4
), it makes sense to consider Harish-Chandra bimodules for
\mathcal{D}
. This category will be denoted by
\HC(\mathcal{D})
.

4.13.
Restriction and extension functors for Harish-Chandra bimodules

Let
X
be a conical symplectic singularity and let
\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}
be a quantization of
\mathbb{C}[X]
. Let
\Gamma
be the algebraic fundamental group of the regular locus
X^{\mathrm{reg}}\subset X
. Recall from Section
4.6
that
\Gamma
is the maximal finite quotient of
\pi_{1}(X^{\mathrm{reg}})
. There is a close relationship between Harish-Chandra bimodules with full support for
\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}
and (finite-dimensional) representations of
\Gamma
, which we will now describe.

In
[
Los21
, Sec 4]
, the first-named author constructs an exact monoidal functor

\bullet_{\dagger}:\HC(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Gamma\modd.

The image of
\mathcal{B}\in\HC(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda})
under this functor is characterized by the following property: let
\widehat{X}^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}X^{\mathrm{reg}}
be the universal algebraic cover, i.e. the unique Galois cover with Galois group
\Gamma
. Then for
\mathcal{B}\in\HC(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda})
(and any good filtration), there is an isomorphism of Poisson coherent sheaves on
X^{\mathrm{reg}}

(4.13.1)

(\gr\mathcal{B})|_{X^{\mathrm{reg}}}\simeq\left(\mathcal{B}_{\dagger}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{X}^{\prime}}\right)^{\Gamma}.

We will give a formal construction of
\bullet_{\dagger}
towards the end of this section.

From (
4.13.1
) it follows that
\ker{(\bullet_{\dagger})}=\HC_{\partial}(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda})
. Thus,
\bullet_{\dagger}
descends to an embedding

\bullet_{\dagger}:\overline{\HC}(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda})\hookrightarrow\Gamma\modd.

This embedding is full and monoidal, and its image in
\Gamma\modd
is closed under taking direct summands, see
[
Los21
, Lem 4.6]
. Thus,
\mathrm{Im}(\bullet_{\dagger})=\Gamma/\Gamma(\lambda)\modd
for a normal subgroup
\Gamma(\lambda)\subseteq\Gamma
, which is uniquely determined by
\lambda
. We will provide a more explicit description of
\Gamma(\lambda)
in Section
4.15
.

In
[
Los21
, Sec 4.4]
, it is shown that
\bullet_{\dagger}
admits a right adjoint

\bullet^{\dagger}:\Gamma\modd\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\HC(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}).

For any
\mathcal{B}\in\HC(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda})
, the kernel and cokernel of the unit homomorphism
\mathcal{B}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}(\mathcal{B}_{\dagger})^{\dagger}
are supported on
X^{\mathrm{sing}}
, see
[
Los21
, Lem 4.6]
. Hence,
\bullet^{\dagger}:\Gamma\modd\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\overline{\HC}(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda})
is a left inverse for
\bullet_{\dagger}:\overline{\HC}(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Gamma\modd
, and there are quasi-inverse equivalences

(4.13.2)

\bullet_{\dagger}:\overline{\HC}(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda})\xrightarrow{\sim}\Gamma/\Gamma(\lambda)\modd,\qquad\bullet^{\dagger}:\Gamma/\Gamma(\lambda)\modd\xrightarrow{\sim}\overline{\HC}(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}).

Next, we will give constructions of
\bullet_{\dagger}
and
\bullet^{\dagger}
, following
[
Los21
, Section 4]
with some modifications. Consider the universal cover
\widehat{X}^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}X^{\mathrm{reg}}
, and let
\widehat{X}=\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\widehat{X}^{\prime}])
. The Poisson variety
\widehat{X}
is a finite Galois cover of
X
in the sense of Section
4.6
. As we mentioned there, it is a conical symplectic singularity. Consider the étale lift of
\mathcal{D}^{X^{\mathrm{reg}}}_{\lambda}
to
\widehat{X}^{\prime}
, compare to

[
Los21
, Section 2.6]
. Denote the resulting sheaf of algebras by
\widehat{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}
. It has an action of
\Gamma
by filtered algebra automorphisms with
(\widehat{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime})^{\Gamma}\simeq\mathcal{D}^{X^{\mathrm{reg}}}_{\lambda}
. Consider the category of
\Gamma
-equivariant Harish-Chandra bimodules over
\widehat{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}
, denoted
\HC^{\Gamma}(\widehat{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime})
.
Taking
\Gamma
-invariants defines an equivalence of categories

\HC^{\Gamma}(\widehat{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime})\rightarrow\HC(\mathcal{D}^{X^{\mathrm{reg}}}_{\lambda})
, compare to the penultimate paragraph in
[
Los21
, Section 4.2]
. There is a full embedding
\Gamma\operatorname{-mod}\hookrightarrow\HC^{\Gamma}(\widehat{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime})

given by
V\mapsto\widehat{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}\otimes V
. This embedding is an equivalence: using a
\Gamma
-invariant regluing as in
[
Los21
, Section 4.2]
we get an equivalence of categories
\HC^{\Gamma}(\widehat{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime})\xrightarrow{\sim}\HC^{\Gamma}(\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{0}_{\lambda^{1}})
, where
\HC^{\Gamma}(\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{0}_{\lambda^{1}})
is as defined in
[
Los21
, Section 4.2]
. Furthermore,
\HC^{\Gamma}(\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{0}_{\lambda^{1}})
is equivalent to
\Gamma\operatorname{-mod}
, see
[
Los21
, Section 4.2]
. The functor
\bullet^{\dagger}:\Gamma\operatorname{-mod}\rightarrow\HC(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda})

is then given by

(4.13.3)

V\mapsto\Gamma(\widehat{X}^{\prime},(\widehat{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}\otimes V)^{\Gamma}).

This construction is equivalent to that of
[
Los21
, Section 4]
. There the equivalence

\Gamma\operatorname{-mod}\xrightarrow{\sim}\HC(\mathcal{D}|_{X^{\mathrm{reg}}})
was the composition

(4.13.4)

\Gamma\operatorname{-mod}\xrightarrow{\sim}\HC^{\Gamma}(\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{0}_{\lambda^{1}})\xrightarrow{\sim}\HC((\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{0}_{\lambda^{1}})^{\Gamma})\xrightarrow{\sim}\HC(\mathcal{D}|_{X^{\mathrm{reg}}}),

where the third equivalence is regluing. Here we use the composition

(4.13.5)

\Gamma\operatorname{-mod}\xrightarrow{\sim}\HC^{\Gamma}(\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{0}_{\lambda^{1}})\xrightarrow{\sim}\HC^{\Gamma}(\widehat{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime})\xrightarrow{\sim}\HC(\mathcal{D}|_{X^{\mathrm{reg}}}),

where the second equivalence is regluing. Since we are regluing with the same cocycles, (
4.13.4
) and (
4.13.5
) define isomorphic tensor functors.

The functor
\bullet_{\dagger}
can be constructed as the composition of the microlocalization functor
\HC(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda})\rightarrow\HC(\mathcal{D}|_{X^{\mathrm{reg}}})

and the inverse of the composition (
4.13.5
). This construction shows that
\bullet_{\dagger}
is tensor, since it is the composition of tensor functors.

Finally, it is evident from the constructions that
\bullet^{\dagger}
and
\bullet_{\dagger}
respect good filtrations. For example, a good filtration on
\mathcal{B}\in\HC(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda})
gives rise to a filtration on
\mathcal{B}_{\dagger}
. To see this, we note that the microlocalization and global section functors as well as all equivalences in (
4.13.5
) preserve good filtrations (and the functors (
4.13.5
) are equivalences of the categories of filtered objects).

4.14.
Finite covers of filtered quantizations

Let
X
be a conical symplectic singularity and let
\mathcal{A}^{X}=\mathcal{A}^{X}_{\lambda}
be a filtered quantization of
\mathbb{C}[X]
(here we introduce our convention, used throughout the paper, of writing the underlying variety of a filtered quantization as a superscript). Let
p:\widetilde{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}X
be a finite cover, as defined in Section
4.6
.

Definition 4.14.1
.

A
cover of

\mathcal{A}^{X}
(with respect to the map
p:\widetilde{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}X
) is
a filtered quantization
\mathcal{A}^{\widetilde{X}}
of
\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{X}]
which admits a filtered algebra embedding
\mathcal{A}^{X}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{A}^{\widetilde{X}}
such that the associated graded homomorphism is the pullback map
\mathbb{C}[X]\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{X}]
.

Recall the normal subgroup
\Gamma(\lambda)\subset\Gamma
defined in Section
4.13
, and consider the Galois cover
\breve{X}
of
X
with Galois group
\Gamma/\Gamma(\lambda)
. Let
\breve{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}
denote the étale lift of
\mathcal{D}|_{X^{\mathrm{reg}}}
to
\breve{X}^{\prime}
, and let
\breve{\mathcal{A}}:=\Gamma(\breve{X}^{\prime},\breve{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime})
. Note that
\Gamma/\Gamma(\lambda)
acts on

\breve{\mathcal{A}}
and
\breve{\mathcal{A}}^{\Gamma/\Gamma(\lambda)}

is identified with
\mathcal{A}^{X}
. Also note that we have a natural inclusion
\gr\breve{\mathcal{A}}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}[\breve{X}]
. This inclusion may fail to be an isomorphism, so
\breve{\mathcal{A}}
may fail to be a cover of
\mathcal{A}
.

The following lemma explains the relationship between
\breve{\mathcal{A}}
and
\bullet^{\dagger}
.

Lemma 4.14.2
.

The following are true:

(i)

For
V\in\Gamma/\Gamma(\lambda)\operatorname{-mod}
, we have
V^{\dagger}=(\breve{\mathcal{A}}\otimes V)^{\Gamma/\Gamma(\lambda)}
.

(ii)

The natural morphism

\breve{X}\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(\gr\breve{\mathcal{A}})
is an isomorphism over
X^{\mathrm{reg}}
.

Proof.

In the notation of (
4.13.3
), there is an isomorphism of filtered quantizations of
\breve{X}^{\prime}
,
\breve{\mathcal{D}}\simeq\widehat{\mathcal{D}}^{\Gamma(\lambda)}
. Now (i) follows from (
4.13.3
).

Next we prove (ii). Since the morphism in question is finite and étale over
X^{\mathrm{reg}}
, it suffices to show that the generic degree of
\operatorname{Spec}(\gr\breve{\mathcal{A}})
over
X
is
|\Gamma/\Gamma(\lambda)|
. By (
4.13.1
), it is enough to show that

(4.14.1)

\dim\breve{\mathcal{A}}_{\dagger}=|\Gamma/\Gamma(\lambda)|.

Thanks to (i),
\breve{\mathcal{A}}=V^{\dagger}
for
V=\mathbb{C}[\Gamma/\Gamma(\lambda)]
.
Now (
4.14.1
) follows from (
4.13.2
).
∎

The next proposition relates covers of
\mathcal{A}
and their categories of Harish-Chandra bimodules to
\breve{\mathcal{A}}
.

Proposition 4.14.3
.

Let
\mathcal{A}^{\widetilde{X}}
be a cover of
\mathcal{A}^{X}
. The following are true:

(i)

There is a subgroup
\Gamma^{0}\subset\Gamma/\Gamma(\lambda)
with the following properties:

\bullet

\widetilde{X}\simeq\breve{X}/\Gamma^{0}
.

\bullet

There is an isomorphism of filtered algebras
\mathcal{A}^{\widetilde{X}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\breve{\mathcal{A}}^{\Gamma^{0}}
with associated graded equal to the natural isomorphism
\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{X}]\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{C}[\breve{X}]^{\Gamma^{0}}
.

(ii)

There is an equivalence of categories
\Gamma^{0}\operatorname{-mod}\xrightarrow{\sim}\overline{\HC}(\mathcal{A}^{\widetilde{X}})
given by
V\mapsto(\breve{\mathcal{A}}\otimes V)^{\Gamma^{0}}
.

Proof.

First we prove (i). Let
\Gamma_{0}
be a subgroup in
\Gamma
(defined up to conjugacy) such that
\widetilde{X}\simeq\widehat{X}/\Gamma_{0}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}\simeq\widehat{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\Gamma_{0}}
. It follows that
\mathcal{A}^{\widetilde{X}}_{\dagger}\simeq\mathbb{C}[\Gamma/\Gamma_{0}]
.
The action of
\Gamma
on the image of every Harish-Chandra
\mathcal{A}^{X}
-bimodule under
\bullet_{\dagger}
factors through
\Gamma/\Gamma(\lambda)
by the construction of
\Gamma(\lambda)
in Section
4.13
. Thus
\Gamma(\lambda)\subset\Gamma_{0}
. Set
\Gamma^{0}=\Gamma_{0}/\Gamma(\lambda)
. We wish to establish an isomorphism
\mathcal{A}^{\widetilde{X}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\breve{\mathcal{A}}^{\Gamma^{0}}
. We begin by exhibiting a filtered algebra isomorphism

(4.14.2)

\mathcal{A}^{\widetilde{X}}_{\dagger}\xrightarrow{\sim}(\breve{\mathcal{A}}^{\Gamma^{0}})_{\dagger}.

Note that both sides carry natural filtered algebra structures. This is evident from the construction of
\bullet_{\dagger}
at the end of Section
4.13
. The left hand side comes with a filtration with the property that there is a graded
\Gamma
-equivariant algebra isomorphism
\gr(\mathcal{A}^{\widetilde{X}}_{\dagger})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{C}[\Gamma/\Gamma_{0}]
, where the target is concentrated in degree
0
. It follows that there is a filtered algebra isomorphism
\mathcal{A}^{\widetilde{X}}_{\dagger}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{C}[\Gamma/\Gamma_{0}]
. Similarly,
\breve{\mathcal{A}}_{\dagger}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{C}[\Gamma/\Gamma(\lambda)]
and hence
(\breve{\mathcal{A}}^{\Gamma^{0}})_{\dagger}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{C}[\Gamma/\Gamma_{0}]
. This proves (
4.14.2
).

Next we will show that (
4.14.2
) implies
\mathcal{A}^{\widetilde{X}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\breve{\mathcal{A}}^{\Gamma^{0}}
. Indeed, by the construction of
\bullet_{\dagger}
, (
4.14.2
) comes from a filtered algebra isomorphism between the microlocalizations of
\mathcal{A}^{\widetilde{X}},\breve{\mathcal{A}}^{\Gamma^{0}}
to
X^{\mathrm{reg}}
. Passing to the global sections of the microlocalizations, we get a filtered algebra isomorphism
\mathcal{A}^{\widetilde{X}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\breve{\mathcal{A}}^{\Gamma^{0}}
. This completes the proof of (i).

Finally we prove (ii). Let
\widetilde{\Gamma}\subset\Gamma^{0}
be the normal subgroup defined analogously to
\Gamma(\lambda)
so that

\Gamma_{0}/\widetilde{\Gamma}\operatorname{-mod}\xrightarrow{\sim}\overline{\HC}(\mathcal{A}^{\widetilde{X}})
, and let
\bullet^{\widetilde{\dagger}},\bullet_{\widetilde{\dagger}}
denote the equivalences. (ii) is equivalent to the equality
\widetilde{\Gamma}=\Gamma(\lambda)
.

Every Harish-Chandra bimodule for
\mathcal{A}^{\widetilde{X}}
is also a Harish-Chandra bimodule for
\mathcal{A}^{X}
, and a good filtration for
\mathcal{A}^{\widetilde{X}}
is also a good filtration for
\mathcal{A}^{X}
. Set
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}:=\mathbb{C}[\Gamma_{0}/\widetilde{\Gamma}]^{\widetilde{\dagger}}
. Note that
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}
comes with a natural good filtration and
\gr(\widetilde{\mathcal{B}})
embeds into
\mathbb{C}[\widehat{X}]^{\widetilde{\Gamma}}

with cokernel supported on
X^{\mathrm{sing}}
. It follows that
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\dagger}=\mathbb{C}[\Gamma/\widetilde{\Gamma}]
, and so
\Gamma(\lambda)\subset\widetilde{\Gamma}
. On the other hand,
\breve{\mathcal{A}}
is a Harish-Chandra
\mathcal{A}^{\widetilde{X}}
-bimodule with

\breve{\mathcal{A}}_{\widetilde{\dagger}}=\mathbb{C}[\Gamma_{0}/\Gamma(\lambda)]
. So

\Gamma(\lambda)\supset\widetilde{\Gamma}
. This finishes the proof of (ii).
∎

Combining Lemma
4.14.2
and
Proposition
4.14.3
we deduce the following result, which we use repeatedly throughout.

Corollary 4.14.4
.

Let
\mathcal{A}^{\widetilde{X}}
be a cover of
\mathcal{A}^{X}
and assume
\overline{\HC}(\mathcal{A}^{\widetilde{X}})\simeq\Vect
. Then

(i)

\widetilde{X}\simeq\breve{X}

and
\mathcal{A}^{\widetilde{X}}\simeq\breve{\mathcal{A}}
. In particular,
\widetilde{X}
is a Galois cover of
X
with Galois group
\Gamma/\Gamma(\lambda)
, and the action of
\Gamma/\Gamma(\lambda)
on
\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{X}]
lifts to
\mathcal{A}^{\widetilde{X}}
.

(ii)

The equivalence

\bullet^{\dagger}:\Gamma/\Gamma(\lambda)\modd\xrightarrow{\sim}\overline{\HC}(\mathcal{A}^{X})

is given by
V\mapsto(\mathcal{A}^{\widetilde{X}}\otimes V)^{\Gamma/\Gamma(\lambda)}
.

4.15.
Normal subgroup
\Gamma(\lambda)\subset\Gamma

Let
X
be a conical symplectic singularity and let
\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}
be a filtered quantization of
\mathbb{C}[X]
. In this section, we will give a description of the normal subgroup
\Gamma(\lambda)\subset\Gamma
, following
[
Los21
]
.

First, suppose
X=\mathbb{C}^{2}/\Gamma
is a Kleinian singularity. Let
\mathfrak{g}
be the complex simple Lie algebra corresponding to
\Gamma
(cf. Section
4.2
). Fix a Cartan sublagbera
\mathfrak{h}\subset\mathfrak{g}
, and let
\Lambda_{r}
,
\Lambda
,
W^{a}=\Lambda_{r}\rtimes W
, and
W^{ae}=\Lambda\rtimes W
denote the root lattice, weight lattice, affine Weyl group, and extended affine Weyl group, respectively. Let
\Gamma_{\mathcal{N}}
denote the fundamental group of the principal nilpotent orbit in
\mathfrak{g}^{*}
. There is a well-known isomorphism
\Gamma/[\Gamma,\Gamma]\simeq\Gamma_{\mathcal{N}}
, see e.g.
[
Los21
, Sec 5.1]
. Write
\mathfrak{X}(H)
for the characters of a group
H
and
G^{\mathrm{sc}}
for the connected simply connected group corresponding to
\mathfrak{g}
. Then

\Gamma_{\mathcal{N}}\simeq Z(G^{\mathrm{sc}})\simeq\mathfrak{X}(\Lambda/\Lambda_{r})\simeq\mathfrak{X}(W^{ae}/W^{a}),

Hence, there is an isomorphism

(4.15.1)

\mathfrak{X}(\Gamma)\simeq W^{ae}/W^{a}.

If
\lambda\in\mathfrak{h}^{*}
, write
W^{ae}_{\lambda}
(resp.
W^{a}_{\lambda}
) for the stabilizer of
\lambda
under the natural action of
W^{ae}
(resp.
W^{a}
) on
\mathfrak{h}^{*}
.

Proposition 4.15.1

(
[
Los21
]
, Prop 5.3)
.

Let
X
be a Kleinian singularity. Then
\mathfrak{X}(\Gamma/\Gamma(\lambda))\subset\mathfrak{X}(\Gamma)
corresponds to
W^{ae}_{\lambda}/W^{a}_{\lambda}\subset W^{ae}/W^{a}
under the isomorphism (
4.15.1
).

If
\Gamma
is not of type
E_{8}
, Proposition
4.15.2
can be used to compute
\Gamma(\lambda)
, see
[
Los21
, Section 5]
. This result is rather technical and will not be used in this paper, so we omit it.

In some sense, the general case can be reduced to the case of Kleinian singularities. Indeed, let
X
be an arbitrary conical symplectic singularity, and
\mathfrak{L}_{1},...,\mathfrak{L}_{t}\subset X
the codimension 2 leaves. For each
\mathfrak{L}_{k}
, there is a Kleinian singularity
\Sigma_{k}=\mathbb{C}^{2}/\Gamma_{k}
, see Section
4.5
, and a group homomorphism
\phi_{k}:\Gamma_{k}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Gamma
, see Section
4.6
. The quantization parameter
\lambda\in\mathfrak{P}
determines an element
\lambda_{k}\in\mathfrak{P}_{k}\simeq(\mathfrak{h}_{k}^{*})^{\pi_{1}(\mathfrak{L}_{k})}
via the restriciton map
\mathfrak{P}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathfrak{P}_{k}
, see Proposition
4.5.1
, and this element defines a normal subgroup
\Gamma_{k}(\lambda_{k})\subset\Gamma_{k}
.

Theorem 4.15.2

(
[
Los21
]
, Thm 6.1)
.

\Gamma(\lambda)
is the smallest normal subgroup of
\Gamma
containing
\bigcup_{k=1}^{t}\phi_{k}(\Gamma_{k}(\lambda_{k}))
.

5.
Canonical quantizations of conical symplectic singularities

Let
X
be a conical symplectic singularity, and let
\mathfrak{P}
denote the associated (complex) Namikawa space. For each
\lambda\in\mathfrak{P}
, write
\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}
for the corresponding filtered quantization of
\mathbb{C}[X]
, see
Section
4.7
.

Definition 5.0.1
.

The
canonical quantization
of
\mathbb{C}[X]
is the filtered quantization
\mathcal{A}_{0}
.

Canonical quantizations of nilpotent covers are closely related to our main objects of study—unipotent ideals and unipotent bimodules, to be defined in Section
6
. In this section, we will establish some general facts about canonical quantizations for use in later sections. For many of these results, we will impose the following condition on
X

(5.0.1)

X\text{ does not contain a}\text{ 2-dimensional formal slice of type }E_{8}

For our purposes, this is a relatively harmless assumption. For nilpotent covers, (
5.0.1
) is satisfied in all but one case (the principal orbit in type
E_{8}
). This isolated exception can be handled separately by classical methods.

5.1.
Canonical quantizations

First, we study the group
\Aut(\mathcal{A}_{0})
of filtered algebra automorphisms of
\mathcal{A}_{0}
. As in Section
4.10
, let
\mathcal{G}
denote the reductive part of the group of graded Poisson automorphisms of
\mathbb{C}[X]
.

Proposition 5.1.1
.

The
\mathcal{G}
-action on
\mathbb{C}[X]
lifts to a
\mathcal{G}
-action on
\mathcal{A}_{0}
by filtered algebra automorphisms. Furthermore,
\mathcal{G}\subset\Aut(\mathcal{A}_{0})
is a maximal reductive subgroup.

Proof.

The action on
\mathcal{G}
on
\mathfrak{P}/W
(see Proposition
4.10.1
(i))
stabilizes
0
. The assertions follow from Proposition
4.10.1
(ii).
∎

Next, we describe the normal subgroup
\Gamma(0)\subset\Gamma
defined in Section
4.13
. Let
\mathfrak{L}_{1},...,\mathfrak{L}_{t}\subset X
be the codimension 2 leaves. Recall that for each
\mathfrak{L}_{k}
there is a group homomorphism
\phi_{k}:\Gamma_{k}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Gamma
, see (
4.6.1
).

Proposition 5.1.2
.

Suppose
X
has property (
5.0.1
). Then
\Gamma(0)\subset\Gamma
is the minimal normal subgroup containing
\phi_{k}(\Gamma_{k})
for all
k
.

Proof.

By Theorem
4.15.2
, it suffices to show that
\Gamma_{k}(0)=\Gamma_{k}
for
1\leq k\leq t
. By Proposition
4.15.1
, the characters of
\Gamma_{k}/\Gamma_{k}(0)
are in bijection with
W^{ae}_{0}/W^{a}_{0}=W/W=1
. Thus,
\Gamma_{k}/\Gamma_{k}(0)
has no nontrivial characters. Because
X
satisfies (
5.0.1
), we have that
\Gamma_{k}
, and hence
\Gamma_{k}/\Gamma_{k}(0)
, is solvable. A solvable group with no nontrivial characters is trivial.
∎

Remark 5.1.3
.

The purpose of this remark is to explain the special role of the quantization parameter
0\in\mathfrak{P}
.
Let
Y
be a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial terminalization of
X
, and consider the set of isomorphism classes of graded formal quantizations of
Y
(for definitions, see e.g.
[
Los12
, Sec 2.2]
). By
[
Los12
, Cor 2.3.3]
, this set is in natural bijection with
\mathfrak{P}
.
Recall that a graded formal quantization
\mathcal{D}_{\hbar}
comes with a lattice
\mathcal{D}_{\hbar^{d}}
over
\mathbb{C}[[\hbar^{d}]]
. We say that
\mathcal{D}_{\hbar}
is
even
if there is a graded anti-automorphism
\mathcal{D}_{\hbar}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathcal{D}_{\hbar}
which restricts to an involution of
\mathcal{D}_{\hbar^{d}}
sending
\hbar^{d}
to
-\hbar^{d}
. It follows from the results of
[
Los12
, Sec 2.3]
that the graded formal quantization of
Y
corresponding to the parameter
0\in\mathfrak{P}
is the unique even such.

5.2.
Invariants in canonical quantizations: case of Kleinian singularities

Suppose
\widetilde{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}X
is a finite Galois cover of conical symplectic singularities (cf. Section
4.14
). The Galois group
\Pi
acts on
\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{X}]
by graded Poisson automorphisms and
\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{X}]^{\Pi}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{C}[X]
. By Proposition
4.10.1
, the
\Pi
-action lifts to the canonical quantization
\mathcal{A}_{0}^{\widetilde{X}}
of
\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{X}]
. The algebra of invariants
(\mathcal{A}_{0}^{\widetilde{X}})^{\Pi}
has the structure of a filtered quantiation of
X
. It is important to note that this quantization is typically not canonical. In this section and the next, we will compute its quantization parameter. First, we handle the case of Kleinian singularities.

Let
\Sigma=\mathbb{C}^{2}/\Gamma
be a Kleinian singularity, let
\Gamma^{\prime}
be a normal subgroup of
\Gamma
, and consider the induced Galois cover

\Sigma^{\prime}:=\mathbb{C}^{2}/\Gamma^{\prime}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{C}^{2}/\Gamma=\Sigma

The Galois group
\Pi:=\Gamma/\Gamma^{\prime}
acts on
\mathbb{C}[\Sigma^{\prime}]
by graded Poisson automorphisms and
\mathbb{C}[\Sigma^{\prime}]^{\Pi}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{C}[\Sigma]
. By Proposition
4.10.1
, the
\Pi
-action lifts to the canonical quantization
\mathcal{A}_{0}^{\Sigma^{\prime}}
of
\mathbb{C}[\Sigma^{\prime}]
. The algebra of invariants
(\mathcal{A}_{0}^{\Sigma^{\prime}})^{\Pi}
has the structure of a filtered quantization of
\mathbb{C}[\Sigma]
. We will compute its quantization parameter.

Let
\mathfrak{g}
be the complex simple Lie algebra corresponding to
\Gamma
, see Section
4.2
. Choose a Cartan subalgebra
\mathfrak{h}\subset\mathfrak{g}
. Let
\{V_{1},...,V_{n}\}
be the nontrivial irreducible representations of
\Gamma
and let
\{\omega_{1},...,\omega_{n}\}\subset\mathfrak{h}^{*}
be the corresponding fundamental weights. Define a dominant weight
\epsilon(\Gamma^{\prime})\in\mathfrak{h}^{*}
by the formula

(5.2.1)

\epsilon(\Gamma^{\prime}):=\frac{|\Gamma^{\prime}|}{|\Gamma|}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\dim(V_{i}^{\Gamma^{\prime}})\ \omega_{i}.

Proposition 5.2.1
.

There is an isomorphism of filtered quantizations

(\mathcal{A}_{0}^{\Sigma^{\prime}})^{\Pi}\simeq\mathcal{A}_{\epsilon(\Gamma^{\prime})}^{\Sigma}.

Proof.

Let
H=\mathbb{C}[x,y]\#\Gamma/(xy-yx-|\Gamma^{\prime}|e^{\prime})
and
H^{\prime}=\mathbb{C}[x,y]\#\Gamma^{\prime}/(xy-yx-|\Gamma^{\prime}|e^{\prime})
, where
e^{\prime}\in\mathbb{C}[\Gamma^{\prime}]
is the averaging idempotent. Note that
H^{\prime}
embeds as a subalgebra in
H
and
\Pi
acts on
e^{\prime}H^{\prime}e^{\prime}
by algebra automorphisms. If
z\in(e^{\prime}H^{\prime}e^{\prime})^{\Pi}
, then
ze\in eHe
, and the map
z\mapsto ze
defines a filtered algebra homomorphism
(e^{\prime}H^{\prime}e^{\prime})^{\Pi}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}eHe
. Its associated graded homomorphism is the natural identification

\mathbb{C}[\Sigma^{\prime}]^{\Pi}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{C}[\Sigma].

Thus,
(e^{\prime}H^{\prime}e^{\prime})^{\Pi}\simeq eHe
as filtered quantizations. Now we have isomorphisms of filtered quantizations

(\mathcal{A}_{0}^{\Sigma^{\prime}})^{\Pi}\simeq(e^{\prime}H^{\prime}e^{\prime})^{\Pi}\simeq eHe\simeq\mathcal{A}_{\lambda^{e^{\prime}}}^{\Sigma},

where
\lambda^{e^{\prime}}\in\mathfrak{h}^{*}
is the parameter defined in Section
4.8
. The first isomorphism is by (
4.8.3
), and the third is by (
4.8.2
). It remains to show that
\lambda^{e^{\prime}}=\epsilon(\Gamma^{\prime})
. If
V^{\prime}
is an irreducible representation of
\Gamma^{\prime}
, then

\mathrm{tr}_{V^{\prime}}(e^{\prime})=\begin{cases}1&V^{\prime}\simeq\mathbb{C}\\
0&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

Hence,

\mathrm{tr}_{V_{i}}(e^{\prime})=|\Gamma^{\prime}|\dim(V_{i}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}),\qquad 1\leq i\leq n,

and therefore

\langle\lambda^{e^{\prime}},\alpha_{i}^{\vee}\rangle=\frac{1}{|\Gamma|}\mathrm{tr}_{V_{i}}(e^{\prime})=\frac{|\Gamma^{\prime}|}{|\Gamma|}\dim(V_{i}^{\Gamma^{\prime}})=\langle\epsilon(\Gamma^{\prime}),\alpha_{i}^{\vee}\rangle,\qquad 1\leq i\leq n.

This completes the proof.
∎

Example 5.2.2
.

Suppose
\Sigma^{\prime}=\mathbb{C}^{2}
, so that
\Gamma^{\prime}=1
. Then
\epsilon(1)
is the weighted barycenter parameter
\lambda^{1}\in\mathfrak{h}^{*}
defined in Section
4.8
:

\epsilon(1)=\lambda^{1}=\frac{1}{|\Gamma|}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\dim(V_{i})\ \omega_{i}\in\mathfrak{h}^{*}.

5.3.
Invariants in canonical quantizations: general case

Fix
p:\widetilde{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}X
and
\Pi
as in the first paragraph of Section
5.2
. In this section, we will compute the quantization parameter of
(\mathcal{A}_{0}^{\widetilde{X}})^{\Pi}
.

Let
\mathfrak{L}_{k}\subset X
be a codimension 2 leaf and let
x\in\mathfrak{L}_{k}
. Fix the notation of Section
4.6
, i.e.
\Sigma_{k}=\mathbb{C}^{2}/\Gamma_{k}
,
\widetilde{x}_{j}
,
\Sigma_{k}^{\prime}=\mathbb{C}^{2}/\Gamma_{k}^{\prime}
,
\phi_{k}:\Gamma_{k}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Gamma
and so on. Let
\mathfrak{g}_{k}
be the complex simple Lie algebra corresponding to
\Gamma_{k}
, and let
\mathfrak{h}_{k}\subset\mathfrak{g}_{k}
be a Cartan subalgebra. Let
\{V_{1}(k),...,V_{n(k)}(k)\}
be the nontrivial irreducible representations of
\Gamma_{k}
and
\{\omega_{1}(k),...,\omega_{n(k)}(k)\}\subset\mathfrak{h}_{k}^{*}
the corresponding fundamental weights. Define the element

(5.3.1)

\epsilon_{k}:=\epsilon(\Gamma_{k}^{\prime})=\frac{|\Gamma_{k}^{\prime}|}{|\Gamma_{k}|}\sum_{i=1}^{n(k)}\dim(V_{i}(k)^{\Gamma_{k}^{\prime}})\omega_{i}(k)\in\mathfrak{h}_{k}^{*}.

Proposition 5.3.1
.

The element
\epsilon_{k}\in\mathfrak{h}_{k}^{*}
is a fixed point for the monodromy action of
\pi_{1}(\mathfrak{L}_{k})
on
\mathfrak{h}_{k}^{*}
, and hence an element of
\mathfrak{P}_{k}^{X}:=(\mathfrak{h}_{k}^{*})^{\pi_{1}(\mathfrak{L}_{k})}
. Define the quantization parameter

\epsilon:=(0,\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},...,\epsilon_{t})\in\bigoplus_{k=0}^{t}\mathfrak{P}_{k}^{X}\simeq\mathfrak{P}^{X}.

There is an isomorphism of filtered quantizations

(\mathcal{A}_{0}^{\widetilde{X}})^{\Pi}\simeq\mathcal{A}_{\epsilon}^{X}.

Proof.

By Theorem
4.7.6
, there is a parameter
\lambda\in\mathfrak{P}^{X}
(well-defined modulo
W
) such that

(\mathcal{A}_{0}^{\widetilde{X}})^{\Pi}\simeq\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}^{X}.

We will show that
\lambda_{0}=0
and
W_{k}\lambda_{k}=W_{k}\epsilon_{k}
for
1\leq k\leq t
.

Step 1
.
First, we show that
\lambda_{0}=0
. Fix a
\mathbb{Q}
-terminalization
\rho:\widetilde{Y}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\widetilde{X}
. The identification
\mathrm{Quant}(\widetilde{Y})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak{P}^{\widetilde{X}}
is the composition

\mathrm{Quant}(\widetilde{Y})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathrm{Quant}(\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{reg}})\overset{\mathrm{Per}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}}\mathfrak{P}^{\widetilde{X}}

where the first map is restriction, see
4.7.3
. Let
\mathcal{D}^{\widetilde{Y}}
denote the quantization of
\widetilde{Y}

with parameter
0
so that
\mathcal{A}_{0}^{\widetilde{X}}=\Gamma(Y,\mathcal{D}^{\widetilde{Y}})
. Let
\mathcal{D}^{\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{reg}}}
,
\mathcal{D}^{\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{reg}}}
denote the restrictions of
\mathcal{D}^{\widetilde{Y}}
to
\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{reg}}
and
\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{reg}}
and let
\mathcal{D}^{X^{\mathrm{reg}}}=(\mathcal{D}^{\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{reg}}})^{\Pi}
. Note that
\mathcal{D}^{X^{\mathrm{reg}}}
is a quantization of
X^{\mathrm{reg}}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{reg}}}
is its (étale) lift to
\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{reg}}
. Since the period map is functorial, see
[
BK04
, Sec 4]
,
\mathrm{Per}(\mathcal{D}^{\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{reg}}})\in H^{2}(\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{reg}},\mathbb{C})
is the image of
\mathrm{Per}(\mathcal{D}^{\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{reg}}})=0\in H^{2}(\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{reg}},\mathbb{C})
under the restriction map
H^{2}(\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{reg}},\mathbb{C})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}H^{2}(\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{reg}},\mathbb{C})
and of
\mathrm{Per}(\mathcal{D}^{X^{\mathrm{reg}}})\in H^{2}(X^{\mathrm{reg}},\mathbb{C})
under the pullback map
H^{2}(X^{\mathrm{reg}},\mathbb{C})\hookrightarrow H^{2}(\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{reg}},\mathbb{C})
. Thus,
\mathrm{Per}(\mathcal{D}^{X^{\mathrm{reg}}})=0
.
Since
\mathcal{D}^{X^{\mathrm{reg}}}
is the restriction of
(\mathcal{A}_{0}^{\widetilde{X}})^{\Pi}
to
X^{\mathrm{reg}}
, this implies
\lambda_{0}=0
. In steps 2 and 3, we will treat the case of
k>0
.

Step 2
. Consider the codimension 2 leaf
\mathfrak{L}_{k}\subset X
and the fiber product (
4.6.2
). As noted there, the natural map
\bigsqcup_{j}\widetilde{X}^{\wedge\widetilde{x}_{j}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\widetilde{X}
is
\Pi
-equivariant. For each
\widetilde{x}_{j}
let
\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}_{j}
denote the symplectic leaf in
\widetilde{X}
containing
\widetilde{x}_{j}
. There is an isomorphism

\mathcal{A}_{0,\hbar}^{\widetilde{X},\wedge}\simeq\mathbb{A}_{\hbar}(V_{j})^{\wedge}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{C}[[\hbar]]}\mathcal{A}_{0,\hbar}^{{\Sigma}^{\prime},\wedge},

where
V_{j}=T_{\widetilde{x}_{j}}\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}_{j}
, see (
4.7.1
). Here
\mathcal{A}_{0,\hbar}^{\widetilde{X},\wedge}
(resp.
\mathcal{A}_{0,\hbar}^{{\Sigma}^{\prime},\wedge}
) denotes the completion of the Rees algebra of the canonical quantization
\mathcal{A}_{0}^{\widetilde{X}}
(resp.
\mathcal{A}_{0}^{{\Sigma}^{\prime}}
) at
\widetilde{x}_{j}\in\widetilde{X}
(resp.
0\in{\Sigma}^{\prime}
). Since taking invariants commutes with completions, there is a natural isomorphism

(5.3.2)

\mathcal{A}_{\lambda,\hbar}^{X,\wedge_{x}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\left(\bigoplus\mathbb{A}_{\hbar}(V_{j})^{\wedge}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{C}[[\hbar]]}\mathcal{A}_{0,\hbar}^{{\Sigma}^{\prime},\wedge}\right)^{\Pi}

Fix a preimage
\widetilde{x}
of
x
in
\widetilde{X}
and let
\Pi^{\prime}:=\Pi_{\widetilde{x}},V:=V_{j}
. Since
\Pi
acts transitively on
\{\widetilde{x}_{j}\}
, we have

\left(\bigoplus\mathbb{A}_{\hbar}(V_{j})^{\wedge}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{C}[[\hbar]]}\mathcal{A}_{0,\hbar}^{{\Sigma}^{\prime},\wedge}\right)^{\Pi}\xrightarrow{\sim}\left(\mathbb{A}_{\hbar}(V)^{\wedge}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{C}[[\hbar]]}\mathcal{A}_{0,\hbar}^{{\Sigma}^{\prime},\wedge}\right)^{\Pi^{\prime}}.

and therefore

(5.3.3)

\mathcal{A}_{\lambda,\hbar}^{X,\wedge_{x}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\left(\mathbb{A}_{\hbar}(V)^{\wedge}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{C}[[\hbar]]}\mathcal{A}_{0,\hbar}^{{\Sigma}^{\prime},\wedge}\right)^{\Pi^{\prime}}.

Step 3
. Since
\mathcal{L}_{k}
is a smooth and symplectic and
\mathcal{L}_{k}=p^{-1}(\mathcal{L}_{k})/\Pi
,
\Pi^{\prime}
acts trivially on
V
(otherwise,
V/\Pi^{\prime}
would be singular because the action of
\Pi^{\prime}
on
V
is symplectic, while
\mathcal{L}_{k}
would have singularity
V/\Pi^{\prime}
at
x
).
In particular, there is an identification
T_{x}\mathcal{L}\simeq V
and also

(5.3.4)

\mathbb{C}[X]^{\wedge_{x}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{C}[V]^{\wedge}\widehat{\otimes}\mathbb{C}[\Sigma^{\prime}_{k}]^{\wedge}.

Furthermore

(\mathbb{A}_{\hbar}(V)^{\wedge}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{C}[[\hbar]]}\mathcal{A}_{0,\hbar}^{{\Sigma}^{\prime},\wedge})^{\Pi^{\prime}}=\mathbb{A}_{\hbar}(V)^{\wedge}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{C}[[\hbar]]}(\mathcal{A}_{0,\hbar}^{{\Sigma}^{\prime},\wedge})^{\Pi^{\prime}}.

So (
5.3.3
) becomes

(5.3.5)

\mathcal{A}_{\lambda,\hbar}^{X,\wedge_{x}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{A}_{\hbar}(V)^{\wedge}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{C}[[\hbar]]}(\mathcal{A}_{0,\hbar}^{{\Sigma}^{\prime},\wedge})^{\Pi^{\prime}}.

This isomorphism lifts (
5.3.4
).
As noted at the end of Section
4.7
, the isomorphism (
5.3.5
) determines
W_{k}\lambda_{k}
uniquely. By Proposition

5.2.1
, we have
W_{k}\lambda_{k}=W_{k}\epsilon_{k}
.

∎

Example 5.3.2
.

Suppose
\widetilde{X}
has no codimension 2 leaves. Then for each
\Sigma_{k}
, the quantization parameter
\epsilon_{k}\in\mathfrak{h}_{k}^{*}
(cf. (
5.3.1
)) is the ‘weighted barycenter’ parameter considered in Example
5.2.2

\epsilon_{k}=\frac{1}{|\Gamma_{k}|}\sum_{i=1}^{n(k)}\dim V_{i}(k)\ \omega_{i}(k)\in\mathfrak{h}_{k}^{*}.

We call the parameter

\epsilon=(0,\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},...,\epsilon_{t})\in\bigoplus_{k=0}^{t}\mathfrak{P}_{k}^{X}\simeq\mathfrak{P}^{X}

the
weighted barycenter
of
\mathfrak{P}^{X}
. By Proposition
5.3.1
,
\epsilon
is the quantization parameter of the
\Pi
-invariants in the canonical quantization
\mathcal{A}_{0}^{\widetilde{X}}
. This parameter will play an important role in Section
8
in the computation of infinitesimal characters.

5.4.
Almost étale covers of symplectic singularities

In this section, we will define and study finite covers of a very special type. The ideas in this section will be used in Section
6
for the classification of unipotent ideals and bimodules. Let
p:\widetilde{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}X
be a finite cover and fix the notation of Section
4.6
(e.g.
\Gamma
,
\Gamma^{\prime}
,
\Gamma_{k}
,
\Gamma_{k}^{\prime}
and so on).

Proposition 5.4.1
.

The following conditions are equivalent:

(i)

p
is étale over the open subset

X^{\mathrm{reg}}\cup\bigcup_{k=1}^{t}\mathfrak{L}_{k}\subset X.

(ii)

For each codimension 2 leaf
\mathfrak{L}_{k}\subset X
, there is an equality
\Gamma_{k}^{\prime}=\Gamma_{k}
.

If either of these equivalent conditions is satisfied, we say that
p:\widetilde{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}X
is
almost étale
.

Proof.

First, we prove (i)
\Rightarrow
(ii). Choose
x\in\mathfrak{L}_{k}
and
\widetilde{x}\in p^{-1}(x)
. By assumption,
p
is étale at
\widetilde{x}
. Thus
p
induces an étale map (and hence an isomorphism)
\widetilde{X}^{\wedge\widetilde{x}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}X^{\wedge x}
. Consider the symplectic vector space
V:=\mathbb{C}^{2}\oplus T_{x}\mathcal{L}_{k}
. There are identifications
\widetilde{X}^{\wedge\widetilde{x}}\simeq V^{\wedge_{0}}/\Gamma_{k}^{\prime}
and

X^{\wedge x}\simeq V^{\wedge_{0}}/\Gamma_{k}
. In particular,
\Gamma_{k},\Gamma_{k}^{\prime}
are recovered as the algebraic fundamental groups of the regular loci of
X^{\wedge x}

and
\widetilde{X}^{\wedge\widetilde{x}}
, respectively. Since

\widetilde{X}^{\wedge\widetilde{x}}\xrightarrow{\sim}X^{\wedge x}
, we conclude that
\Gamma_{k}=\Gamma_{k}^{\prime}
.

Next, we prove (ii)
\Rightarrow
(i).
A neighborhood of
x\in X
in the analytic topology is identified with
D/\Gamma_{k}
for a disc
D\subset V

around
0
. Similarly, a neighborhood
of
\widetilde{x}
in
\widetilde{X}
is identified with
\widetilde{D}/\Gamma_{k}
. Shrinking
D,\widetilde{D}
if necessary we can assume that
p
restricts to
\widetilde{D}/\Gamma_{k}\rightarrow D/\Gamma_{k}
. This restriction is a quasi-finite morphism of complex analytic spaces. We claim it is étale. Let
D^{0}:=D-(\{0\}\times T_{x}\mathcal{L}_{k})
. Note that
D^{0}
is the locus in
D
where
\Gamma_{k}
acts freely. Define
\widetilde{D}_{0}
similarly. Let
\widetilde{D}^{1}\subset\widetilde{D}^{0}
be the preimage of
D^{0}/\Gamma_{k}
in
\widetilde{D}
.
The complement to
\widetilde{D}^{1}
in
\widetilde{D}
is of codimension
2
because
p
is quasi-finite. In particular,
\widetilde{D}^{1}
is simply connected. The map
\widetilde{D}^{1}/\Gamma_{k}\rightarrow D^{0}/\Gamma_{k}
is a Poisson morphism of symplectic complex analytic manifolds, and is therefore étale. Thus it lifts to
a Poisson morphism of universal covers, which coincide with
\widetilde{D}^{1}
and
D^{0}
, respectively. By Hartog’s theorem, the composition
\widetilde{D}^{1}\rightarrow D^{0}\hookrightarrow D
extends to
\widetilde{D}
. This extension is étale outside of codimension
2
, and hence étale. This implies that
\widetilde{D}/\Gamma_{k}\rightarrow D/\Gamma_{k}
is étale, as desired.
∎

If
p:\widetilde{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}X
is Galois, there is a third characterization of almost étale. Recall the group homomorphisms
\phi_{k}:\Gamma_{k}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Gamma
, see (
4.6.1
).

Proposition 5.4.2
.

Suppose
p:\widetilde{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}X
is Galois. Then
p
is almost étale if and only if
\Gamma^{\prime}\supset\phi_{k}(\Gamma_{k})
for all
k
.

Proof.

First, suppose
p
is almost étale. For each codimension 2 leaf
\mathfrak{L}_{k}\subset X
, there is an inclusion
\phi^{\prime}_{k}(\Gamma^{\prime}_{k})\subseteq\phi_{k}(\Gamma_{k})\cap\Gamma^{\prime}
, see (
4.6.3
). By Proposition
5.4.1
,
\Gamma_{k}^{\prime}=\Gamma_{k}
, and so
\phi_{k}(\Gamma_{k})\subset\Gamma^{\prime}
.

Conversely, suppose
\phi_{k}(\Gamma_{k})\subset\Gamma^{\prime}
. Let
q:\widecheck{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}X
be the finite Galois cover corresponding to the minimal normal subgroup
\underline{\Gamma}\subset\Gamma
containing all
\phi_{k}(\Gamma_{k})
. It suffices to show that that
q
is almost étale. Let
\mathfrak{L}_{k}\subset X
be a codimension 2 leaf and let
x\in\mathfrak{L}_{k}
. Choose a small contractible open neighborhood
D
of
x
in
X
. We wish to show that
q:q^{-1}(D)\rightarrow D
is unramified.
Let
\widecheck{D}
be a connected component of
q^{-1}(D)
, and choose a base point
y\in D-\mathfrak{L}
for
\pi_{1}(D-\mathfrak{L})
. Any loop in
D-\mathfrak{L}
is conjugate to an element in the preimage of
\phi_{k}(\Gamma_{k})\subset\Gamma=\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{alg}}(X^{\mathrm{reg}})
in
\pi_{1}(X^{\mathrm{reg}})
by the construction of
\phi_{k}
. Therefore it lies in the preimage of
\Gamma^{\prime}
in
\pi_{1}(X^{\mathrm{reg}})
. It follows that the cover
\widecheck{D}-q^{-1}(\mathfrak{L})\rightarrow D-\mathfrak{L}
is an isomorphism. Hence the restriction of
q
to
\widecheck{D}
is an isomorphism. This completes the proof.
∎

If
\widetilde{X},\widetilde{X}^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}X
are finite covers of
X
, a
morphism of covers
is a map
f:\widetilde{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\widetilde{X}^{\prime}
such that the following diagram commutes
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{X}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

This defines a partial order on the set of isomorphism classes of finite covers of
X
, analogously to (
2.2.2
).

Proposition 5.4.3
.

There is a unique
maximal
almost étale cover
p:\breve{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}X
. This cover is Galois. The corresponding Galois group is
\Gamma/\underline{\Gamma}
, where
\underline{\Gamma}
is the minimal normal subgroup of
\Gamma
containing
\phi_{k}(\Gamma_{k})
for all
k
.

Proof.

First, we will show that
X
admits a maximal almost étale cover. Since
\Gamma
is finite (and the identity map
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}X
is almost étale), it suffices to show that for any pair of almost étale covers
\widetilde{X},\widetilde{X}^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}X
, there is an almost étale cover
\breve{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}X
which covers both
\widetilde{X}
and
\widetilde{X}^{\prime}
. First, form the fiber product
\widetilde{X}\times_{X}\widetilde{X}^{\prime}
. The natural morphisms
\widetilde{X}\times_{X}\widetilde{X}^{\prime}\rightarrow X,\widetilde{X},\widetilde{X}^{\prime}
are finite and étale outside of codimension 4. Note that
\widetilde{X}\times_{X}\widetilde{X}^{\prime}

may fail to be irreducible. Let
\breve{X}

denote the normalization of an irreducible component. Then
\breve{X}
is an almost étale cover of
X
,
\widetilde{X}
, and
\widetilde{X}^{\prime}
. This proves both the existence and uniqueness of the maximal almost étale cover.

It remains show that the unique maximal almost étale cover
\breve{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}X
is Galois. The claim regarding the Galois group then follows from Proposition

5.4.2
.

First, note that there is a unique minimal Galois cover
\widecheck{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}X
dominating
\breve{X}
. Indeed, if
\breve{X}
corresponds to a subgroup
H\subset\Gamma
, then
\widecheck{X}
corresponds to
\bigcap_{\gamma\in\Gamma}\gamma H\gamma^{-1}\subset\Gamma
. Let
\widecheck{\Gamma},\overline{\Gamma}
denote the Galois groups of
\widecheck{X}\rightarrow X
and

\widecheck{X}\rightarrow\breve{X}
, respectively. So
\overline{\Gamma}\subset\widecheck{\Gamma}
, and we have
\breve{X}=\widecheck{X}/\overline{\Gamma}

and
X=\widecheck{X}/\widecheck{\Gamma}
. We claim that

(*)

for any subgroup
\overline{\Gamma}^{\prime}\subset\widecheck{\Gamma}
conjugate to
\overline{\Gamma}
the cover
\widecheck{X}/\overline{\Gamma}^{\prime}\rightarrow X
is almost étale.

Since

\breve{X}
is maximal, this will imply that
\overline{\Gamma}=1
, completing the proof. Let
\widecheck{X}^{2}
denote the preimage in
\widecheck{X}
of the open subset

X^{\mathrm{reg}}\cup\bigcup_{k}\mathfrak{L}_{k}\subset X.

We claim that the following conditions are equivalent:

(i)

\widecheck{X}^{2}/\overline{\Gamma}^{\prime}\rightarrow\widecheck{X}^{2}/\widecheck{\Gamma}

is étale.

(ii)

For any
x\in\widecheck{X}^{2}
, we have
\widecheck{\Gamma}_{x}=\overline{\Gamma}^{\prime}_{x}
.

Note that (i) is equivalent to the assertion that
\widecheck{X}/\overline{\Gamma}^{\prime}\rightarrow\widecheck{X}/\widecheck{\Gamma}
is almost étale. The equivalence of (i) and (ii) implies (*): in (ii) we can replace
\overline{\Gamma}^{\prime}
with
g\overline{\Gamma}^{\prime}g^{-1}
for any
g\in\widecheck{\Gamma}
(by replacing
x
with
gx
).

To show (i)
\Rightarrow
(ii) we observe that, assuming (i), each fiber of
\widecheck{X}^{2}/\overline{\Gamma}^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\widecheck{X}^{2}/\widecheck{\Gamma}
has the same number of elements. Generically, this number is the index
|\widecheck{\Gamma}/\overline{\Gamma}^{\prime}|
and for
a given point
\overline{\Gamma}^{\prime}x\in\widecheck{X}/\overline{\Gamma}^{\prime}
it is the quotient

|\Gamma x|/|\overline{\Gamma}^{\prime}x|
. The equality

|\widecheck{\Gamma}/\overline{\Gamma}^{\prime}|=|\Gamma x|/|\overline{\Gamma}^{\prime}x|
is equivalent to (ii).

To show (ii)
\Rightarrow
(i) we use a special case of the Luna étale slice theorem, see e.g.
[
PV94
, Thm 6.1]
. Namely, for any
x\in\widecheck{X}
, there is an étale morphism
U\rightarrow X
from an irreducible affine variety
U
with image containing
\widecheck{\Gamma}x
and such that
U\times_{X}\widecheck{X}
is a homogeneous fiber bundle over
\widecheck{\Gamma}/\widecheck{\Gamma}_{x}
. It is easy to see that for a homogeneous fiber bundle
\widecheck{\Gamma}\times^{\widecheck{\Gamma}_{x}}U^{\prime}
, where
U^{\prime}
is an affine variety with
\widecheck{\Gamma}_{x}
-action, the morphism
(\widecheck{\Gamma}\times^{\widecheck{\Gamma}_{x}}U^{\prime})/\overline{\Gamma}^{\prime}\rightarrow(\widecheck{\Gamma}\times^{\widecheck{\Gamma}_{x}}U^{\prime})/\widecheck{\Gamma}
is etale at the preimages of
x

provided
\overline{\Gamma}^{\prime}_{x}=\widecheck{\Gamma}_{x}
.
This implies (i).

∎

Remark 5.4.4
.

Note that if
X
satisfies (
5.0.1
), then
\Gamma(0)=\underline{\Gamma}
by
Proposition
5.1.2
. Thus, the Galois group of the cover
\breve{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}X
is
\Gamma/\Gamma(0)
.

The next proposition relates almost étale covers to canonical quantizations.

Proposition 5.4.5
.

Let
p:\widetilde{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}X
be a finite Galois almost étale cover, and let
\Pi=\Gamma/\Gamma^{\prime}
be its Galois group. There is an isomorphism of filtered quantizations

(\mathcal{A}_{0}^{\widetilde{X}})^{\Pi}\simeq\mathcal{A}_{0}^{X}.

Proof.

By Proposition
5.3.1
, there is an isomorphism of filtered quantizations

(\mathcal{A}_{0}^{\widetilde{X}})^{\Pi}\simeq\mathcal{A}_{\epsilon}^{X}.

where
\epsilon\in\mathfrak{P}^{X}
is the quantization parameter defined in the statement of that proposition. By Proposition
5.4.1
, we have
\Gamma_{k}^{\prime}=\Gamma_{k}
for
1\leq k\leq t
. Hence

\epsilon_{k}=\epsilon(\Gamma_{k}^{\prime})=\frac{|\Gamma_{k}^{\prime}|}{|\Gamma_{k}|}\sum_{i=1}^{n(k)}\dim(V_{i}(k)^{\Gamma_{k}})\ \omega_{i}(k)=0.

∎

5.5.
Simplicity conjecture

In this section, we gather evidence for the following general conjecture:

Conjecture 5.5.1
.

Suppose
X
is a conical symplectic singularity. Then the canonical quantization
\mathcal{A}_{0}
of
\mathbb{C}[X]
is a simple algebra.

Note that if
X
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial and terminal, this conjecture is a special case of
[
Los19
, Conj 3.1]
.

Example 5.5.2
.

Let
G
be a complex connected reductive algebraic group, let
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
be a
G
-equivariant nilpotent cover, and let
X=\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}])
. In Section
6
, we consider the primitive ideal
I_{0}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})=\ker{\Phi}\subset U(\mathfrak{g})
(where
\Phi:U(\mathfrak{g})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathcal{A}_{0}
is the quantum co-moment map for
\mathcal{A}_{0}
). Such ideals are called ‘unipotent’ and play a central role in this paper. By Proposition
6.3.3
below, the simplicity of
\mathcal{A}_{0}
is equivalent to the maximality of
I_{0}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})
. In Appendix
B
, we show that
I_{0}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})
is always maximal if
G
is linear classical. This argument is generalized to arbitrary groups in the paper
[
MM23
]
.

The other cases where we know Conjecture
5.5.1
to
be true are not otherwise relevant to this paper, so we will be brief.

Example 5.5.3
.

Fix the notation of Example
5.5.2
. Let
\mathbb{O}^{\prime}\subset\partial\mathbb{O}

be an orbit, and
\bullet_{\dagger^{\prime}}
be the restriction functor, see Section
3.5
. Let
S^{\prime}
be the Slodowy slice to
\mathbb{O}^{\prime}
and
X^{\prime}
be the preimage of
S^{\prime}

in
X
. If
\mathcal{A}_{0}
is the canonical quantization of
X
, one can show that
(\mathcal{A}_{0})_{\dagger^{\prime}}
is
the canonical quantization of
X^{\prime}
. The simplicity of
(\mathcal{A}_{0})_{\dagger^{\prime}}
should
follow from the simplicity of
\mathcal{A}_{0}
(together with a transitivity property
for restriction functors).

Example 5.5.4
.

Let
X
be an affine Nakajima quiver variety of the form
\mathcal{M}(v,w)

in the notation of
[
BL21
, Sec 2.1]
(slughtly modified: to each loop we assign the space of traceless matrices). The formal slices to symplectic leaves in

X
are of the same form for “smaller” quivers. The quantization
\mathcal{A}_{0}

is a simple algebra if and only if none of the quantum slice algebras (which are also
canonical qunatizations) have finite dimensional representations. So
Conjecture
5.5.1
in this case is equivalent to the assertion that
the canonical quantization of a Nakajima quiver variety has no
finite dimensional representations. If the underlying quiver
Q
is
of finite or affine type, this follows from the results of
[
BL21
]
,
[
Los14
]
,
[
Los18a
]
, and
[
Los17b
]
.
First, assume that
Q
has no loops.
The argument of
[
Los17b
, Section 3]
can be used to prove
that if
[
BL21
, Conjecture 1.1]
holds (in the stronger form which also
includes the claim that the map
\mathsf{CC}
from there is injective),
then
\mathcal{A}_{0}
has no finite dimensional representations. If

Q
is of finite or affine type, the stronger form of
[
BL21
, Conjecture 1.1]

was proved in
[
BL21
]
,
[
Los17b
]
. And in the case when
Q
is a Jordan
quiver,
\mathcal{A}_{0}
has no finite dimensional representations by

[
Los18a
]
. Thus Conjecture
5.5.1

holds when
Q
is of finite or affine type.

Example 5.5.5
.

Let
X=V/\Gamma
, where
V
is a symplectic vector space and
\Gamma
is a finite
group of linear symplectomorphisms of
V
. Consider the normal subgroup
\Gamma^{\prime}\subset\Gamma
generated by all symplectic reflections. Let
X_{0}=V/\Gamma^{\prime}
and let
\mathcal{A}_{0}^{X_{0}}
be the canonical
quantization of
V/\Gamma^{\prime}
. Then
\mathcal{A}_{0}=(\mathcal{A}_{0}^{X_{0}})^{\Gamma/\Gamma^{\prime}}
by Proposition
5.4.5
. It is easy to see that the simplicity of
\mathcal{A}_{0}^{X_{0}}
implies that of

\mathcal{A}_{0}
. So for Conjecture
5.5.1
we can assume
that
\Gamma
is generated by symplectic reflections. An important case
is when
V=(\mathbb{C}^{2})^{\oplus n},\Gamma=S_{n}\ltimes\Gamma_{1}^{n}
, where
\Gamma_{1}\subset\mathrm{SL}(2)
. The variety
V/\Gamma
in this case is a Nakajima
quiver variety as in Example
5.5.4
. So in this case Conjecture

5.5.1
should follow. Another infinite family of examples are
complex reflection groups
G(\ell,r,n)
(for
r=1
we recover
S_{n}\ltimes\Gamma_{1}^{n}
,
where
\Gamma_{1}\simeq\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}
). In this case, the conjecture should also
follow from the approach of
[
Los14
, Section 3]
. This leaves only exceptional groups generated by symplectic reflections.

6.
Unipotent ideals and bimodules

Let
G
be a connected reductive algebraic group and let
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
be a
G
-equivariant nilpotent cover. Recall that the affine variety
\widetilde{X}:=\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}])
is a conical symplectic singularity, see Example
4.3.4
. There is a
G
-action on
\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}]
by graded Poisson automorphisms and a classical co-moment map

\varphi:\mathfrak{g}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}].

induced from the natural map of varieties
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathbb{O}\subset\mathfrak{g}^{*}
.

Consider the extended Namikawa space
\overline{\mathfrak{P}}^{\widetilde{X}}=\mathfrak{P}^{\widetilde{X}}\oplus\mathfrak{z}(\mathfrak{g})^{*}
, see Section
4.11
. By Proposition
4.11.3
, there is a bijection

\overline{\mathfrak{P}}^{\widetilde{X}}/W^{\widetilde{X}}\simeq\mathrm{Quant}^{G}(\widetilde{X}),\qquad W\cdot\lambda\mapsto(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}^{\widetilde{X}},\Phi_{\lambda}^{\widetilde{X}})

where
\Phi_{\lambda}^{\widetilde{X}}:U(\mathfrak{g})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}^{\widetilde{X}}
is the unique quantum co-moment map which restricts to the character of
\mathfrak{z}(\mathfrak{g})
determined by
\lambda
.

For each
\lambda\in\overline{\mathfrak{P}}^{\widetilde{X}}
, consider the two-sided ideal

I_{\lambda}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}):=\ker{(\Phi_{\lambda}^{\widetilde{X}}:U(\mathfrak{g})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathcal{A}^{\widetilde{X}}_{\lambda})}\subset U(\mathfrak{g}).

Ideals of this form enjoy a number of favorable properties, see Proposition
6.1.2
below. In particular,
I_{\lambda}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})
is primitive, completely prime, and has associated variety
\overline{\mathbb{O}}
.

Definition 6.0.1
.

The
unipotent ideal
attached to
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
is the primitive ideal
I_{0}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})\subset U(\mathfrak{g})
.

The set of unipotent ideals includes, as a proper subset, all
special
unipotent ideals, cf. Definition
1.2.1
. We will give a proof of this containment in Section
9.2
. More examples (and non-examples) are given in Section
6.2
. In Section
6.3
, we will provide further motivation for Definition
6.0.1
. In particular, we will demonstrate that our definition follows naturally from Vogan’s desiderata, cf. Section
1.3
, and the orbit method philosophy.

In many cases, non-isomorphic covers give rise to the same unipotent ideal. In Section
6.5
, we will introduce an equivalence relation on nilpotent covers. Two covers are equivalent if, roughly speaking, they have the same dimension 2 singularities (see Definition
6.5.1
for a more precise condition). We will show that the map
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}\mapsto I_{0}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})
induces a bijection

\{\text{equivalence classes of }G\text{-eqvt nilpotent covers}\}\xrightarrow{\sim}\{\text{unipotent ideals}\},

proving Theorem
1.5.3
from the introduction. In Section
6.6
, we will turn our attention to bimodules. In view of Definition
6.0.1
, it is natural to define:

Definition 6.0.2
.

A
unipotent bimodule
attached to
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
is an irreducible Harish-Chandra bimodule
\mathcal{B}\in\HC^{G}(U(\mathfrak{g}))
such that

\mathrm{LAnn}(\mathcal{B})=\mathrm{RAnn}(\mathcal{B})=I_{0}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}).

Denote the set of (isomorphism classes of) unipotent bimodules by
\mathrm{Unip}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}}(G)
.

In Section
6.6
, we will give a geometric classification of unipotent bimodules. The main result is as follows. The equivalence class of
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
contains a unique maximal element, see Lemma
6.5.3
. Since Definition
6.0.2
depends only on the equivalence class, we can assume that
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
is maximal. Let
\Pi=\Aut_{\mathbb{O}}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})
. For each irreducible
\Pi
-representation
V
, there is a Harish-Chandra bimodule
(\mathcal{A}_{0}^{\widetilde{X}}\otimes V)^{\Pi}
. This bimodule has left and right infinitesimal characters since
I_{0}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})
is primitive. So, by Lemma
3.4.2
, it has finite length. Below we will show that
(\mathcal{A}_{0}^{\widetilde{X}}\otimes V)^{\Pi}
has a unique composition factor with associated variety
\overline{\mathbb{O}}
. Denote it by
\mathcal{B}_{V}
. We will show that the map
V\mapsto\mathcal{B}_{V}
defines a bijection

\{\text{irreducible representations of }\Pi\}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Unip}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}}(G),

proving Theorem
1.5.4
from the introduction. In fact, assuming that
I_{0}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})
is maximal (a condition which we check in many cases; the remaining cases are checked in
[
MM23
]
), the bimodule
(\mathcal{A}_{0}^{\widetilde{X}}\otimes V)^{\Pi}
is irreducible.

Every Harish-Chandra bimodule carries the structure of a
G
-representation (via the adjoint action of
\mathfrak{g}
). In Section
6.7
, we will show that if
\mathcal{B}\in\mathrm{Unip}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}}(G)
and
I_{0}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})
is maximal, then there is a subgroup
H\subset G
and a finite-dimensional
H
-representation
\chi
such that

\mathcal{B}\simeq_{G}\mathrm{AlgInd}^{G}_{H}\chi.

This result, which follows easily from the classification above, provides an affirmative answer, in the complex case, to an old conjecture of Vogan (Desideratum (5) from Section
1.3
).

6.1.
Hamiltonian quantizations of nilpotent covers

Let
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
be a
G
-equivariant nilpotent cover. Choose
\lambda\in\overline{\mathfrak{P}}^{\widetilde{X}}
, and let
(\mathcal{A},\Phi):=(\mathcal{A}^{\widetilde{X}}_{\lambda},\Phi^{\widetilde{X}}_{\lambda})
be the associated Hamiltonian quantization of
\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}]
. As above, let
I_{\lambda}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})=\ker{\Phi}\subset U(\mathfrak{g})
. Below, we will collect some basic facts about
\mathcal{A}
,
\Phi
, and
I_{\lambda}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})
.

Choose
e\in\mathbb{O}
and
x\in\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
over
e
. Write
R
(resp.
R_{x}
) for the reductive part of the stabilizer
G_{e}
(resp.
G_{x}
). Note that
R_{x}\subset R
is a finite-index subgroup. Let
\mathcal{W}
denote the
W
-algebra associated to
\mathbb{O}
. Recall the functors defined in Section
3.5

\bullet_{\dagger}:\HC^{G}(U(\mathfrak{g}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\HC^{R}(\mathcal{W}),\qquad\bullet^{\dagger}:\HC^{R}_{\mathrm{fin}}(\mathcal{W})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\HC^{G}_{\overline{\mathbb{O}}}(U(\mathfrak{g})).

as well as the maps

\bullet_{\dagger}:\mathrm{Prim}_{\overline{\mathbb{O}}}(U(\mathfrak{g}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathrm{Id}_{\mathrm{fin}}(\mathcal{W}),\qquad\bullet^{{\ddagger}}:\mathrm{Prim}_{\mathrm{fin}}(\mathcal{W})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathrm{Prim}_{\overline{\mathbb{O}}}(U(\mathfrak{g}))

We will need the following result.

Lemma 6.1.1
.

The following are true:

(i)

\mathcal{A}_{\dagger}
is a filtered algebra,
R
-equivariantly isomorphic to the
\mathbb{C}[R/R_{x}]
(with the trivial filtration).

(ii)

There is an
R_{x}
-stable ideal
J\subset\mathcal{W}
of codimension 1 such that

\mathcal{A}_{\dagger}\simeq\bigoplus_{r\in R/R_{x}}\mathcal{W}/rJ.

(iii)

The adjunction map
\mathcal{A}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}(\mathcal{A}_{\dagger})^{\dagger}
is an isomorphism of filtered algebras.

(iv)

The ideal
I_{\lambda}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})
is primitive. Moreover,
I_{\lambda}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})=J^{{\ddagger}}
, where
J\subset\mathcal{W}
is the ideal in (ii).

Proof.

(i) is
[
Los16
, Lem 5.2(1)]
. (ii) is an immediate consequence of (i). (iii) is
[
Los16
, Lem 5.2(3)]
. We proceed to proving (iv). The proof that
I_{\lambda}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})
is primitive repeats the beginning of
[
Los16
, Section 5.3]
. By Theorem
3.5.3
,
J^{\ddagger}
is the unique primitive ideal
I\subset U(\mathfrak{g})
such that
I_{\dagger}
is the intersection of the
R
-conjugates of
J
. The ideal
I_{\lambda}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})
satisfies this property by the definition of
J
, so
I_{\lambda}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})=J^{\ddagger}
, as desired.
∎

Proposition 6.1.2
.

The ideal
I_{\lambda}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})\subset U(\mathfrak{g})
enjoys the following properties:

(i)

V(I_{\lambda}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}))=\overline{\mathbb{O}}
.

(ii)

\operatorname{\operatorname{\mathcal{W}-\dim}}(I_{\lambda}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}))=1
(cf. Definition
3.5.4
).

(iii)

Suppose
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathbb{O}
is Galois and the
\Aut_{\mathbb{O}}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})
-action on
\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}]
lifts to
\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}^{\widetilde{X}}
. Then
m_{\overline{\mathbb{O}}}(U(\mathfrak{g})/I_{\lambda}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}))=1
.

(iv)

I_{\lambda}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})
is completely prime.

Proof.

Properties (i) and (ii) are immediate from Lemma
6.1.1
. For (iii), let
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
denote the algebra of
\Aut_{\mathbb{O}}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})
-invariants in
\mathcal{A}
. Note that
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
is a Hamiltonian quantization of
\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{O}]
. By Lemma
4.11.2
its co-moment map coincides with the restriction of
\Phi
to
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{A}
. Thus, we get an embedding
U(\mathfrak{g})/I_{\lambda}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
. Taking multiplicities, we get an inequality
m_{\overline{\mathbb{O}}}(U(\mathfrak{g})/I_{\lambda}({\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}}))\leq m_{\overline{\mathbb{O}}}(\mathcal{A}^{\prime})
. Since
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
is a quantization of
\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{O}]
, we have
m_{\overline{\mathbb{O}}}(\mathcal{A}^{\prime})=1
. Property (iii) follows. (iv) follows from Corollary
3.5.6
combined with (iv) of Lemma
6.1.1
.
∎

6.2.
Examples of unipotent ideals and bimodules

In this section, we will collect some examples (and non-examples) of unipotent ideals and bimodules.

Example 6.2.1
.

(i)

Let
\mathbb{O}=\{0\}
. Then
I_{0}(\mathbb{O})
is the
augmentation ideal
(i.e. the annihilator of the trivial
U(\mathfrak{g})
-module).

(ii)

More generally, let
\mathbb{O}^{\vee}
be a nilpotent orbit for the Langlands dual group
G^{\vee}
and consider the
special unipotent ideal

I_{\mathrm{max}}(\frac{1}{2}h^{\vee})\subset U(\mathfrak{g})
(cf. Definition
1.2.1
). In Section
9.2
, we will show that
I_{\mathrm{max}}(\frac{1}{2}h^{\vee})
is unipotent (and describe the cover it is attached to).

(iii)

Let
\mathfrak{g}
be a simple Lie algebra and let
\mathbb{O}_{\mathrm{min}}
be the minimal nilpotent orbit. If
\mathfrak{g}
is not of type
A
, there is a unique (maximal) completely prime ideal
I_{0}\subset U(\mathfrak{g})
such that
V(I_{0})=\overline{\mathbb{O}}_{\mathrm{min}}
called the
Joseph ideal
(see
[
Jos76
]
). This ideal can be constructed in a number of equivalent ways, see e.g.
[
Gar82
]
and
[
LSS88
]
. It follows from (iv) of Proposition
6.1.2
that
I_{0}(\mathbb{O}_{\mathrm{min}})=I_{0}
.

(iv)

As a special case of (iii), consider the Joseph ideal
I_{0}
for
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{sp}(2n)
