\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}\cap[\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}])
.

(iii)

The universal graded deformation
\widetilde{Y}_{\mathrm{univ}}
(see Proposition
4.7.1
) is identified with the Poisson scheme
G\times^{P}(\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})\times\widetilde{X}_{L}\times\mathfrak{p}^{\perp})
over
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})
for a uniquely determined isomorphism
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{2}(\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{reg}},\mathbb{C})
. This isomorphism coincides with
\eta
.

Proof.

(i) is proved for nilpotent orbits in
[
Los16
, Prop 4.7]
. The proof there can be easily generalized to arbitrary nilpotent covers. (ii) is
[
Nam20a
, Proposition 10]
.

The isomorphism
G\times^{P}(\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})\times\widetilde{X}_{L}\times\mathfrak{p}^{\perp})\xrightarrow{\sim}\widetilde{Y}_{\mathrm{univ}}
is proved in the same way as
[
Los16
, Prop 4.7(2)]
, see also
[
Nam20a
, Thm 9]
. It remains to check that the isomorphism
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak{P}^{\widetilde{X}}
in Proposition
4.7.1
coincides with the map
\eta
defined above. The variety
\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{reg}}=G\times^{P}(\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{reg}}\times\mathfrak{p}^{\perp})
is obtained as the Hamiltonian reduction of the product variety
T^{*}G\times\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{reg}}
under the action of
P
and the deformation
G\times^{P}(\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})\times\widetilde{X}_{L}\times\mathfrak{p}^{\perp})
is realized as the universal Hamiltonian reduction for this action, i.e. as
\mu_{P}^{-1}(\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{p}))/P
(where
\mu_{P}
is the moment map). It remains to show that
\eta
coincides with the period map from Remark
4.7.2
. This is a special case of
[
Los12
, Proposition 3.2.1]
.
∎

Next, we explain the classification of
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial terminalizations of
\widetilde{X}
. Recall the subset
\mathfrak{P}^{\mathrm{sing}}\subset\mathfrak{P}
defined in Section
4.9
. Let
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}\cap[\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}])_{\mathbb{R}}^{\geq 0}\subset\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}\cap[\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}])_{\mathbb{R}}
denote the cone of
\mathfrak{p}
-dominant weights.

Lemma 7.2.3
.

The following are true:

(i)

Under the identification
\eta:\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}\cap[\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}])\simeq\mathfrak{P}
, the subset
\mathfrak{P}^{\mathrm{sing}}
corresponds to the subset

\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}\cap[\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}])^{\mathrm{sing}}:=\{\lambda\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}\cap[\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}])\mid L\subsetneq G_{\lambda}\}.

(ii)

Under the identification
\eta:\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}\cap[\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}])_{\mathbb{R}}\simeq\mathfrak{P}_{\mathbb{R}}
, the ample cone
\mathrm{Amp}(G\times^{P}(\widetilde{X}_{L}\times\mathfrak{p}^{\perp}))
(defined by (
4.9.2
)) corresponds to the subset
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}\cap[\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}])_{\mathbb{R}}^{\geq 0}
.

(iii)

Every
\mathbb{Q}
-terminalization of
\widetilde{X}
is of the form
G\times^{P_{1}}(\widetilde{X}_{L}\times\mathfrak{p}_{1}^{\perp})
, where
P_{1}
is a parabolic subgroup of
G
with Levi subgroup
L
. Two parabolic subgroups give rise to the same
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial terminalization if and only if their dominant cones are
W^{\widetilde{X}}
-conjugate.

Proof.

By definition
\mathfrak{P}^{\mathrm{sing}}
consists of all parameters
\lambda\in\mathfrak{P}
for which
\widetilde{Y}_{\lambda}
is not affine. Viewing
\lambda
as an element of
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})
, we have
\widetilde{Y}_{\lambda}=G\times^{P}(\{\lambda\}\times\mathfrak{p}^{\perp}\times\widetilde{X}_{L})
. If
G_{\lambda}=L
, this variety is affine. If
G_{\lambda}\neq L
, then
\widetilde{Y}_{\lambda}
contains the projective variety
G_{\lambda}/(G_{\lambda}\cap P)
and is therefore not affine. This completes the proof of (i). (ii) follows immediately from Proposition
7.1.3
. Cones of the form
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}\cap[\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}])^{\geq 0}_{\mathbb{R}}
partition
\mathfrak{P}_{\mathbb{R}}
. Now (iii) follows from Theorem
4.9.1
.
∎

Remark 7.2.4
.

In
[
Nam20a
, Thm 7]
, Namikawa provides an alternative proof of Lemma
7.2.3
(iii). His proof does not use Theorem
4.9.1
; he deduces the first part of (iii) from the fact that ample cones of the form
\mathrm{Amp}(G\times^{P}(\widetilde{X}_{L}\times\mathfrak{p}^{\perp}))
cover the fundamental chamber
\mathfrak{P}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\geq 0}
. The second part of (iii) is standard.

Our definition of
\eta
depends on the choice of
P
. The next result is essential for our computation of
\eta
.

Proposition 7.2.5
.

For two choices of
P
, the corresponding maps
\eta:\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}\cap[\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}])\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak{P}^{\widetilde{X}}
coincide up to the action of
W^{\widetilde{X}}
on the target.

Proof.

Replacing
G
with its commutator subgroup, we can assume that
G
is semisimple. The proof has several steps.

Step 1
.
Let
P_{1},P_{2}
be parabolics with Levi factor
L
and write
\eta_{1},\eta_{2}
for the corresponding isomorphisms. We identify
\mathbb{C}[G\times^{P}(\widetilde{X}_{L}\times\mathfrak{p}^{\perp})]
with
\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{X}]
via the pullback map under the terminalization morphism

G\times^{P}(\widetilde{X}_{L}\times\mathfrak{p}^{\perp})\twoheadrightarrow\widetilde{X}
.

Step 2
.
Choose a Zariski-generic element
\lambda\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})
. Thanks to (iii) of Theorem
4.7.5
, it suffices to show that
\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{X}_{\eta_{1}(\lambda)}]

and
\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{X}_{\eta_{2}(\lambda)}]
are isomorphic as filtered Poisson deformations of
\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{X}]
.

Step 3
.
Let
\ell=\mathbb{C}\lambda
. Consider the variety
\widetilde{Y}_{\ell,i}:=G\times^{P_{i}}(\ell\times\widetilde{X}_{L}\times\mathfrak{p}_{i}^{\perp})
and the natural map
\rho_{i}:\widetilde{Y}_{\ell,i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathfrak{g}^{*}
. Let
\ell^{\times}:=\ell-\{0\}
and
\widetilde{Y}^{\times}_{\ell,i}:=\ell^{\times}\times_{\ell}\widetilde{Y}_{\ell,i}
. There is a
\mathbb{C}^{\times}\times G
-equivariant identification
\widetilde{Y}^{\times}_{\ell,i}\simeq G\times^{L}(\ell^{\times}\times\widetilde{X}_{L})
intertwining the maps to
\mathfrak{g}^{*}
.

Step 4
.
The pullback
\widetilde{X}_{\eta_{i}(\ell)}
of the universal deformation
\widetilde{X}_{\mathrm{univ}}
(see Proposition

4.7.7
) to
\eta_{i}(\ell)
coincides with
\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\eta_{i}(\ell)\times_{\mathfrak{P}}\widetilde{Y}_{\mathrm{univ}}])
as a Poisson scheme over
\ell
with
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
-action. So there is the Stein factorization
Y^{\times}_{\ell,i}\rightarrow\widetilde{X}_{\eta_{i}(\ell)}\rightarrow\mathfrak{g}^{*}
. This gives rise to a
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
-equivariant isomorphism of Poisson
\ell
-schemes

\widetilde{X}_{\eta_{1}(\ell)}\xrightarrow{\sim}\widetilde{X}_{\eta_{2}(\ell)}
. By the construction in Step 1, this isomorphism gives the identity on
\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{X}]
, i.e. is an isomorphism of graded Poisson deformations. It follows that

\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{X}_{\eta_{1}(\lambda)}]

and
\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{X}_{\eta_{2}(\lambda)}]

are isomorphic as filtered Poisson deformations, completing the proof.
∎

By Proposition
4.5.1
,
\mathfrak{P}^{\widetilde{X}}
decomposes into partial Namikawa spaces

(7.2.2)

\mathfrak{P}^{\widetilde{X}}\simeq\mathfrak{P}^{\widetilde{X}}_{0}\oplus\bigoplus_{k=1}^{t}\mathfrak{P}^{\widetilde{X}}_{k}.

Here,
\mathfrak{P}_{0}^{\widetilde{X}}=H^{2}(\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{reg}},\mathbb{C})
and
\mathfrak{P}_{k}^{\widetilde{X}}
is the space of
\pi_{1}(\mathfrak{L}_{k})
-invariants in the dual Cartan subalgebra
\mathfrak{h}_{k}^{*}
associated to the Kleinian singularity
\Sigma_{k}
.

Remark 7.2.6
.

We note that Proposition
7.2.2
together with (
7.2.2
) provides a more conceptual proof of Lemma
6.5.2
, at least in exceptional types. Let
\mathbb{O}
be a nilpotent orbit in a simple exceptional Lie algebra and suppose
X=\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{O}])
contains a codimension 2 leaf
\mathfrak{L}_{k}\subset X
such that the corresponding singularity is of type
E_{8}
. Note that
E_{8}
has no nontrivial diagram automorphisms. Hence,
\dim\mathfrak{P}^{X}_{k}=\dim\mathfrak{h}_{k}^{*}=8
. Choose a Levi subgroup
L\subset G
and a birationally rigid
L
-orbit
\mathbb{O}_{L}
such that
\mathbb{O}=\mathrm{Bind}^{G}_{L}\mathbb{O}_{L}
. Then by Proposition
7.2.2
and the decomposition (
7.2.2
), we have
\dim(\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}))=\dim\mathfrak{P}^{X}\geq\dim\mathfrak{P}^{X}_{k}=8
. Thus,
G=E_{8}
and
(L,\mathbb{O}_{L})=(T,\{0\})
. That is,
\mathbb{O}
is the principal nilpotent orbit in
E_{8}
.

For our computation of
\eta
, we will need Lie-theoretic descriptions of the spaces
\mathfrak{P}_{k}^{\widetilde{X}}
analogous to Proposition
7.2.2
. First, we will describe the space
\mathfrak{P}_{0}^{\widetilde{X}}
. We will describe the spaces
\mathfrak{P}_{k}^{\widetilde{X}}
,
k\geq 1
, in Section
7.5
, under some additional restrictions.
For the following lemma, suppose
G
is simply connected. Let
R_{x}
denote the reductive part of the stabilizer of
x\in\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
and let
\mathfrak{r}
be its Lie algebra. Note that the adjoint action of
R_{x}
on
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{r})
factors through
R_{x}/R_{x}^{\circ}\simeq\pi^{G}_{1}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})
.

Lemma 7.2.7
.

Suppose that
G
is semisimple and simply connected.
The following are true:

(i)

There are natural identifications
\Pic(\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{reg}})\xrightarrow{\sim}\Pic(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})
and
\Pic(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak{X}(R_{x})
.

(ii)

There are isomorphisms

\Pic(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{C}\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{2}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}},\mathbb{C})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak{X}(R_{x})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{C}=\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{r})^{\pi_{1}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})},

where the first map is
c_{1}
.

(iii)

Restriction along the embedding
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{reg}}
induces an isomorphism
H^{2}(\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{reg}},\mathbb{C})\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{2}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}},\mathbb{C})
.

Proof.

(i): the first isomorphism follows from the inequality
\operatorname{codim}(\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{reg}}-\widetilde{\mathbb{O}},\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{reg}})\geqslant 2
and the fact that
\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{reg}}
is smooth. The second isomorphism is standard, see e.g.

[
KKV89
, Proposition 3.2]
.

(ii): The isomorphism
H^{2}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}},\mathbb{C})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{r})^{\pi_{1}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})}
is also standard, see e.g.

[
BC12
, Theorem 3.3]
. Under the identifications
H^{2}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}},\mathbb{C})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{r})^{\pi_{1}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})}
and
\Pic(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak{X}(R_{x})
, the Chern class map
c_{1}:\Pic(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})\rightarrow H^{2}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}},\mathbb{C})
coincides with the natural map
\mathfrak{X}(R_{x})\rightarrow\mathfrak{X}(R_{x})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{C}=\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{r})^{\pi_{1}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})}
. (ii) follows at once.

(iii): For
i\geq 0
, let
\widetilde{X}^{i}
denote the union of
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}\subset\widetilde{X}
and all
G
-orbits in
\widetilde{X}
of codimension
\leq 2i
. Since
G
acts on
\widetilde{X}
with finitely many orbits,
\widetilde{X}=\widetilde{X}^{N}
for some
N\geq 1
. Thus, it suffices to show that
H^{2}(\widetilde{X}^{i+1},\mathbb{C})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}H^{2}(\widetilde{X}^{i},\mathbb{C})
is an isomorphism for all
i
. Let
Y_{1},...,Y_{m}\subset\widetilde{X}^{i+1}
denote the
G
-orbits of codimension
2i
, so that
\widetilde{X}^{i+1}=\widetilde{X}^{i}\cup\bigcup_{k=1}^{m}Y_{k}
. For each
k
, choose a tubular neighborhood
U_{k}\subset\widetilde{X}^{i+1}
of
Y_{k}
and set
U_{k}^{\times}:=U_{k}\cap\widetilde{X}^{i}
. There is a Mayer-Vietoris sequence

\displaystyle\ldots\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}H^{1}(\widetilde{X}^{i},\mathbb{C})\oplus\bigoplus H^{1}(U_{k},\mathbb{C})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\bigoplus H^{1}(U_{k}^{\times},\mathbb{C})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}H^{2}(\widetilde{X}^{i+1},\mathbb{C})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}H^{2}(\widetilde{X}^{i},\mathbb{C})\oplus\bigoplus H^{2}(U_{k},\mathbb{C})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\bigoplus H^{2}(U_{k}^{\times},\mathbb{C})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\ldots

Note that
U_{k}
and
U_{k}^{\times}
are fibrations over
Y_{k}
with fibers
D_{2i}
and
D_{2i}^{\times}
respectively, where
D_{2i}
is the
2i
-dimensional disc and
D_{2}^{\times}
is its puncture. Since

H^{1}(D_{2i},\mathbb{Q})=H^{2}(D_{2i},\mathbb{C})=H^{1}(D_{2i}^{\times},\mathbb{C})=H^{2}(D_{2i}^{\times},\mathbb{C})=0,

the maps

H^{j}(Y_{k},\mathbb{C})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}H^{j}(U_{k},\mathbb{C}),\quad H^{j}(Y_{k},\mathbb{C})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}H^{j}(U_{k}^{\times},\mathbb{C}),\qquad j=1,2

are isomorphisms. It follows that the restriction maps
H^{j}(U_{i},\mathbb{C})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}H^{j}(U_{i}^{\times},\mathbb{C})
are isomorphisms, and therefore
H^{2}(\widetilde{X}^{i+1},\mathbb{C})\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{2}(\widetilde{X}^{i},\mathbb{C})
, as asserted.
∎

7.3.
Parabolic induction of Hamiltonian quantizations

In this section, we will define the notion of parabolic induction for Hamiltonian quantizations of nilpotent covers.

Choose a parabolic subgroup
P\subset G
with Levi decomposition
P=LN
. Let
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{L}
be an
L
-equivariant nilpotent cover and let
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}=\mathrm{Bind}^{G}_{L}\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{L}
. Consider the partial resolution
\rho:\widetilde{Y}:=G\times^{P}(\widetilde{X}_{L}\times\mathfrak{p}^{\perp})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\widetilde{X}
defined in Section
7.1
.

Starting with a Hamiltonian quantization
\mathcal{A}_{L}
of
\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{L}]
, we will produce a Hamiltonian quantization
\Ind^{G}_{L}\mathcal{A}_{L}
of
\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}]
, thus defining a map

\Ind^{G}_{L}:\mathrm{Quant}^{L}(\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{L}])\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathrm{Quant}^{G}(\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}])

called
parabolic induction
.

Our construction requires a bit of additional notation.

•

Let
\mu_{L}:\widetilde{X}_{L}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\overline{\mathbb{O}}_{L}\subset\mathfrak{l}^{*}
denote the (classical) moment map for the right
L
-action on
\widetilde{X}_{L}
.

•

Let
\Phi_{L}:\mathfrak{l}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathcal{A}_{L}
denote the (quantum) co-moment map for the right
L
-action on
\mathcal{A}_{L}
.

•

Let
\mathcal{D}_{L}
denote the microlocalization of
\mathcal{A}_{L}
over
\widetilde{X}_{L}
.

•

Let
\mathfrak{D}_{G/N}
denote the sheaf of differential operators on
G/N
(regarded as a sheaf in the conical topology on
T^{*}(G/N)
). Since
L
normalizes
N
, there is a right
L
-action on
G/N
and hence on
T^{*}(G/N)
and
\mathfrak{D}_{G/N}
.

•

Let

\mu_{G/N}:T^{*}(G/N)\simeq G\times^{N}(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{n})^{*}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{n})^{*}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}(\mathfrak{q}/\mathfrak{n})^{*}\simeq\mathfrak{l}^{*}

denote the (classical) moment map for the right
L
-action on
T^{*}(G/N)
: the map
\mu^{*}_{G/N}
sends any element of
\mathfrak{l}
to the corresponding vector field viewed as a function on
T^{*}(G/N)
.

•

Let
\Phi^{0}_{G/N}:\mathfrak{l}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Gamma(T^{*}(G/N),\mathfrak{D}_{G/N})
denote the usual (quantum) co-moment map for the right
L
-action on
\mathfrak{D}_{G/N}
, i.e.
\Phi^{0}_{G/N}
takes
\xi\in\mathfrak{l}
to the right-invariant vector field on
G/N
determined by
\xi
.

•

Let
\Phi_{G/N}:=\Phi_{G/N}^{0}+\rho_{\mathfrak{n}}
, where
\rho_{\mathfrak{n}}
denotes one-dimensional representation of
\mathfrak{l}
defined in (
3.4.2
).

Consider the product variety
T^{*}(G/N)\times\widetilde{X}_{L}
with the diagonal left
L
-action

l(d,x):=(dl^{-1},lx),\qquad l\in L,\ d\in T^{*}(G/N),\ x\in\widetilde{X}_{L}.

This action has a moment map

\mu:=-\mu_{G/N}+\mu_{\widetilde{X}_{L}}:T^{*}(G/N)\times\widetilde{X}_{L}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathfrak{l}^{*}.

Note that

\mu^{-1}(0)\simeq G\times^{N}(\widetilde{X}_{L}\times\mathfrak{p}^{\perp})\subset G\times^{N}(\widetilde{X}_{L}\times\mathfrak{n}^{\perp})\simeq T^{*}(G/N)\times\widetilde{X}_{L}.

and therefore

\mu^{-1}(0)/L\simeq G\times^{P}(\widetilde{X}_{L}\times\mathfrak{p}^{\perp})=\widetilde{Y}.

Form the completed tensor product

\mathcal{D}^{\prime}:=\mathfrak{D}_{G/N}\ \widehat{\otimes}\ \mathcal{D}_{L}.

Here, the completion is defined with respect to the filtrations on the factors. Note that
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
is an
L
-equivariant filtered quantization of
T^{*}(G/N)\times\widetilde{X}_{L}
with a naturally defined quantum co-moment map

\Phi:=-\Phi_{G/N}\otimes 1+1\otimes\Phi_{L}:\mathfrak{l}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Gamma(T^{*}(G/N)\times\widetilde{X}_{L},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}).

Let
\mathrm{Ind}^{G}_{L}\mathcal{D}_{L}
denote the quantum Hamiltonian reduction of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
at
0\in\mathfrak{l}^{*}
. More precisely, form the left ideal

I:=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\ \mathrm{Span}\{\Phi(\xi)\mid\xi\in\mathfrak{l}\}\subset\mathcal{D}^{\prime}.

The quotient
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}/I
is an
L
-equivariant sheaf of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
-modules on
T^{*}(G/N)\times\widetilde{X}_{L}
, set-theoretically supported on
\mu^{-1}(0)
. Consider the quotient morphism
q:\mu^{-1}(0)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\widetilde{Y}
and set

\Ind^{G}_{L}\mathcal{D}_{L}:=\left(q_{*}[\mathcal{D}^{\prime}/I]\right)^{L}.

Since the
L
-action on
\mu^{-1}(0)
is free,
\Ind^{G}_{L}\mathcal{D}_{L}
is a filtered quantization of
\widetilde{Y}
, see e.g.
[
Los12
, Lem 3.3.1]
. It has a left
G
-action (coming from the left
G
-action on
\mathfrak{D}_{G/N}
) and a quantum co-moment map (coming from the quantum co-moment map
\Phi_{G,G/N}\otimes 1:\mathfrak{g}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Gamma(\mathfrak{D}_{G/N}\widehat{\otimes}\mathcal{D}_{L})
, where
\Phi_{G,G/N}:\mathfrak{g}\rightarrow\Gamma(\mathfrak{D}_{G/N})
is the quantum comoment map for the
G
-action, taking
\xi\in\mathfrak{g}
to the corresponding vector field on
G/N
).

Finally, define

\Ind^{G}_{L}\mathcal{A}_{L}:=\Gamma(\widetilde{Y},\Ind^{G}_{L}\mathcal{D}_{L}).

We now show that
\Ind^{G}_{L}\mathcal{A}_{L}
is a quantization of
\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}]=\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{X}]
. The following lemma is standard.

Lemma 7.3.1
.

Let
X_{1},X_{2}
be normal graded Poisson varieties and let
\rho:X_{1}\rightarrow X_{2}
be a
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
-equivariant Poisson morphism such that
\mathcal{O}_{X_{2}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\rho_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X_{1}}
and
R^{1}\rho_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X_{1}}=0
. Then for any filtered quantization
\mathcal{D}_{1}
of
X_{1}
,
\rho_{*}\mathcal{D}_{1}
is a filtered quantization of
X_{2}
.

We provide a proof for the reader’s convenience.

Proof.

The proof reduces to the case when
X_{2}
is affine. The algebra
\Gamma(X_{1},\mathcal{D}_{1})
comes with a complete and separated filtration induced from the filtration on
\mathcal{D}_{1}
. We wish to show that the embedding
\gr\Gamma(\mathcal{D}_{1})\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}[X_{1}](=\mathbb{C}[X_{2}])
is an isomorphism or, equivalently, that every homogeneous element lies in the image. Cover
X_{1}
by
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
-stable open affine subvarieties
U_{1},\ldots,U_{k}
. For a homogeneous element
f\in\mathbb{C}[X_{2}]
of degree
d
we can find elements
\widetilde{f}_{i}\in\Gamma(U_{i},\mathcal{D}_{1,\leqslant d})
whose principal symbols satisfy
\gr\widetilde{f}_{i}=\rho^{*}(f)|_{U_{i}}
. The elements

\widetilde{f}_{i}-\widetilde{f}_{j}
lie in
\Gamma(U_{i}\cap U_{j},\mathcal{D}_{1,\leqslant d-1})
. Let
f_{ij}\in\mathbb{C}[U_{i}\cap U_{j}]
denote the degree
d-1
component of
\widetilde{f}_{i}-\widetilde{f}_{j}
. It is a 1-cocycle. Since
H^{1}(X_{1},\mathcal{O}_{X_{1}})=0
, it is a coboundary: we can find degree
d-1
elements
g_{i}\in\mathbb{C}[U_{i}]
with
f_{ij}=g_{i}-g_{j}
. Lift
g_{i}
to an element
\widetilde{g}_{i}\in\Gamma(U_{i},\mathcal{D}_{1,\leqslant d-1})
. Replacing
\widetilde{f}_{i}
with
\widetilde{f}_{i}-\widetilde{g}_{i}
if necessary we can arrange so that the elements
\widetilde{f}_{i}-\widetilde{f}_{j}
live in degree
\leqslant d-2
. We can iterate this procedure, each time reducing the degree by
1
. This process converges because the filtration on
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is complete and separated. Thus we can assume that
\widetilde{f}_{i}-\widetilde{f}_{j}=0
, i.e. that the elements
\widetilde{f}_{i}
glue to
\widetilde{f}\in\Gamma(\mathcal{D}_{1})
. The principal symbol of this element is
f
and we are done.
∎

By Lemma
4.3.2
, we have
\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{X}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\rho_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Y}}
and
R^{i}\rho_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Y}}=0
for
i>0
. Hence by Lemma
7.3.1
, the filtered algebra
\Ind^{G}_{L}\mathcal{A}_{L}
is a quantization of
\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}]
. Since
\Ind^{G}_{L}\mathcal{D}_{L}
has a Hamiltonian
G
-action, so does
\Ind^{G}_{L}\mathcal{A}_{L}
. Thus, we have defined the desired map
\Ind^{G}_{L}:\mathrm{Quant}^{L}(\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{L}])\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathrm{Quant}^{G}(\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}])
. In fact, below we will see that
\Ind^{G}_{L}\mathcal{A}_{L}
does not depend on the choice of
P
justifying the notation.

Next, we will show that parabolic induction of Hamiltonian quantizations is transitive. Choose a parabolic subgroup
Q=MU\subset G
such that
P\subset Q
and
L\subset M
. Let
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{M}=\mathrm{Bind}^{M}_{L}\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{L}
and
\widetilde{X}_{M}=\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{M}])
.

Lemma 7.3.2
.

Let
\mathcal{A}_{L}
be a Hamiltonian quantization of
\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{L}]
. Then there is an isomorphism of Hamiltonian quantizations

\Ind^{G}_{L}\mathcal{A}_{L}\simeq\Ind^{G}_{M}(\Ind^{M}_{L}\mathcal{A}_{L}).

Proof.

It is clear from the construction that the central part of the co-moment map is preserved under induction. Thus, we can reduce to the case when
\mathfrak{g}
is semisimple. In this case, the Hamiltonian structure is determined uniquely, see Lemma
4.11.2
. So it suffices to exhibit an isomorphism of filtered quantizations.

Consider the partial resolution
\overline{\rho}:\widetilde{Z}:=G\times^{Q}(\widetilde{X}_{M}\times\mathfrak{q}^{\perp})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\widetilde{X}
. Note that
\rho:\widetilde{Y}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\widetilde{X}
factors through a proper birational map
\rho^{\prime}:\widetilde{Y}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\widetilde{Z}
. By the transitivity of pushforwards, it suffices to show that

(7.3.1)

\rho^{\prime}_{*}(\Ind^{G}_{L}\mathcal{D}_{L})\simeq\Ind^{G}_{M}\mathcal{D}_{M}.

as filtered quantizations. Let
\rho_{M}:\widetilde{Y}_{M}=M\times^{M\cap P}(\widetilde{X}_{L}\times\mathfrak{p}^{\perp})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\widetilde{X}_{M}
denote the partial resolution. Consider the variety
T^{*}(G/U)\times\widetilde{Y}_{M}
and the filtered quantization

(7.3.2)

\mathfrak{D}_{G/U}\ \widehat{\otimes}\ \operatorname{Ind}_{L}^{M}\mathcal{D}_{L}.

The quantization
\mathfrak{D}_{G/U}\widehat{\otimes}\mathcal{D}_{M}
appearing in the construction of
\Ind^{G}_{M}\mathcal{D}_{M}
is
the pushforward of (
7.3.2
)
under
\operatorname{id}_{T^{*}(G/U)}\times\rho_{M}
. On the other hand, (
7.3.2
) is the reduction of

(7.3.3)

\mathfrak{D}_{G/U}\ \widehat{\otimes}\ \mathfrak{D}_{M/M\cap N}\ \widehat{\otimes}\ \mathcal{D}_{L}

under the diagonal action of
L
on the second and third factors.

Note that the
M
-action on (
7.3.3
) is trivial on the third factor. Thus, if we reduce (
7.3.3
) under the
M
-action, we recover the filtered quantization

\mathfrak{D}_{G/N}\ \widehat{\otimes}\ \mathcal{D}_{L}
.
The quantum co-moment map for the
L
-action on
\mathfrak{D}_{G/N}\ \widehat{\otimes}\ \mathcal{D}_{L}
is induced from the quantum co-moment map on (
7.3.3
), and is therefore shifted by the character

\rho_{\mathfrak{n}}=\rho_{\mathfrak{u}}|_{\mathfrak{l}}+\rho_{\mathfrak{u}\cap\mathfrak{m}}
. So if we reduce
(
7.3.3
) under the action of
M\times L
we recover the quantization
\Ind^{G}_{L}\mathcal{D}_{L}
.

Thus, the left hand side of (
7.3.1
) is obtained from (
7.3.2
) by first pushing forward along the map
\operatorname{id}_{T^{*}(G/U)}\times\rho_{M}
and then reducing under the
M
-action. The right hand side of (
7.3.1
) is obtained from
(
7.3.2
) by first reducing under the
M
-action and then pushing forward along

\rho^{\prime}
. Note that
\rho^{\prime}
is the morphism induced by
\widehat{\rho}:=\operatorname{id}_{T^{*}(G/U)}\times\rho_{M}
under classical Hamiltonian reduction. The group
M
acts freely on both
T^{*}(G/U)\times\widetilde{X}_{M}
and
T^{*}(G/U)\times\widetilde{Y}_{M}
. Note that the morphisms
\widehat{\rho}
and
\rho^{\prime}
satisfy the conditions of Lemma
7.3.1
. Write
\bullet/\!/\!/M
for the quantum Hamiltonian reduction of a Hamiltonian quantization by a free action of
M
. To prove (
7.3.1
), it suffices to show for an arbitrary Hamiltonian quantization
\mathcal{D}
of
T^{*}(G/U)\times\widetilde{Y}_{M}
, there is an isomorphism of quantizations

(\widehat{\rho}_{*}\mathcal{D})/\!/\!/M\xrightarrow{\sim}\rho^{\prime}_{*}(\mathcal{D}/\!/\!/M)
. We note that Hamiltonian reduction sends quantizations to quantizations because the action of
M
is free, while
\rho^{\prime}_{*},\widehat{\rho}_{*}
send quantizations to quantizations thanks to Lemma
7.3.1
. Note that there is a natural homomorphism

(\widehat{\rho}_{*}\mathcal{D})/\!/\!/M\rightarrow\rho^{\prime}_{*}(\mathcal{D}/\!/\!/M)
with associated graded equal to the identity map on
\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Z}}
. Since both sides are filtered quantizations, this homomorphism is an isomorphism.

∎

Now suppose
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{L}
is birationally rigid. By Theorem
4.7.6
, quantizations of
\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}]
are parameterized by
W^{\widetilde{X}}
-orbits on
\mathfrak{P}^{\widetilde{X}}
. By Proposition
7.2.2
, the latter space is identified with
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}\cap[\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}])
. Thus, we get a bijection

(7.3.4)

\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}\cap[\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}])/W^{\widetilde{X}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Quant}(\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}]),\qquad W^{\widetilde{X}}\cdot\beta\mapsto\mathcal{A}_{\eta(\beta)}^{\widetilde{X}}.

Abusing notation slightly, we will often abbreviate
\mathcal{A}_{\eta(\beta)}^{\widetilde{X}}
by writing
\mathcal{A}_{\beta}^{\widetilde{X}}
. The isomorphism
\eta:\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}\cap[\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}])\simeq\mathfrak{P}^{\widetilde{X}}
of Proposition
7.2.2
extends to an isomorphism (still denoted by
\eta
)

\eta:\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}\cap[\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}])\oplus\mathfrak{z}(\mathfrak{g})^{*}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak{P}^{\widetilde{X}}\oplus\mathfrak{z}(\mathfrak{g})^{*}=:\overline{\mathfrak{P}}^{\widetilde{X}}

Hence by Proposition
4.11.3
, there is a bijection

(7.3.5)

\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})/W^{\widetilde{X}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Quant}^{G}(\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}]),\qquad W^{\widetilde{X}}\cdot\beta\mapsto(\mathcal{A}^{\widetilde{X}}_{\beta},\Phi^{\widetilde{X}}_{\beta})

Here,
\Phi_{\beta}^{\widetilde{X}}:U(\mathfrak{g})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathcal{A}_{\beta}^{\widetilde{X}}
is the (unique) quantum co-moment map satisfying

\Phi_{\beta}^{\widetilde{X}}|_{\mathfrak{z}(\mathfrak{g})}=\beta|_{\mathfrak{z}(\mathfrak{g})},

see Section
4.11
. The next proposition shows that the quantization parameter
\beta
is preserved under
\Ind^{G}_{M}
.

Proposition 7.3.3
.

For each
\beta\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})
, there is an isomorphism of Hamiltonian quantizations

(7.3.6)

\Ind^{G}_{M}\mathcal{A}_{\beta}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}}\simeq\mathcal{A}_{\beta}^{\widetilde{X}}.

Proof.

As in the proof of Lemma
7.3.2
, we can assume that
\mathfrak{g}
is semisimple. Thanks to Lemma
7.3.2
, we can also assume that
M=L
. Consider the
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial terminalization
\widetilde{Y}:=G\times^{P}(\widetilde{X}_{L}\times\mathfrak{p}^{\perp})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\widetilde{X}
, and its universal graded Poisson deformation

\widetilde{Y}_{\mathrm{univ}}=G\times^{P}(\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})\times\widetilde{X}_{L}\times\mathfrak{p}^{\perp}),

see Proposition
7.2.2
(iii). Let
\mathcal{D}^{\widetilde{Y}_{\mathrm{univ}}}
(resp.
\mathcal{D}^{\widetilde{X}_{L}}
) denote the canonical quantization of
\widetilde{Y}_{\mathrm{univ}}
(resp.
\widetilde{X}_{L}
), see Proposition
4.7.4
. Consider the filtered quantization
\overline{\mathcal{D}}^{\widetilde{Y}_{\mathrm{univ}}}
of
\widetilde{Y}_{\mathrm{univ}}
defined by

\overline{\mathcal{D}}^{\widetilde{Y}_{\mathrm{univ}}}:=\left([\mathfrak{D}_{G/N}\ \widehat{\otimes}\ \mathcal{D}^{{\widetilde{X}_{L}}}]/[\mathfrak{D}_{G/N}\ \widehat{\otimes}\ \mathcal{D}^{\widetilde{X}_{L}}]\Phi([\mathfrak{l},\mathfrak{l}])\right)^{L},

By
[
Los12
, Theorem 5.4.1]
,
\overline{\mathcal{D}}^{\widetilde{Y}_{\mathrm{univ}}}
is an even quantization (more precisely, its restriction to
\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{reg}}_{\mathrm{univ}}
is the specialization of a graded even quantization). Thus, by the results of
[
Los12
, Section 2.3]
, there is an isomorphism of filtered quantizations

\overline{\mathcal{D}}^{\widetilde{Y}_{\mathrm{univ}}}\simeq\mathcal{D}^{\widetilde{Y}_{\mathrm{univ}}}

Specializing both sides to
\beta\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})
, we get an isomorphism of filtered quantizations

\Ind^{G}_{L}\mathcal{D}_{\beta}^{\widetilde{X}_{L}}\simeq\mathcal{D}_{\beta}^{\widetilde{Y}}.

Now, the result follows by taking global sections.

∎

Using the classification of filtered quantizations of
\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{X}]
, see
Theorem
4.7.6
, and
Proposition
7.3.3
we obtain the following.

Corollary 7.3.4
.

Let
\mathcal{A}_{M}
be a Hamiltonian quantization of
\widetilde{X}_{M}
. Then, up to an isomorphism of Hamiltonian quantizations,
\Ind^{G}_{M}\mathcal{A}_{M}
depends only on
M
(and not on
Q
).

7.4.
Namikawa spaces vs parabolic induction

In this section we begin the task of computing the isomorphism
\eta:\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}\cap[\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}])\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak{P}^{\widetilde{X}}
. Here we establish some basic facts about the behavior of Namikawa spaces under parabolic induction.

Choose a parabolic subgroup
P=LN\subset G
and a birationally rigid
L
-equivariant nilpotent cover
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{L}
such that
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}=\mathrm{Bind}^{G}_{L}\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{L}
. Choose also a parabolic subgroup
Q=MU\subset G
such that
P\subset Q
and
L\subset M
. Let
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{M}=\mathrm{Bind}^{M}_{L}\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{L}
and
P_{M}=P\cap M
, a parabolic in
M
. Consider the
\mathbb{Q}
-terminalizations

\rho:\widetilde{Y}=G\times^{P}(\widetilde{X}_{L}\times\mathfrak{p}^{\perp})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\widetilde{X},\qquad\rho_{M}:\widetilde{Y}_{M}=M\times^{P_{M}}(\widetilde{X}_{L}\times\mathfrak{p}_{M}^{\perp})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\widetilde{X}_{M}

Note that
\widetilde{Y}_{M}
is an
M
-equivariant fiber bundle over
M/P_{M}=Q/P
. Hence
G\times^{Q}(\widetilde{Y}_{M}\times\mathfrak{q}^{\perp})
is a
G
-equivariant fiber bundle over
G/P
. Also
G\times^{Q}(\widetilde{Y}_{M}\times\mathfrak{q}^{\perp})
has a
G
-equivariant projective birational morphism to
G\times^{Q}(\widetilde{X}_{M}\times\mathfrak{q}^{\perp})
and hence to
\widetilde{X}
. Note that both varieties
\widetilde{Y},G\times^{Q}(\widetilde{Y}_{M}\times\mathfrak{q}^{\perp})

come with natural
G\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}
-actions
(on
G\times^{Q}(\widetilde{X}_{M}\times\mathfrak{q}^{\perp})
the factor
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
acts fiberwise via
t.(y,z)=(ty,t^{d}z)
for
t\in\mathbb{C}^{\times},y\in\widetilde{Y}_{M},z\in\mathfrak{q}^{\perp}
, where
d
is the weight of the Poisson bracket on
\widetilde{Y}_{M}
), and the morphisms to
\widetilde{X}
are
G\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}
-equivariant.

Lemma 7.4.1
.

There is a
G\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}
-equivariant isomorphism

\widetilde{Y}\xrightarrow{\sim}G\times^{Q}(\widetilde{Y}_{M}\times\mathfrak{q}^{\perp})

intertwining the morphisms to
\widetilde{X}
and to
G/P
.

Proof.

The variety
G\times^{Q}(\widetilde{Y}_{M}\times\mathfrak{q}^{\perp})
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial terminal and so is a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial terminalization of
\widetilde{X}
. The fiber of
0\in\widetilde{X}
is
G/P
so restricting a relatively ample line bundle from
G\times^{Q}(\widetilde{Y}_{M}\times\mathfrak{q}^{\perp})
to
G/P
gives an ample line bundle. Thus, by Proposition
7.1.3
, the ample cone of
G\times^{Q}(\widetilde{Y}_{M}\times\mathfrak{q}^{\perp})

is contained in that of
\widetilde{Y}
.
By Lemma
7.2.3
, there is an isomorphism of
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial terminalizations of
\widetilde{X}

(7.4.1)

\widetilde{Y}\xrightarrow{\sim}G\times^{Q}(\widetilde{Y}_{M}\times\mathfrak{q}^{\perp}).

Such an isomorphism is necessarily unique because the terminalization morphisms are birational. In particular, it is
G\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}
-equivariant. The morphisms
\widetilde{Y},G\times^{Q}(\widetilde{Y}_{M}\times\mathfrak{q}^{\perp})\rightarrow G/P
are recovered as the categorical quotient morphisms for the action of
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
. Hence, they are intertwined by (
7.4.1
) as well.
∎

For the next result, we will assume that
G
is semisimple in order to simplify the statements. Let
{\pi}^{\prime}
denote the composition of
\pi:\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{reg}}\rightarrow G/P
with the projection
G/P\rightarrow G/Q
.
Consider the composition

(7.4.2)

{\eta}^{\prime}:\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{m})\simeq H^{2}(G/Q,\mathbb{C})\overset{{\pi}^{{}^{\prime}*}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}}H^{2}(\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{reg}},\mathbb{C})=\mathfrak{P}^{\widetilde{X}}.

Note that
{\eta}^{\prime}
coincides with the composition

(7.4.3)

\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{m})\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})\xrightarrow{\eta}\mathfrak{P}^{\widetilde{X}}.

Thanks to Lemma
7.4.1
, we can view
\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{reg}}
as a fiber bundle over
G/Q
with fiber
\widetilde{Y}_{M}^{\mathrm{reg}}\times\mathfrak{q}^{\perp}
. In particular, there are pullback maps

(7.4.4)

H^{2}(G/Q,\mathbb{C})\rightarrow H^{2}(\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{reg}},\mathbb{C}),\qquad H^{2}(\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{reg}},\mathbb{C})\rightarrow H^{2}(\widetilde{Y}_{M}^{\mathrm{reg}},\mathbb{C}).

We wish to describe (
7.4.4
) under the identifications

(7.4.5)

\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{m})\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{2}(G/Q,\mathbb{C}),\quad\eta:\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{2}(\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{reg}},\mathbb{C}),\quad\eta_{M}:\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}\cap[\mathfrak{m},\mathfrak{m}])\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{2}(\widetilde{Y}_{M}^{\mathrm{reg}},\mathbb{C}).

Proposition 7.4.2
.

Under the identifications in (
7.4.5
), the pullback maps in (
7.4.4
) correspond to the inclusion
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{m})\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})
and the projection
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})\twoheadrightarrow\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}\cap[\mathfrak{m},\mathfrak{m}])
, respectively.

Proof.

Since
\eta^{\prime}
coincides with (
7.4.3
),
H^{2}(G/Q)\rightarrow H^{2}(\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{reg}},\mathbb{C})
is indeed the inclusion
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{m})\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})
. Next we show that
H^{2}(\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{reg}},\mathbb{C})\rightarrow H^{2}(\widetilde{Y}_{M}^{\mathrm{reg}},\mathbb{C})
coincides with
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})\twoheadrightarrow\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}\cap[\mathfrak{m},\mathfrak{m}])
. There is a commutative diagram
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{Y}_{M}^{\mathrm{reg}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{reg}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M/P_{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G/P}

Here the vertical maps are the projections, while the horizontal maps are the fiber inclusions (for fiber bundles over
G/Q
). We have
H^{2}(G/P,\mathbb{C})\simeq\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}),H^{2}(M/P_{M},\mathbb{C})\simeq\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}\cap[\mathfrak{m},\mathfrak{m}])
, and the pullback map for the bottom arrow is the projection
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})\twoheadrightarrow\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}\cap[\mathfrak{m},\mathfrak{m}])
. Since the pullback maps for the vertical arrows are
\eta_{M}
and
\eta
, we are done.

∎

7.5.
Partial resolutions from codimension 2 leaves

In this section, we will provide a Lie-theoretic description of the partial Namikawa spaces
\mathfrak{P}_{k}^{\widetilde{X}}
,
k\geq 1
, under some additional restrictions. Assume
G
is semisimple in order to simplify the statements.

Choose a Levi subgroup
L\subset G
and a birationally rigid
L
-equivariant nilpotent cover
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{L}
such that
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}=\mathrm{Bind}^{G}_{L}\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{L}
. Choose a codimension 2 leaf
\mathfrak{L}_{k}\subset\widetilde{X}
and let
\Sigma_{k}
be the corresponding Kleinian singularity. There is a closed embedding
\Sigma_{k}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{X}
, constructed as follows. The image of
\overline{\mathfrak{L}}_{k}
under the moment map
\mu:\widetilde{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\overline{\mathbb{O}}\subset\mathfrak{g}^{*}
is the closure of a codimension
2
orbit
\mathbb{O}^{\prime}\subset\overline{\mathbb{O}}
. Consider the Slodowy slice
S^{\prime}
to
\mathbb{O}^{\prime}
in
\mathfrak{g}^{*}
. Recall from Section
3.5
that
S^{\prime}
comes with a natural contracting
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
-action. Since
S^{\prime}
is transverse to
\mathbb{O}^{\prime}
,
\mu^{-1}(S^{\prime})
is transverse to every leaf in
\mu^{-1}(\mathbb{O}^{\prime})
. Thanks to the contracting
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
-action on
S^{\prime}
,
\mu^{-1}(S^{\prime})
splits into a disjoint union of connected components indexed by the points in
\mu^{-1}(S^{\prime}\cap\mathbb{O}^{\prime})
. Choose a point in this set lying in
\mathfrak{L}_{k}
and let
\Sigma_{k}
be a connected component of
\mu^{-1}(S^{\prime})
containing this point. Note that
\Sigma_{k}
is a Kleinian singularity and
\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{C}[\Sigma_{k}]^{\wedge})
is a formal slice to
\mathfrak{L}_{k}
.

Assume for the remainder of this subsection that
H^{2}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}},\mathbb{C})=0
. By Lemma
7.2.7
, this is equivalent to the condition
\mathfrak{P}_{0}^{\widetilde{X}}=0
. Under this assumption, we will define, for each codimension 2 leaf
\mathfrak{L}_{k}\subset\widetilde{X}
, a Levi subgroup
M_{k}\subset G
containing
L
which is ‘adapted’ to
\mathfrak{L}_{k}
. We will show that this Levi subgroup is uniquely determined by the following property: for any parabolic
Q\subset G
with Levi factor
M_{k}
, there is a partial resolution
\rho_{k}:\widetilde{Z}_{k}=G\times^{Q}(\widetilde{X}_{M_{k}}\times\mathfrak{q}^{\perp})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\widetilde{X}
such that:

•

\rho_{k}
is a minimal resolution over
\Sigma_{k}\subset\widetilde{X}
.

•

\rho_{k}
is an isomorphism over
\Sigma_{j}\subset\widetilde{X}

j\neq k
.

In other words,
\rho_{k}
resolves
\Sigma_{k}
and preserves all other slices. Replacing
G
with a covering group if necessary, we can (and will) assume that
G
is simply connected. To simplify the notation we write
\mathfrak{P}
instead of
\mathfrak{P}^{\widetilde{X}}
and
\mathfrak{P}_{k}
instead of
\mathfrak{P}^{\widetilde{X}}_{k}
.

Recall, see Proposition
7.2.2
, that a choice of parabolic
P\subset G
with Levi factor
L
gives rise to an identification
\eta:\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak{P}
. Different parabolics
P
give rise to different identifications, but these identifications coincide up to the
W^{\widetilde{X}}
-action on the target, see Proposition
7.2.5
.

Lemma 7.5.1
.

There is a parabolic
P
with Levi factor
L
such that the intersection
\mathfrak{C}:=\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})_{\mathbb{R}}^{\geqslant 0}\cap\eta^{-1}(\mathfrak{P}_{\mathbb{R},k}^{\geqslant 0})
is a face in
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})_{\mathbb{R}}^{\geqslant 0}
of dimension
\dim\mathfrak{P}_{k}
.

Proof.

Since
\mathfrak{P}_{\mathbb{R},0}\simeq H^{2}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}},\mathbb{R})=0
, the product
\prod_{i}\mathfrak{P}_{\mathbb{R},i}^{\geq 0}
is a fundamental chamber for the
W^{\widetilde{X}}
-action on
\mathfrak{P}_{\mathbb{R}}
, and
\mathfrak{P}_{\mathbb{R},k}
is a face of this chamber. By Theorem
4.9.1
, the ample cones of all
\mathbb{Q}
-terminalizations of
\widetilde{X}
partition the fundamental chamber. Hence, there is a
\mathbb{Q}
-terminalization
\widetilde{Y}
of
\widetilde{X}
such that the intersection
\eta(\mathfrak{C}):=\mathrm{Amp}(\widetilde{Y})\cap\mathfrak{P}_{\mathbb{R},k}
is of dimension
\dim\mathfrak{P}_{\mathbb{R},k}
. Note that
\eta(\mathfrak{C})
is automatically a face of
\mathrm{Amp}(\widetilde{Y})
. By Lemma
7.2.3
,
\widetilde{Y}=G\times^{P}(\widetilde{X}_{L}\times\mathfrak{p}^{\perp})
for a parabolic
P
with Levi factor
L
and
\mathrm{Amp}(\widetilde{Y})=\eta(\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})_{\mathbb{R}}^{\geq 0})
. Hence,
\mathfrak{C}=\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})_{\mathbb{R}}^{\geq 0}\cap\eta^{-1}(\mathfrak{P}_{\mathbb{R},k}^{\geq 0})
is a face of
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})_{\mathbb{R}}^{\geq 0}
of dimension
\dim\mathfrak{P}_{\mathbb{R},k}
.
∎

Choose
\mathfrak{C}
as in Lemma
7.5.1
. Let
\mathfrak{m}_{k}
be the Levi subalgebra

(7.5.1)

\mathfrak{m}_{k}:=\mathfrak{h}\oplus\bigoplus_{\alpha\in\Delta(\mathfrak{C})}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha},\qquad\Delta(\mathfrak{C}):=\{\alpha\in\Delta\mid\langle\alpha^{\vee},\mathfrak{C}\rangle=0\},

and let
M_{k}
be the corresponding Levi subgroup of
G
. By construction,
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{m}_{k})_{\mathbb{R}}=\mathbb{R}\mathfrak{C}
and
\eta(\mathbb{R}\mathfrak{C})=\mathfrak{P}_{\mathbb{R},k}
. Thus,
\eta
restricts to an isomorphism

(7.5.2)

\eta_{k}:\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{m}_{k})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak{P}_{k}

Remark 7.5.2
.

Note that the Levi subgroup
M_{k}\subset G
is uniquely determined by the triple
(L,\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{L},\mathfrak{L}_{k})
. Indeed,
\mathfrak{m}_{k}
is the Levi subalgebra corresponding to the co-roots which vanish on the subspace
\eta^{-1}(\mathfrak{P}_{k})
. And by Proposition
7.2.5
, this subspace is independent of
P
.

Next, we will give a geometric characterization of the Levi subgroup
M_{k}
. It will be convenient to introduce some additional terminology.

Definition 7.5.3
.

A
resolution datum
for
L
is a triple
(P,M,Q)
consisting of a parabolic
P\subset G
with Levi factor
L
, a Levi subgroup
M\subset G
containing
L
, and a parabolic
Q\subset G
containing
P
with Levi factor
M
. A resolution datum is said to be
adapted to the leaf

\mathfrak{L}_{k}\subset\widetilde{X}
if
M=M_{k}
.

Remark 7.5.4
.

If
M
and
Q
are fixed, we can always find a resolution datum
(P,M,Q)
. However, if
M
and
P
are fixed, it may not be possible to do so. Nonetheless, we can always find a resolution datum
(P^{\prime},M,Q)
such that the identification
\eta^{\prime}:\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak{P}
corresponding to
P^{\prime}
coincides with
\eta
. Indeed,
\eta
is determined by a choice of fundamental chamber for the
W^{\widetilde{X}}
-action on
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})_{\mathbb{R}}
.
Take
P^{\prime}
such that
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})_{\mathbb{R}}^{\geq 0}
intersects
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{m}_{k})
. Since every fundamental chamber intersects
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{m}_{k})
, such a
P^{\prime}
exists.

A choice of resolution datum
(P,M,Q)
gives rise to the following package of data:

•

A
\mathbb{Q}
-terminalization
\rho:\widetilde{Y}:=G\times^{P}(\widetilde{X}_{L}\times\mathfrak{p}^{\perp})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\widetilde{X}
.

•

A projection
\pi:\widetilde{Y}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}G/P
.

•

An
M
-equivariant nilpotent cover
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{M}:=\mathrm{Bind}^{M}_{L}\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{L}
.

•

A partial resolution
\overline{\rho}:\widetilde{Z}:=G\times^{Q}(\widetilde{X}_{M}\times\mathfrak{q}^{\perp})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\widetilde{X}
.

•

A projection
\overline{\pi}:\widetilde{Z}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}G/Q
.

•

A
\mathbb{Q}
-terminalization
\rho^{\prime}:\widetilde{Y}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\widetilde{Z}
such that
\rho=\overline{\rho}\circ\rho^{\prime}
.

For an
\mathfrak{L}_{k}
-adapted resolution datum
(P,M_{k},Q)
, we write
\widetilde{Z}_{k}
for
\widetilde{Z}
,
\rho_{k}
for
\overline{\rho}
,
\pi_{k}
for
\overline{\pi}
, and
\rho_{k}^{\prime}
for
\rho^{\prime}
.

Remark 7.5.5
.

Suppose
(P,M,Q)
is a resolution datum for
L
. Under the identifications

\operatorname{Pic}(\widetilde{Z})\simeq\mathfrak{X}(M)
and
\operatorname{Pic}(\widetilde{Y})\simeq\mathfrak{X}(L)
constructed in Section
7.1
, the pullback map

\rho^{{}^{\prime}*}:\operatorname{Pic}(\widetilde{Z})\rightarrow\operatorname{Pic}(\widetilde{Y})
coincides with the inclusion
\mathfrak{X}(M)\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{X}(L)
induced by the restriction of characters.

Proposition 7.5.6
.

Suppose
(P,M,Q)
is a resolution datum for
L
. Then
M=M_{k}
if and only if the following conditions are satisfied:

(i)

The map
\overline{\rho}:\overline{\rho}^{-1}(\Sigma_{k})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Sigma_{k}
is a minimal resolution, and
{\rho}^{\prime}:\rho^{-1}(\Sigma_{k})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\overline{\rho}^{-1}(\Sigma_{k})
is an isomorphism.

(ii)

If
j\neq k
, the map
\overline{\rho}:\overline{\rho}^{-1}(\Sigma_{j})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Sigma_{j}
is an isomorphism, and
{\rho}^{\prime}:\rho^{-1}(\Sigma_{j})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\overline{\rho}^{-1}(\Sigma_{j})
is a minimal resolution.

Proof.

First, we will show that (i) and (ii) are satisfied for any
\mathfrak{L}_{k}
-adapted resolution datum
(P,M_{k},Q)
. Choose a strictly dominant weight
\tau\in\mathfrak{X}(M_{k})^{>0}
and consider the relatively ample line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{\widetilde{Z}_{k}}(\tau)=\pi_{k}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{G/Q}(\tau)
on
\widetilde{Z}_{k}
. Write
\mathcal{L}_{\widetilde{Z}_{k}}(\tau)_{i}
for the restriction of
\mathcal{L}_{\widetilde{Z}_{k}}(\tau)
to
\rho_{k}^{-1}(\Sigma_{i})
. Similarly, define
\mathcal{L}_{\widetilde{Y}}(\tau)
and
\mathcal{L}_{\widetilde{Y}}(\tau)_{i}
.

Consider the line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{\widetilde{Y}}(\tau)_{k}
. Note that
c_{1}(\mathcal{L}_{\widetilde{Y}}(\tau)_{k})=\eta_{k}(\tau)
. Hence,
\mathcal{L}_{\widetilde{Y}}(\tau)_{k}
is relatively ample. There are isomorphisms

(7.5.3)

\mathcal{L}_{\widetilde{Y}}(\tau)_{k}\simeq[\overline{\rho}_{k}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{\widetilde{Z}_{k}}(\tau)]_{k}\simeq\overline{\rho}_{k}^{*}[\mathcal{L}_{\widetilde{Z}_{k}}(\tau)_{k}].

Assume that
\rho^{\prime}_{k}:\rho^{-1}(\Sigma_{k})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\rho_{k}^{-1}(\Sigma_{k})
is
not
an isomorphism, and let
C
be a simple curve in its exceptional divisor. Then by (
7.5.3
), we have
\langle\mathcal{L}_{\widetilde{Y}}(\tau)_{k},C\rangle=0
, contradicting the relative ampleness of
\mathcal{L}_{\widetilde{Y}}(\tau)_{k}
. Thus,
\rho^{\prime}_{k}:\rho^{-1}(\Sigma_{k})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\rho_{k}^{-1}(\Sigma_{k})
is an isomorphism and
\rho_{k}:\rho_{k}^{-1}(\Sigma_{k})\rightarrow\Sigma_{k}
is a minimal resolution.

On the other hand,
\mathcal{L}_{\widetilde{Y}}(\tau)_{i}
is the trivial line bundle on
\rho^{-1}(\Sigma_{i})
, pulled back
from a relatively ample line bundle on
\rho_{k}^{-1}(\Sigma_{i})
. It follows that

\rho_{k}:\rho_{k}^{-1}(\Sigma_{i})\xrightarrow{\sim}\Sigma_{i}
is an isomorphism and hence that

\rho^{\prime}_{k}:\rho^{-1}(\Sigma_{i})\rightarrow\rho_{k}^{-1}(\Sigma_{i})
is a minimal resolution.

Next, we will show that if
(P,M,Q)
is a resolution datum for which (i) and (ii) hold, then
M=M_{k}
. By Remark
7.5.2
, it is enough to show that
\eta(\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{m}))=\mathfrak{P}_{k}
. For each codimension 2 leaf
\mathfrak{L}_{j}\subset\widetilde{X}
let
\mathfrak{S}_{j}=\rho^{-1}(\Sigma_{j})
be the minimal resolution of
\Sigma_{j}
. Consider the map

\eta_{j}^{\prime}:\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{m})\subset\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak{P}\twoheadrightarrow\mathfrak{P}_{j},

where the middle isomorphism is
\eta
. Note that
\eta_{j}^{\prime}
coincides with the composition

\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{m})\simeq H^{2}(\widetilde{Z},\mathbb{C})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}H^{2}(\widetilde{Y},\mathbb{C})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}H^{2}(\mathfrak{S}_{j},\mathbb{C}),

and hence with the composition

\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{m})\simeq H^{2}(\widetilde{Z},\mathbb{C})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}H^{2}(\rho^{-1}(\Sigma_{j}),\mathbb{C})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}H^{2}(\mathfrak{S}_{j},\mathbb{C}).

If
j=k
, then
\overline{\rho}^{-1}(\Sigma_{j})\simeq\mathfrak{S}_{j}
and therefore
\eta_{j}^{\prime}
is an isomorphism. If
j\neq k
, then
\overline{\rho}^{-1}(\Sigma_{j})\simeq\Sigma_{j}
and therefore
\eta_{j}^{\prime}=0
. Hence
\eta(\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{m}))=\mathfrak{P}_{k}
, as asserted.
∎

Suppose
(P,M_{k},Q)
is an
\mathfrak{L}_{k}
-adapted resolution datum. By Proposition
7.5.6
, there is a closed embedding
\mathfrak{S}_{k}\subset\widetilde{Z}_{k}
. This gives rise to a restriction map
\Pic(\widetilde{Z}_{k})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Pic(\mathfrak{S}_{k})
whose image lies in
\Pic(\mathfrak{S}_{k})^{\pi_{1}(\mathfrak{L}_{k})}
. The following lemma is useful for computing
\eta_{k}
.

Lemma 7.5.7
.

The isomorphism
\eta_{k}:\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{m}_{k})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak{P}_{k}
restricts to a group homomorphism
\eta_{k}:\mathfrak{X}(M_{k})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Lambda_{k}^{\pi_{1}(\mathfrak{L}_{k})}
. This homomorphism corresponds to the restriction map
\Pic(\widetilde{Z}_{k})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Pic(\mathfrak{S}_{k})^{\pi_{1}(\mathfrak{L}_{k})}
under the natural identifications
\mathfrak{X}(M_{k})\simeq\Pic(\widetilde{Z}_{k})
and
\Lambda_{k}^{\pi_{1}(\mathfrak{L}_{k})}\simeq\Pic(\mathfrak{S}_{k})^{\pi_{1}(\mathfrak{L}_{k})}
.

Proof.

Recall, Lemma
4.4.6
, that
\operatorname{Pic}(\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{reg}})\otimes\mathbb{C}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak{P}
.
Note that
\eta:\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak{P}
is obtained by base change to
\mathbb{C}
from the abelian group homomorphism

\mathfrak{X}(L)\simeq\Pic(\widetilde{Y})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Pic(\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{reg}}).

Hence,
\eta_{k}
restricts to the map

(7.5.4)

\mathfrak{X}(M_{k})\subset\mathfrak{X}(L)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Pic(\widetilde{Y})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Pic(\mathfrak{S}_{k})^{\pi_{1}(\mathfrak{L}_{k})}\simeq\Lambda_{k}^{\pi_{1}(\mathfrak{L}_{k})}

By Remark
7.5.5
, the inclusion
\mathfrak{X}(M_{k})\subset\mathfrak{X}(L)
corresponds to the pullback map
\Pic(\widetilde{Z}_{k})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Pic(\widetilde{Y})
under the natural identifications
\Pic(\widetilde{Z}_{k})\simeq\mathfrak{X}(M_{k})
and
\Pic(\widetilde{Y})\simeq\mathfrak{X}(L)
. Hence, the homomorphism (
7.5.4
) corresponds to the restriction map
\Pic(\widetilde{Z}_{k})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Pic(\mathfrak{S}_{k})^{\pi_{1}(\mathfrak{L}_{k})}
. This completes the proof.
∎

The conditions appearing in Proposition
7.5.6
are difficult to check in practice. In the remainder of this subsection, we will develop some additional tools for computing
M_{k}
.

Lemma 7.5.8
.

Suppose
(P,M,Q)
is a resolution datum for
L
. Then
M=M_{k}
if and only if the following conditions are satisfied:

(i)

The semisimple corank of
M
equals the dimension of
\mathfrak{P}_{k}
.

(ii)

For every
j\neq k
, the map
\overline{\rho}:\overline{\rho}^{-1}(\Sigma_{j})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Sigma_{j}
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

For
M=M_{k}
, condition (i) is satisfied by Remark
7.5.2
, while (ii) is satisfied by Proposition
7.5.6
. Next we prove that (i) and (ii) imply
M=M_{k}
.
Define
\eta_{j}^{\prime}:\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{m})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathfrak{P}_{j}
as in the proof of Proposition
7.5.6
. Arguing as in the proof of the same proposition, condition (ii) of Lemma
7.5.8
implies that
\eta_{j}^{\prime}=0
for
j\neq k
. By
4.5.1
and Lemma
7.2.7
there is a decomposition
\mathfrak{P}\simeq\bigoplus\mathfrak{P}_{j}
. Since the image of the embedding
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{m})\subset\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})\simeq\mathfrak{P}
intersects trivially with
\bigoplus_{j\neq k}\mathfrak{P}_{j}
,
\eta_{k}^{\prime}
is injective. By condition (i), this implies that
\eta^{\prime}_{k}
is an isomorphism, and thus that
\eta(\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{m}))=\mathfrak{P}_{k}
, as desired.
∎

Our final characterization of
M_{k}
is applicable only in certain special cases. Suppose first of all that
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
is a nilpotent orbit
\mathbb{O}
(rather than a cover). We say that
\overline{\mathbb{O}}
is
normal in codimension 2
if
\overline{\mathbb{O}}
is normal along every codimension 2 orbit
\mathbb{O}^{\prime}\subset\overline{\mathbb{O}}
. This is equivalent to the condition that every dimension 2 slice in
\overline{\mathbb{O}}
is normal (and hence a Kleinian singularity). Consider the moment map
\mu:\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{O}])\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\overline{\mathbb{O}}
.

Lemma 7.5.9
.

If
\mathfrak{L}\subset\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{O}])
is a codimension 2 leaf, then
\mu(\overline{\mathfrak{L}})
is the closure of a codimension 2 orbit
\mathbb{O}^{\prime}\subset\overline{\mathbb{O}}
. This defines a surjection

(7.5.5)

\{\text{codimension 2 leaves in }\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{O}])\}\twoheadrightarrow\{\text{codimension 2 orbits in }\overline{\mathbb{O}}\},\qquad\mathfrak{L}_{k}\mapsto\mathbb{O}_{k}.

If
\overline{\mathbb{O}}
is normal in codimension 2, then this map is a bijection, and the singularity of
\mathfrak{L}_{k}
is equivalent to that of
\mathbb{O}_{k}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
4.6.1
, (
7.5.5
) is well-defined. If
\overline{\mathbb{O}}
is normal in codimension 2, then
\mu:X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\overline{\mathbb{O}}
is an isomorphism over all codimension
2
orbits. In particular, the map (
7.5.5
) is surjective and preserves singularities.
∎

Lemma 7.5.10
.

Suppose
M\subset G
is a Levi subgroup containing
L
. Then
M=M_{k}
if the following conditions are satisfied:

(i)

The semisimple corank of
M
equals the dimension of
\mathfrak{P}_{k}
.

(ii)

Both
\overline{\mathbb{O}}
and
\overline{\mathbb{O}}_{M}
are normal in codimension 2.

(iii)

For every
j\neq k
, there is a nilpotent
M
-orbit
\mathbb{O}_{M,j}\subset\overline{\mathbb{O}}_{M}
of codimension 2 such that

\mathbb{O}_{j}=\mathrm{Ind}^{G}_{M}\mathbb{O}_{M,j}

(iv)

For every
j\neq k
, the singularity of
\mathbb{O}_{j}\subset\overline{\mathbb{O}}
is equivalent to that of
\mathbb{O}_{M,j}\subset\overline{\mathbb{O}}_{M}
.

Proof.

Assume (i). We will show that conditions (ii)-(iv) of Lemma
7.5.10
imply condition (ii) of Lemma
7.5.8
. Then Lemma
7.5.10
will follow from Lemma
7.5.8
. Fix a resolution datum
(P,M,Q)
and consider the partial resolution
\overline{\rho}:Z=G\times^{Q}(X_{M}\times\mathfrak{q}^{\perp})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}{X}
. The restriction
\overline{\rho}:\overline{\rho}^{-1}(\Sigma_{j})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Sigma_{j}
is a partial resolution of the Kleinian singularity
\Sigma_{j}
. We will show that it is an isomorphism for
j\neq k
. Let
x
be a generic point in
G\times^{Q}({\mathbb{O}}_{M,j}\times\mathfrak{q}^{\perp})\subset{Z}
, and let
\mathfrak{L}_{j}\subset X
be a symplectic leaf corresponding to
\mathbb{O}_{j}
under (
7.5.5
). Since
\mathbb{O}_{j}=\mathrm{Ind}^{G}_{M}\mathbb{O}_{M,j}
, we have
\overline{\rho}(x)\in\mathfrak{L}_{j}
. Note that
Z^{\mathrm{reg}}\simeq G\times^{Q}(X_{M}^{\mathrm{reg}}\times\mathfrak{q}^{\perp})
, and
{\mathbb{O}}_{M,j}\subset X_{M}^{\mathrm{sing}}
. Note that
x
belongs to a codimension
2
leaf
\mathfrak{L}\subset{Z}
. The singularity of
\mathfrak{L}\subset{Z}
is equivalent to that of
\mathfrak{L}_{M,j}\subset{X}_{M}
, and hence to
\Sigma_{j}
. Hence, the singularity of
\overline{\rho}^{-1}(\Sigma_{j})
is equivalent to
\Sigma_{j}
. This implies that
\overline{\rho}^{-1}(\Sigma_{j})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Sigma_{j}
is an isomorphism. This is exactly condition (ii) of Lemma
7.5.8
.
∎

Remark 7.5.11
.

There is a version of Lemma
7.5.10
which holds for arbitrary nilpotent covers. The statement is more technical, but the proof is analogous (and we omit it). Let
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
be a
G
-equivariant nilpotent cover and suppose
M\subset G
is a Levi subgroup containing
L
. Then
M=M_{k}
if the following conditions are satisfied:

(i)

The semisimple corank of
M
equals the dimension of
\mathfrak{P}_{k}
.

(ii)

For every codimension 2 orbit
\mathbb{O}^{\prime}\subset\overline{\mathbb{O}}
(resp.
\mathbb{O}^{\prime}\subset\overline{\mathbb{O}}_{M}
), there is at most one codimension 2 leaf
\mathfrak{L}\subset\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}])
(resp.
\mathfrak{L}_{M}\subset\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{M}])
which maps to it under
\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}])\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\overline{\mathbb{O}}
(resp.
\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{M}])\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\overline{\mathbb{O}}_{M}
).

(iii)

If
\mathbb{O}_{j}\subset\overline{\mathbb{O}}
is a codimension 2
G
-orbit which is the image of a codimension 2 leaf
\mathfrak{L}_{j}\subset\widetilde{X}
with
j\neq k
there is a codimension 2
M
-orbit
\mathbb{O}_{M,j}\subset\overline{\mathbb{O}}_{M}
which corresponds to a codimension 2 leaf
\mathfrak{L}_{M,j}\subset\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{M}])
such that

\mathbb{O}_{j}=\mathrm{Ind}^{G}_{M}\mathbb{O}_{M,j}

(iv)

For every such
\mathbb{O}_{j}
, the singularity of
\mathfrak{L}_{j}\subset\widetilde{X}
is equivalent to that of
\mathfrak{L}_{M,j}\subset\widetilde{X}_{M}
.

7.6.
Birationally rigid and 2-leafless covers

In this section, we will collect some facts about orbits which admit birationally rigid covers. As explained in the introduction, such orbits play an important role in the computation of unipotent infinitesimal characters. The following result provides a useful criterion for birational rigidity.

Corollary 7.6.1
.

The following conditions are equivalent:

(i)

\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
is birationally rigid.

(ii)

\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}]
admits a unique filtered quantization.

(iii)

\mathfrak{P}^{\widetilde{X}}=0
.

(iv)

H^{2}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}},\mathbb{C})=0
and
\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}])
has no codimension
2
leaves.

Proof.

(i) and (iii) are equivalent by Proposition
7.2.2
. (iii) and (iv) are equivalent by Proposition
4.5.1
and the isomorphism
\mathfrak{P}^{\widetilde{X}}_{0}\simeq H^{2}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}},\mathbb{C})
established in Lemma
7.2.7
. Finally, (ii) and (iii) are equivalent by Proposition
4.7.6
.
∎

Sometimes it will be convenient to consider a larger class of covers.

Definition 7.6.2
.

Let
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
be a nilpotent cover. We say that
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
is
2-leafless

if
\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}])
has no codimension 2 leaves.

7.6.1.
Linear classical groups

Using Corollary
7.6.1
and results of Namikawa, we can give a complete classification of birationally rigid orbits in classical types.

Proposition 7.6.3
.

Suppose
\mathfrak{g}
is classical and let
\mathbb{O}\subset\mathfrak{g}^{*}
be a nilpotent orbit corresponding to a partition
p
. Then
\mathbb{O}
is birationally rigid if and only if one of the following is true:

(i)

\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{sl}(n)
and
\mathbb{O}=\{0\}
.

(ii)

\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{so}(2n+1)
or
\mathfrak{sp}(2n)
and
p
satisfies

p_{i}\leq p_{i+1}+1,\qquad\forall i.

(iii)

\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{so}(2n)
,
p
satisfies

p_{i}\leq p_{i+1}+1,\qquad\forall i,

and
p
is not of the form
(2^{m},1^{2})
for some
m
.

Proof.

If
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{l}(n)
all induction is birational. So (i) follows from (i) of
2.3.3
. Suppose
\mathfrak{g}
is of type B, C or D. By Lemma
7.5.9

\mathbb{O}
is 2-leafless if and only if
\overline{\mathbb{O}}
has no codimension 2 orbits. So by
7.6.1

\mathbb{O}
is birationally rigid if and only if:

(1)

\overline{\mathbb{O}}
has no codimension 2 orbits, and

(2)

H^{2}(\mathbb{O},\mathbb{C})=0
.

Let
p
be the partition corresponding to
\mathbb{O}
. By the results
[
KP82
]
(see also
[
Nam09
, Prop 1.3.2]
)
\mathbb{O}
satisfies (1) if and only if

(7.6.1)

p_{i}-p_{i+1}\leq 1,\qquad\forall i.

On the other hand, by
[
BC12
, Thms 5.5,5.6]
,
H^{2}(\mathbb{O},\mathbb{C})\neq 0
if and only if
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{o}(2n)
and
p
has the following property:
p
contains an odd part with multiplicity 2, and no other odd parts. Under the condition (
7.6.1
) this can only happen if
p
is of the form
(2^{m},1^{2})
for some
m
.
∎

We now turn our attention to nilpotent covers. For
G=\mathrm{SL}(n)
, we can give a complete classification of birationally rigid covers.

Proposition 7.6.4
.

Suppose
G=\mathrm{SL}(n)
and let
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
be a
G
-equivariant nilpotent cover. Then
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
is birationally rigid if and only if it is the unversal cover of a nilpotent orbit
\mathbb{O}
corresponding to a partition
p=(d^{m})
for
m,d\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
satisfying
md=n
.

Proof.

Let
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
be the universal cover of the orbit corresponding to the partition
(d^{m})
. By
[
Nam20
, Prop 1.9]
,
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
is 2-leafless. Recall that
\mathfrak{r}
denotes the Lie algebra of the maximal reductive subgroup in the stabilizer of an element of
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
. By
[
CM93
, Thm 6.1.3]
,
\mathfrak{r}\simeq\mathfrak{sl}(\mathfrak{m})
. So
H^{2}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}},\mathbb{C})=0
by Lemma
7.2.7
. Thus,
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
is birationally rigid by Corollary
7.6.1
. Conversely, Namikawa shows in the proof of
[
Nam20
, Claim 1.10.1]
that every
G
-equivariant nilpotent cover not of this form is birationally induced.
∎

Remark 7.6.5
.

Let
L
be a standard Levi subgroup of
\mathrm{SL}(n)
, i.e.

L=\mathrm{S}(\mathrm{GL}(a_{1})\times...\times\mathrm{GL}(a_{t})),\qquad\sum a_{i}=n

Adapting the proof of Proposition
7.6.4
, one can classify all birationally rigid
L
-equivariant nilpotent covers. The result is as follows: an
L
-equivariant nilpotent cover
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
is birationally rigid if and only if it is the universal
L
-equivariant cover of a nilpotent orbit

\mathbb{O}=\mathbb{O}^{1}\times...\times\mathbb{O}^{t}\subset\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{GL}(a_{1})}\times...\times\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{GL}(a_{t})}=\mathcal{N}_{L}

such that each
\mathbb{O}^{i}
corresponds to a partition
(d^{m_{i}})
of
a_{i}
for a fixed integer
d
.

Next we consider the groups
\operatorname{SO}(n)
and
\operatorname{Sp}(2n)
. By Corollary
7.6.1
, every birationally rigid cover is 2-leafless. The following is an immediate consequence of Lemma
4.6.1
.

Lemma 7.6.6
.

Let
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
and
\widecheck{\mathbb{O}}
be
G
-equivariant nilpotent covers such that
\widecheck{\mathbb{O}}
covers
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
. If
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
is 2-leafless, then
\widecheck{\mathbb{O}}
is 2-leafless.

The next proposition provides a classification of nilpotent orbits admitting 2-leafless covers for
G=\mathrm{SO}(n)
or
\mathrm{Sp}(2n)
. A result like Proposition
7.6.4
for these groups is possible, but inconvenient to state and not necessary for our purposes.

Proposition 7.6.7
.

Suppose
G=\mathrm{SO}(n)
or
\mathrm{Sp}(2n)
, and let
\mathbb{O}
be a nilpotent
G
-orbit. Write
p
for the partition corresponding to
\mathbb{O}
. Then
\mathbb{O}
admits a 2-leafless
G
-equivariant cover if and only if one of the following is true:

(i)

G=\mathrm{SO}(n)
,
p
satisfies

p_{i}\leq p_{i+1}+2,\qquad\forall i,

and the inequality is strict whenever
p_{i}
is even.

(ii)

G=\mathrm{Sp}(2n)
,
p
satisfies

p_{i}\leq p_{i+1}+2,\qquad\forall i,

and the inequality is strict whenever
p_{i}
is odd.

Proof.

First, suppose
G=\operatorname{SO}(n)
, and let
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}
be the universal
G
-equivariant cover of a nilpotent orbit of the type described in (i). By
[
Nam20
, Prop 3.4]
,
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}
is 2-leafless. Conversely, suppose
\mathbb{O}
admits a 2-leafless
G
-equivariant cover. Then the universal
G
-equivariant cover
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}
of
\mathbb{O}
is 2-leafless. Suppose there is an index
i\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
such that
p_{i}\geq p_{i+1}+2
. Then by
[
KP82
, Section 3]
, there is a codimension 2 orbit
\mathbb{O}^{\prime}\subset\overline{\mathbb{O}}
such that the corresponding partition
q
satisfies

p_{i}>q_{i}\quad\text{and}\quad p_{i+1}<q_{i+1}.

Write
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}^{\prime}\subset\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\widehat{\mathbb{O}}])
for the preimage of
\mathbb{O}^{\prime}
under the
G
-equivariant map
\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\widehat{\mathbb{O}}])\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\overline{\mathbb{O}}
. Since
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}
is 2-leafless,
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}^{\prime}\subset\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\widehat{\mathbb{O}}])^{\mathrm{reg}}
, and hence by
[
Mat20
, Theorem 2.6]
,
p_{i}=q_{i}+1=q_{i+1}+1=p_{i+1}+2
, and
p_{j}=q_{j}
for
j\neq i,i+1
. In particular, the multiplicity of
p_{i}
in
p
is of different parity than its multiplicity in
q
, and thus
p_{i}
is odd. So
p
is of the form described in (i).

Next, suppose
G=\mathrm{Sp}(2n)
, and let
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}
be the universal cover of a nilpotent orbit of the type described in (ii). By
[
Nam20
, Prop 2.3]
,
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}
is 2-leafless. The converse is proved analogously to the
\mathrm{SO}(n)
-case.
∎

Now suppose
G
is linear classical, and choose a nilpotent orbit
\mathbb{O}
which admits a birationally rigid cover. In the proposition below, we will find a standard Levi subgroup
L\subset G
and a birationally rigid
L
-orbit
\mathbb{O}_{L}
such that
\mathbb{O}=\mathrm{Bind}^{G}_{L}\mathbb{O}_{L}
. We will also give a parameterization of the codimension 2 leaves
\mathfrak{L}_{k}\subset X
. For each
\mathfrak{L}_{k}
, we will describe the Kleinian singularity
\Sigma_{k}
, as well as the codimension 2 orbit
\mathbb{O}_{k}\subset\overline{\mathbb{O}}
, see Lemma
7.5.9
. The statements will require a bit of additional notation.

Definition 7.6.8
.

If
p
is a partition, define

S_{2}(p)=\{i:p_{i}=p_{i+1}+2\}.

Regard
S_{2}(p)
as a partition (with no repeated parts).

•

If
i\in S_{2}(p)
, let
c_{i}(p)
be the partition formed by deleting one box from
p_{i}
and adding one box to
p_{i+1}
(‘c’ stands for ‘collapse’).

•

If
x=(x_{1},...,x_{r})
is a subpartition of
S_{2}(p)
, let
p\#x
be the partition formed by deleting the columns in
p
numbered
p_{x_{1}},p_{x_{1}}-1
,
p_{x_{2}}
,
p_{x_{2}}-1
, …,
p_{x_{r}}
,
p_{x_{r}}-1
.

For example, if
p=(6^{2},4,3,2)
, then
S_{2}(p)=(5,2)
,
c_{5}(p)=(6^{2},4,3,1,1)
, and
p\#S_{2}(p)=(2^{3},1)
.

Proposition 7.6.9
.

Suppose
G
is linear classical, and let
\mathbb{O}
be a nilpotent orbit which admits a birationally rigid
G
-equivariant cover. Write
p
for the partition corresponding to
\mathbb{O}
. Then
p
is of the form described in Propositions
7.6.4
(if
G=\mathrm{SL}(n)
) or Proposition
7.6.7
(otherwise).

(i)

Suppose
G=\mathrm{SL}(n)
. Then

L=\mathrm{S}(\mathrm{GL}(m)^{d}),\qquad\mathbb{O}_{L}=\{0\}.

There is a single codimension 2 leaf
\mathfrak{L}_{1}\subset X
, and

\Sigma_{1}\simeq A_{d-1},\qquad\mathbb{O}_{1}=\mathbb{O}_{(d^{m-1},d-1,1)}.

(ii)

Suppose
G=\mathrm{SO}(2n+1),\mathrm{Sp}(2n)
, or
\mathrm{SO}(2n)
. Assume
\mathbb{O}
is
not
of the form
\mathbb{O}_{(4^{2m},3,1)}
for
\mathrm{SO}(8m+4)
. Then

L=\prod_{k\in S_{2}(p)}\mathrm{GL}(k)\times G(n-|S_{2}(p)|),\qquad\mathbb{O}_{L}=\{0\}\times...\times\{0\}\times\mathbb{O}_{p\#S_{2}(p)}.

The codimension 2 leaves in
X
are parameterized by the parts of the partition
S_{2}(p)
. For
k\in S_{2}(p)

\Sigma_{k}\simeq A_{1},\qquad\mathbb{O}_{k}=\mathbb{O}_{c_{k}(p)}.

(iii)

Suppose
G=\mathrm{SO}(8m+4)
and
\mathbb{O}=\mathbb{O}_{(4^{2m},3,1)}
. Then

L=\mathrm{GL}(2m+1)\times\mathrm{GL}(2m+1),\qquad\mathbb{O}_{L}=\{0\}\times\{0\}.

There are two codimension 2 leaves
\mathfrak{L}_{1},\mathfrak{L}_{2}\subset X
, and

\Sigma_{1}\simeq\Sigma_{2}\simeq A_{1},\qquad\mathbb{O}_{1}=\mathbb{O}_{(4^{2m},2^{2})}^{\mathrm{I}},\qquad\mathbb{O}_{2}=\mathbb{O}_{(4^{2m},2^{2})}^{\mathrm{II}}.

Proof.

For the computation of
L
and
\mathbb{O}_{L}
, we refer the reader to
[
Mat20
, Cor 4.13]
. The description of singularities and codimension 2 orbits is immediate from
[
KP82
, Table I]
.
∎

If
\mathbb{O}
is an orbit which admits a birationally rigid cover, then
H^{2}(\mathbb{O},\mathbb{C})=0
. Indeed,
H^{2}(\mathbb{O},\mathbb{C})
embeds into the second cohomology of any cover of
\mathbb{O}
and by Corollary
7.6.1
, the second cohomology of a birationally rigid cover is
0
. Thus we can define, for each codimension 2 leaf
\mathfrak{L}_{k}\subset X
, a Levi subgroup
M_{k}\subset G
containing
L
adapted to
\mathfrak{L}_{k}
and a nilpotent
M_{k}
-orbit
\mathbb{O}_{M_{k}}
with
\mathbb{O}=\mathrm{Bind}^{G}_{M_{k}}\mathbb{O}_{M_{k}}
, see (
7.5.1
). Recall, Remark
7.5.2
, that once
(L,\mathbb{O}_{L})
is fixed,
(M_{k},\mathbb{O}_{M_{k}})
is
uniquely
determined by the codimension 2 leaf
\mathfrak{L}_{k}
. In the next proposition, we will compute
(M_{k},\mathbb{O}_{M_{k}})
for each
\mathfrak{L}_{k}
. For these computations, we will always take
L
to be the (standard) Levi subgroup described in Proposition
7.6.9
. For this choice of
L
, typically
M_{k}
is
not
standard. We will compute the standard Levi subgroup to which it is conjugate under
G
(we will use the symbol ‘
\simeq^{G}
’ to indicate
G
-conjugacy), as well as the partition corresponding to the nilpotent orbit
\mathbb{O}_{M_{k}}
. We will also compute a complete set of generators
\tau_{i}(k)
for the free abelian group
\mathfrak{X}(M_{k})
, written in standard coordinates on
\mathfrak{h}^{*}
. Of course, the elements
\tau_{i}(k)
determine
M_{k}
uniquely (i.e. not just up to conjugacy).

Proposition 7.6.10
.

Suppose
G
is linear classical, and let
\mathbb{O}
be a nilpotent orbit which admits a birationally rigid
G
-equivariant cover. Write
p
for the partition corresponding to
\mathbb{O}
. Then
p
is as described in Propositions
7.6.4
(if
G=\mathrm{SL}(n)
) or Proposition
7.6.7
(otherwise), and
L
is as described in Proposition
7.6.9
.

(a)

Suppose
G=\mathrm{SL}(n)
. Then

M_{1}=L=\mathrm{S}(\mathrm{GL}(m)^{d}),\qquad\mathbb{O}_{M_{1}}=\mathbb{O}_{L}=\{0\},

and

\tau_{i}(1)=\frac{1}{d}(\underbrace{d-i,d-i,...,d-i}_{mi},\underbrace{-i,-i,...,-i}_{m(d-i)})\in\mathfrak{h}^{*},\qquad 1\leq i\leq d-1.

(b)

Suppose
G=\mathrm{SO}(2n+1),\mathrm{Sp}(2n)
, or
\mathrm{SO}(2n)
. Assume
\mathbb{O}
is
not
of the form
\mathbb{O}_{(4^{2m},3,1)}
for
\mathrm{SO}(8m+4)
. For
k\in S_{2}(p)
,

M_{k}\simeq^{G}\mathrm{GL}(k)\times G(n-k),\qquad\mathbb{O}_{M_{k}}=\{0\}\times\mathbb{O}_{p\#(k)},

and

\tau_{1}(k)=(\underbrace{0,...,0}_{l(k)},\underbrace{1,...,1}_{k},\underbrace{0,...,0}_{n-l(k)-k})\in\mathfrak{h}^{*},\qquad l(k):=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in S_{2}(p)\\
j<k\end{subarray}}j.

(c)

Suppose
G=\mathrm{SO}(8m+4)
and
\mathbb{O}=\mathbb{O}_{(4^{2m},3,1)}
. Then

M_{1}\simeq^{G}\mathrm{GL}(4m+2)^{\mathrm{I}},\qquad\mathbb{O}_{M_{1}}=\mathbb{O}_{(2^{2m+1})},\qquad\tau_{1}(1)=\frac{1}{2}(\underbrace{1,...,1}_{2m+1},\underbrace{-1,...,-1}_{2m+1})\in\mathfrak{h}^{*},

and

M_{2}\simeq^{G}\mathrm{GL}(4m+2)^{\mathrm{II}},\qquad\mathbb{O}_{M_{2}}=\mathbb{O}_{(2^{2m+1})},\qquad\tau_{1}(2)=\frac{1}{2}(1,...,1)\in\mathfrak{h}^{*}.

Proof.

As explained in Remark
7.5.2
, the weights
\tau_{i}(k)
determine a Levi subgroup
M\subset G
(
M
is the Levi subgroup corresponding to the coroots which vanish on each
\tau_{i}(k)
). In each case, it is trivial to check that
M
contains
L
and is conjugate to the indicated (standard) Levi subgroup.
We will show that
M=M_{k}
using Lemma
7.5.10
. Condition (i) of that lemma is trivial in all cases. Condition (ii) can be easily verified using
[
KP82
, Table I]
. This leaves conditions (iii) and (iv). In case (a),
\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{O}])
contains a unique codimension 2 leaf. So conditions (iii) and (iv) are vacuous. For case (b), we argue as follows. By Proposition
7.6.9
,
\mathbb{O}_{j}=\mathbb{O}_{c_{j}(p)}
. Let
\mathbb{O}_{M_{k},j}=\{0\}\times\mathbb{O}_{c_{j}(p\#(k))}
. The expression
c_{j}(p\#(k))
makes sense since
j\in S_{2}(p)-\{k\}=S_{2}(p\#(k))
. By
[
KP82
, Table I]
,
\mathbb{O}_{M_{k},j}
is a codimension 2 orbit in
\overline{\mathbb{O}}_{M_{k}}
, and the singularity of
\mathbb{O}_{M_{k},j}\subset\overline{\mathbb{O}}_{M_{k}}
is of type
A_{1}
, and hence equivalent to that of
\mathbb{O}_{j}\subset\overline{\mathbb{O}}
. This proves condition (iv) of Lemma
7.5.10
. Condition (iii) becomes

\mathbb{O}_{c_{j}(p)}=\mathrm{Ind}^{G}_{\mathrm{GL}(k)\times G(n-k)}\{0\}\times\mathbb{O}_{c_{j}(p\#(k))}

By Proposition
2.3.2
, the right hand side corresponds to the partition

c_{j}(p\#(k))+2(\underbrace{1,...,1}_{k})=c_{j}[p\#(k)+2(\underbrace{1,...,1}_{k})]=c_{j}(p),

which proves condition (iii).
We proceed to case (c). Suppose
k=1
,
j=2
(the other case, namely
k=2
,
j=1
, is analogous). Note that the Levi subgroup
M_{1}
determined by
\tau_{1}(1)
is
G
-conjugate to the standard Levi
\mathrm{GL}(4m+2)^{\mathrm{II}}\subset\mathrm{SO}(8m+4)
. By Proposition
7.6.9
,
\mathbb{O}_{2}=\mathbb{O}_{(4^{2m},2^{2})}^{\mathrm{II}}
. Set
\mathbb{O}_{M_{1},2}=\mathbb{O}_{(2^{2m},1^{2})}
. By
[
KP82
, Table I]
,
\mathbb{O}_{M_{1},2}
is a codimension 2 orbit in
\overline{\mathbb{O}}_{M_{1}}
, and the singularity of
\mathbb{O}_{M_{1},2}\subset\overline{\mathbb{O}}_{M_{1}}
is of type
A_{1}
, and hence equivalent to that of
\mathbb{O}_{2}\subset\overline{\mathbb{O}}
. It remains to show that

(7.6.2)

\mathbb{O}_{(4^{2m},2^{2})}^{\mathrm{II}}=\mathrm{Ind}^{G}_{GL(4m+2)^{\mathrm{II}}}\mathbb{O}_{(2^{2m},1^{2})}.

This follows at once from (iii) of
[
CM93
, Thm 7.3.3]
.
∎

7.6.2.
Spin groups

Next, we describe all nilpotent orbits for
\mathrm{SO}(n)
which admit
\mathrm{Spin}(n)
-equivariant 2-leafless covers. Of course, all orbits described in Proposition
7.6.7
(i) have this property. But there are others.

Proposition 7.6.11
.

Let
G=\mathrm{SO}(n)
and let
\mathbb{O}
be a nilpotent
G
-orbit corresponding to a partition
p
of
n
. Then
\mathbb{O}
admits a 2-leafless cover, which is
not

G
-equivariant, if and only if the following conditions are satisfied:

(i)

p
is rather odd (i.e. every odd part occurs with multiplicity 1).

(ii)

p_{i}\leq p_{i+1}+1
if
p_{i}
is even, and
p_{i}\leq p_{i+1}+4
if
p_{i}
is odd.

(iii)

p_{i}\neq p_{i+1}+3
for all
i
.

Proof.

First, suppose
p
satisfies conditions (i)-(iii). Condition (i) implies that
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O})\neq\pi_{1}^{G}(\mathbb{O})
, see Section
2.2
. Hence, the universal cover
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}
of
\mathbb{O}
cannot be
G
-equivariant. And by
[
Nam20
, Prop 3.6]
,
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}
is 2-leafless.

Conversely, suppose
\mathbb{O}
admits a 2-leafless cover which is not
G
-equivariant. Then
p
is rather odd by Section
2.2
. Suppose, for contradiction, that either (ii) or (iii) is false. Then it follows from the discussion after the proof of
[
Nam20
, Lem 3.9]
that the universal cover
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}
is birationally induced from a proper Levi subgroup. Note that Lemma
4.6.1
implies that
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}
is 2-leafless. Thus by Corollary
7.6.1
,
H^{2}(\widehat{\mathbb{O}},\mathbb{C})
is nonzero. By Lemma
7.2.7
,
H^{2}(\widehat{\mathbb{O}},\mathbb{C})
is identified with
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{r})
, where
\mathfrak{r}
is the reductive part of the centralizer of
e\in\mathbb{O}
. For orbits in classical types,
\mathfrak{r}
is described in
[
CM93
, Thm 6.1.3]
. For
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{so}(n)
,
\mathfrak{r}
is semisimple unless
p
contains an odd part of multiplicity 2. This is impossible in our case, since
p
is rather odd. So
\mathfrak{r}
is semisimple and
H^{2}(\widehat{\mathbb{O}},\mathbb{C})=0
, a contradiction.
∎

If
p
is a partition, define

S_{4}(p)=\{i:p_{i}=p_{i+1}+4\}.

We will regard
S_{4}(p)
as a partition (with no repeated parts). If
x\subset S_{4}(p)
is a subpartition, define
p\#x
as in Definition
7.6.8
. For example, if
p=(6,2,1^{2})
, then
S_{4}(p)=(1)
, and
p\#S_{4}(p)
is obtained from
p
by deleting columns numbered
5
and
6
, i.e.
p\#S_{4}(p)=(4,2,1^{2})
.

Proposition 7.6.12
.

Let
G=\mathrm{SO}(n)
and suppose
\mathbb{O}
is a nilpotent
G
-orbit which admits a birationally rigid
\mathrm{Spin}(n)
-equivariant cover which is
not

G
-equivariant. Let
p
be the partition corresponding to
\mathbb{O}
. Then
p
is of the form described in Proposition
7.6.11
. Let
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
denote the universal
G
-equivariant cover of
\mathbb{O}
, and let

L=\prod_{k\in S_{4}(p)}\mathrm{GL}(k)\times\mathrm{SO}(2n-2|S_{4}(p)|),\qquad\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{L}=\{0\}\times...\{0\}\times\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{p\#S_{4}(p)},

where
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{p\#S_{4}(p)}
denotes the universal
\mathrm{SO}(2n-2|S_{4}(p)|)
-equivariant cover of
\mathbb{O}_{p\#S_{4}(p)}
. Then
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{L}
is birationally rigid and
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}=\mathrm{Bind}^{G}_{L}\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{L}
.

The codimension 2 leaves in
\widetilde{X}
are parameterized by
S_{4}(p)
, and all corresponding singularities are of type
A_{1}
. If
k\in S_{4}(p)
, then

M_{k}\simeq^{G}\mathrm{GL}(k)\times\mathrm{SO}(2n-2k),\qquad\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{M_{k}}=\{0\}\times\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{p\#(k)},

and

\tau_{1}(k)=(\underbrace{0,...,0}_{l(k)},\underbrace{1,...,1}_{k},\underbrace{0,...,0}_{n-l(k)-k})\in\mathfrak{h}^{*},\qquad l(k):=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in S_{4}(p)\\
j<k\end{subarray}}j.

Proof.

The claim that the singularities of
\widetilde{X}
are all of type
A_{1}
and parameterized by
S_{4}(p)
is a special case of
[
Mat20
, Theorem 2.6]
. The description of
L
and
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{L}
is a special case of
[
Mat20
, Theorem 4.17]
. For the description of
M_{k}
we apply Lemma
7.5.11
and Remark
7.5.11
. Condition (i) of Remark
7.5.11
is trivial. Note that
p\#(k)
satisfies

•

q_{i}=p_{i}
if
i>k
.

•

q_{i}=p_{i}-2
if
i\leq k
.

Conditions (ii) and (iv) now follow from
[
Mat20
, Thm 2.6]
. Condition (iii) can be verified by inspecting the partitions corresponding to the boundary orbits in
\overline{\mathbb{O}}
and
\overline{\mathbb{O}}_{M}
, see the proof of Proposition
7.6.10
.
∎

7.6.3.
Exceptional groups

Let
\mathbb{O}
be a nilpotent orbit and let
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathbb{O}
denote the universal
G
-equivariant cover. As usual, let
X=\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{O}])
and
\widehat{X}=\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\widehat{\mathbb{O}}])
. Below, we will develop a method for determining whether
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}
is birationally rigid. Although in the cases where we apply it,
G
will be a simple exceptional group, the argument below is general and works for arbitrary
G
.

For the next lemma, choose a codimension 2 leaf
\mathfrak{L}_{k}\subset X
. Let
\mathbb{O}_{k}\subset\overline{\mathbb{O}}
denote the corresponding codimension 2 orbit, see Lemma
7.5.9
.

Lemma 7.6.13
.

Suppose that the covering map
\widehat{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}X
is étale over the locus
\mathbb{O}\cup\mathfrak{L}_{k}
. Then

(7.6.3)

\dim\mathfrak{P}^{\widehat{X}}\geq|\pi_{1}^{G}(\mathbb{O})||\pi_{1}^{G}(\mathbb{O}_{k})|^{-1}.

Proof.

Let
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{k}\subset\widehat{X}
denote the preimage of
\mathbb{O}_{k}
under the finite map
\widehat{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\overline{\mathbb{O}}
. Since
G
acts on
\mathfrak{L}_{k}
with finitely many orbits, there is an open
G
-orbit
\mathfrak{L}_{k}^{\prime}\subset\mathfrak{L}_{k}
, which is a finite connected
G
-equivariant cover of
\mathbb{O}_{k}
. Choose
x\in\mathfrak{L}_{k}^{\prime}
. Since the map
\widehat{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}X
is étale over
x
, the preimage of
x
contains
|\pi_{1}^{G}(\mathbb{O})|
elements. Each connected component of
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{k}
is a
G
-equivariant cover of
\mathbb{O}_{k}
and therefore contains at most
|\pi_{1}^{G}(\mathbb{O}_{k})|
elements in the preimage of
x
. Thus,
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{k}
contains at least
|\pi_{1}^{G}(\mathbb{O})||\pi_{1}^{G}(\mathbb{O}_{k})|^{-1}
connected components. Each such component is a codimension 2 leaf in
\widehat{X}
. Now (
7.6.3
) follows from Proposition
4.5.1
.
∎

For the next lemma, define
