\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{rig}}(\mathbb{O}):=\{(L,\mathbb{O}_{L})=(\text{Levi subgroup},\text{rigid orbit})\mid\mathbb{O}=\Ind^{G}_{L}\mathbb{O}_{L}\}/G,

and let
m(\mathbb{O})
be the integer

m(\mathbb{O}):=\mathrm{max}\{\dim(\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}))\mid(L,\mathbb{O}_{L})\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{rig}}(\mathbb{O})\}.

Lemma 7.6.14
.

Let
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathbb{O}
be a
G
-equivariant cover and let
\widetilde{X}=\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}])
. Then

(7.6.4)

\dim\mathfrak{P}^{\widetilde{X}}\leq m(\mathbb{O}).

Proof.

We can assume in the proof that
G
is semisimple. Choose a Levi subgroup
M\subset G
and a birationally rigid
M
-equivariant nilpotent cover
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{M}
such that
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}=\mathrm{Bind}^{G}_{M}\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{M}
. Since
\mathbb{O}=\Ind^{G}_{M}\mathbb{O}_{M}
, there is a pair
(L,\mathbb{O}_{L})\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{rig}}(\mathbb{O})
such that
L\subset M
. In particular,
\dim(\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{m}))\leq m(\mathbb{O})
. By
7.2.2
,
\dim\mathfrak{P}^{\widetilde{X}}=\dim(\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{m}))
. The lemma follows.
∎

In many cases, Lemmas
7.6.13
and
7.6.14
can be applied in conjuction to prove that
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}
is birationally rigid.

Proposition 7.6.15
.

Suppose

(i)

The reductive part
\mathfrak{r}
of the centralizer of
e\in\mathbb{O}
is semisimple.

(ii)

The finite subgroups of
\mathrm{Sp}(2)
corresponding to the Kleinian singularities in
X
are cyclic of prime order.

(iii)

For each codimension 2 orbit
\mathbb{O}^{\prime}\subset\overline{\mathbb{O}}
, there is a strict inequality

|\pi_{1}^{G}(\mathbb{O})||\pi_{1}^{G}(\mathbb{O}^{\prime})|^{-1}>m(\mathbb{O}).

Then
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}
is birationally rigid.

Proof.

Suppose for contradiction that
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}
is birationally induced.
Property (i), combined with Lemma
7.2.7
(ii), implies that
H^{2}(\widehat{\mathbb{O}},\mathbb{C})=0
. Thus, by Corollary
7.6.1
, there is a codimension 2 leaf
\widehat{\mathfrak{L}}\subset\widehat{X}
. Let
\mathfrak{L}\subset X
denote the image of
\widehat{\mathfrak{L}}
under the covering map
\widehat{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}X
and consider the codimension 2 orbit
\mathbb{O}^{\prime}\subset\overline{\mathbb{O}}
corresponding to
\mathfrak{L}
. Write
\Gamma^{\prime},\widehat{\Gamma}^{\prime}\subset\mathrm{Sp}(2)
for the (nontrivial) finite subgroups corresponding to
\mathfrak{L}
and
\widehat{\mathfrak{L}}
, respectively. By property (ii),
\widehat{\Gamma}^{\prime}=\Gamma^{\prime}
. It follows that the covering map
\widehat{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}X
is étale over
\mathbb{O}\cup\mathfrak{L}
, see Proposition
5.4.1
. From
Lemma

7.6.13
we deduce that
\dim\mathfrak{P}^{\widehat{X}}\geq|\pi_{1}^{G}(\mathbb{O})||\pi_{1}^{G}(\mathbb{O}^{\prime})|^{-1}
. On the other hand, Lemma
7.6.14
, combined with (iii), implies that
\dim\mathfrak{P}^{\widehat{X}}<|\pi_{1}^{G}(\mathbb{O})||\pi_{1}^{G}(\mathbb{O}^{\prime})|^{-1}
. This is a contradiction.
∎

In exceptional types, the Lie algebras
\mathfrak{r}
can be found in
[
Car93
, Sec 13.1]
. The boundary orbits
\mathbb{O}^{\prime}\subset\overline{\mathbb{O}}
and the corresponding singularities can be found in
[
FJLS15
, Tables]
. For
G
simply connected, the fundamental groups
\pi_{1}^{G}(\mathbb{O})\simeq\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O})
are listed in
[
CM93
, Sec 8.4]
. Finally,
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{rig}}(\mathbb{O})
, and hence the integer
m(\mathbb{O})
, is deducible from
[
DE09
, Sec 4]
.

The next result describes the Kleinian singularities which can appear in
\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{O}])
for nilpotent orbits
\mathbb{O}
admitting 2-leafless covers.

Proposition 7.6.16
.

Suppose
G
is a simple exceptional group. Assume
\mathbb{O}
admits a 2-leafless
G
-equivariant cover. Then all codimension 2 singularities in
X
are of type
A_{1}
, except in the following cases:

(i)

G=E_{6}
(simply connected form) and
\mathbb{O}=2A_{2}
. There is a unique codimension 2 leaf, and the corresponding singularity is of type
A_{2}
.

(ii)

G=E_{6}
(simply connected form) and
\mathbb{O}=A_{5}
. There is a unique codimension 2 leaf, and the corresponding singularity is of type
A_{2}
.

(iii)

G=E_{6}
(simply connected form) and
\mathbb{O}=E_{6}(a_{3})
. There are two codimension 2 leaves, and the corresponding singularities are of types
A_{1}
and
A_{2}
.

(iv)

G=E_{8}
and
\mathbb{O}=E_{8}(b_{6})
. There are two codimension 2 leaves, and the corresponding singularities are of types
A_{1}
and
A_{2}
.

In each case listed above, the universal
G
-equivariant cover
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathbb{O}
is birationally rigid.

Proof.

First, we will show that the cases listed above are the only possible exceptions. Fix a codimension 2 leaf
\mathfrak{L}_{k}\subset X=\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{O}])
. We claim first of all that the natural map
\phi_{k}:\Gamma_{k}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O})
defined in (
4.6.1
) is an injection.

Recall that
\Sigma_{k}
embeds into
X
(see the discussion at the beginning of Section
7.5
).
Choose a small neighborhood
N
of
0
in
\Sigma_{k}\subset X
and a point
x\in N-\{0\}
. Let
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathbb{O}
be the universal cover and let
p:\widehat{X}=\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\widehat{\mathbb{O}}])\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}X
be the induced map of affine varieties. Fix a preimage
\widehat{x}\in p^{-1}(x)
, and let
\widehat{N}
denote the connected component of
p^{-1}(N)
containing
\hat{x}
. Since
\widehat{X}
is 2-leafless, we can assume that
\widehat{N}
is a disc and the map
p:\widehat{N}\rightarrow N
is the quotient map for the
\Gamma_{k}
-action on
\widehat{N}
.
For each
\widehat{x}_{i}\in p^{-1}(x)\cap\widehat{N}
, choose a continuous path
\gamma_{i}
inside
\widehat{N}
connecting
\widehat{x}
to
\widehat{x}_{i}
. Note that there are
|\Gamma_{k}|
distinct points
\widehat{x}_{i}
. By construction, the loops
p(\gamma_{i})
exhaust the subgroup
\phi_{k}(\Gamma_{k})
. Since there are
|\Gamma_{k}|
distinct elements, the homomorphism
\phi_{k}
is injective, as asserted.

For simple exceptional
G
, the fundamental groups
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O})
are listed in
[
CM93
, Sec 8.4]
. The dimension 2 singularities
\Gamma_{k}
are described in
[
FJLS15
, Sec 13]
. The four orbits listed in the statement of the proposition are the only orbits with the following two properties:

•

X
contains a dimension 2 singularity
\Sigma_{k}
not of type
A_{1}
, and

•

\Gamma_{k}
admits an embedding into
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O})
.

To complete the proof, we will show that for each of these four orbits, the universal
G
-equivariant cover
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}
is birationally rigid. For orbits (i), (ii), and (iv) we will do so using a straightforward application of Proposition
7.6.15
. Orbit (iii) will require a slightly more elaborate argument, given below.

(i)\ \underline{G=E_{6},\mathbb{O}=2A_{2}.}
We have
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O})\simeq\mathbb{Z}_{3}
and
\mathfrak{r}=G_{2}
. By
[
DE09
, Sec 4]
,
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{rig}}(\mathbb{O})=\{(D_{4},\{0\})\}
, and hence
m(\mathbb{O})=2
. There is one codimension 2 orbit in
\overline{\mathbb{O}}
, indicated below.

k

\mathbb{O}_{k}

\Sigma_{k}

\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O}_{k})

1

A_{2}+A_{1}

A_{2}

1

Note that

|\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O})||\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O}_{1})|^{-1}=3>2=m(\mathbb{O}).

Thus,
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}
is birationally rigid by
7.6.15
.

(ii)\ \underline{G=E_{6},\mathbb{O}=A_{5}.}
We have
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O})\simeq\mathbb{Z}_{3}
and
\mathfrak{r}=A_{1}
. By
[
DE09
, Sec 4]
,
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{rig}}(\mathbb{O})=\{(D_{4},(3,2^{2},1)\}
, and hence
m(\mathbb{O})=2
. There is one codimension 2 orbit in
\overline{\mathbb{O}}
, indicated below.

k

\mathbb{O}_{k}

\Sigma_{k}

\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O}_{k})

1

A_{4}+A_{1}

A_{2}

1

Note that

|\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O})||\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O}_{1})|^{-1}=3>2=m(\mathbb{O}).

Thus,
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}
is birationally rigid by
7.6.15
.

(iii)\ \underline{G=E_{6},\mathbb{O}=E_{6}(a_{3}).}
We have
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O})\simeq S_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{3}
and
\mathfrak{r}=\{0\}
. By
[
DE09
, Sec 4]
,
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{rig}}(\mathbb{O})=\{(3A_{1},\{0\}),(A_{2},\{0\})\}
, and hence
m(\mathbb{O})=4
. There are two codimension 2 orbits in
\overline{\mathbb{O}}
, indicated below.

k

\mathbb{O}_{k}

\Sigma_{k}

\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O}_{k})

1

D_{5}(a_{1})

A_{2}

2

A_{5}

A_{1}

\mathbb{Z}_{3}

By Lemma
7.2.7
(ii),
H^{2}(\widehat{\mathbb{O}},\mathbb{C})=0
. Thus, by Corollary
7.6.1
, it suffices to show that
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}
is 2-leafless, i.e. that
\Sigma_{1}
and
\Sigma_{2}
are smoothened under
\widehat{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}X
. Note that

|\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O})||\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O}_{1})|^{-1}=6>4=m(\mathbb{O}).

Thus,
\Sigma_{1}
is smoothened under
\widehat{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}X
by the argument given in the proof of
7.6.15
. For
\Sigma_{2}
, the analogous inequality fails so a separate argument is needed. Suppose for contradiction that
\Sigma_{2}
is not smoothened under
\widehat{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}X
. Consider the 2-fold cover
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathbb{O}
, and let
\widetilde{X}=\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}])
. Since
\Sigma_{2}
is not smoothened under
\widehat{X}\rightarrow X
, it is not smoothened under the intermediate cover
\widetilde{X}\rightarrow X
, see
Lemma

7.6.6
. Since
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O}_{2})=\mathbb{Z}_{3}
, the preimage
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{2}\subset\widetilde{X}
of
\mathbb{O}_{2}
has 2 connected components. Thus,
\mathfrak{P}^{\widetilde{X}}
contains two 1-dimensional partial Namikawa spaces.

Since
\Sigma_{1}\simeq\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{3}
does not admit a non-trivial
2
-fold cover, the map
\widetilde{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}X
is étale over the locus
\mathbb{O}\cup\mathfrak{L}_{1}
. Since
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O}_{1})=1
, the preimage
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{1}\subset\widetilde{X}
of
\mathbb{O}_{1}
has 2 irreducible components
\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}_{1}^{1}
and
\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}_{1}^{2}
, each corresponding to a singularity of type
A_{2}
. Since for
i=1,2
the real codimension of
\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}_{1}^{i}-\mathbb{O}_{1}
in
\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}_{1}^{i}
is
\geq 4
, we have
\pi_{1}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}_{1}^{i})=\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O}_{1})=1
. Thus,
\mathfrak{P}^{\widetilde{X}}
contains two 2-dimensional partial Namikawa spaces. This, combined with the previous paragraph, gives an inequality
\dim\mathfrak{P}^{\widetilde{X}}\geq 6
. On the other hand
\dim\mathfrak{P}^{\widetilde{X}}\leq m(\mathbb{O})=4
by
Lemma

7.6.14
. This is a contradiction, proving
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}
is birationally rigid.

(iv)\ \underline{G=E_{8},\mathbb{O}=E_{8}(b_{6})}.
We have
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O})\simeq S_{3}
and
\mathfrak{r}=\{0\}
. By
[
DE09
, Sec 4]

\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{rig}}(\mathbb{O})=\{(A_{1}+E_{6},\{0\}\times 2A_{2}+A_{1}),(A_{1}+A_{2}+A_{3},\{0\}),(A_{2}+D_{4},\{0\}\times(2^{2},1^{4}))\},

and hence
m(\mathbb{O})=2
. There are two codimension 2 orbits in
\overline{\mathbb{O}}
, indicated below.

k

\mathbb{O}_{k}

\Sigma_{k}

\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O}_{k})

1

E_{6}(a_{1})+A_{1}

A_{2}

S_{2}

2

A_{7}

A_{1}

1

Note that

|\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O})||\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O}_{1})|^{-1}=3>2=m(\mathbb{O}),

and

|\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O})||\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O}_{2})|^{-1}=6>2=m(\mathbb{O}).

Thus,
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}
is birationally rigid by
7.6.15
.

∎

7.7.
Computation of
\eta_{k}

Suppose
G
is semisimple and simply connected. Let
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
be a
G
-equivariant nilpotent cover. Fix the notation of Section
7.2
, i.e.
\widetilde{X}
,
L
,
P
,
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{L}
,
\widetilde{Y}
,
\rho:\widetilde{Y}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\widetilde{X}
, and so on. For the remainder of this subsection, we will also assume

(a1)

\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
admits a birationally rigid cover.

As explained in the introduction, such covers play an important role in the computation of unipotent infinitesimal characters.

Choose a codimension 2 leaf
\mathfrak{L}_{k}\subset X
and let
\Sigma_{k}=\mathbb{C}^{2}/\Gamma_{k}
be the corresponding Kleinian singularity. Assumption (a1) guarantees that
H^{2}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}},\mathbb{C})=0
, see Corollary
7.6.1
. Thus, we can define a Levi subgroup
M_{k}\subset G
which is adapted to
\mathfrak{L}_{k}
, see (
7.5.1
). Recall that
L\subset M_{k}
and
\eta:\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak{P}
restricts to an isomorphism

(7.7.1)

\eta_{k}:\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{m}_{k})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak{P}_{k}.

For simplicity, we will impose the following additional assumption:

(a2)

\pi_{1}(\mathfrak{L}_{k})
acts trivially on
H^{2}(\mathfrak{S}_{k},\mathbb{C})
.

This is not an unreasonable condition: (a1) implies (a2) in almost all cases (and in all cases, if
G
is classical). Assuming condition (a2),
\mathfrak{P}_{k}
can be identified with the vector space
\mathfrak{h}_{k}^{*}
, i.e. the dual Cartan subalgebra corresponding to the Kleinian singularity
\Sigma_{k}
. In particular,
\mathfrak{P}_{k}
admits a natural basis consisting of fundamental weights, denoted
\{\omega_{i}(k)\mid 1\leq i\leq n(k)\}
. On the other hand,
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{m}_{k})
admits a natural basis consisting of dominant generators for the free abelian group
\mathfrak{X}(M_{k})
, denoted
\{\tau_{i}(k)\mid 1\leq i\leq n(k)\}
. Our goal in this section is to describe
\eta_{k}
in terms of these bases. We will achieve this goal for a large class of covers, including:

•

All nilpotent orbits for linear classical groups which admit birationally rigid covers. See Corollary
7.7.3
for type
A
and Corollary
7.7.6
for types
B
,
C
, and
D
.

•

All universal
\mathrm{SO}(n)
-equivariant covers which admit birationally rigid
\mathrm{Spin}(n)
-equivariant covers. See Corollary
7.7.7
.

•

All nilpotent orbits for simple exceptional groups which admit birationally rigid covers, except for the 4 orbits listed in Proposition
7.6.7
. See Proposition
7.7.4
, as well as Example
7.7.9
.

In all cases above, condition (a2) is satisfied.

7.7.1.
Case 1

Proposition 7.7.1
.

Assume conditions (a1)-(a2) as well as

(b1)

\mathfrak{L}_{k}=\mathfrak{L}_{1}
is the unique codimension 2 leaf in
\widetilde{X}
.

(b2)

\Pic(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})\simeq\mathfrak{X}(\Gamma_{1})
.

(b3)

\Pic(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{M_{1}})=0
.

(b4)

Up to the action of
W^{\widetilde{X}}
, there is a unique parabolic subgroup
P\subset G
with Levi factor
L
.

Then
M_{1}=L
,
\eta_{1}=\eta
, and
\eta_{1}\{\tau_{i}(1)\mid 1\leq i\leq n(1)\}=\{\omega_{i}(1)\mid 1\leq i\leq n(1)\}

By condition (b1) of Proposition
7.7.1
combined with
\mathfrak{P}_{0}\simeq H^{2}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}},\mathbb{C})=0
, we have that
\mathfrak{P}=\mathfrak{P}_{1}
. Hence,
\eta_{1}=\eta
. By Remark
7.5.2
,
M_{1}
is the (unique) Levi subgroup corresponding to the subspace
\eta_{1}^{-1}(\mathfrak{P}_{1})=\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})
. Thus,
M_{1}=L
and
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{M_{1}}=\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{L}
. It remains to show that
\eta\{\tau_{i}(1)\}=\{\omega_{i}(1)\}
. We will do so using Lemma
7.5.7
. The proof will come after a lemma.

Let
E_{\widetilde{X}}
and
E_{\Sigma}
denote the exceptional divisors of the (partial) resolutions
\rho:\widetilde{Y}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\widetilde{X}
and
\mathfrak{S}_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Sigma_{1}
. Write
U_{1},...,U_{d}
for the irreducible components of
E_{\widetilde{X}}
and
V_{1},...,V_{d^{\prime}}
for the irreducible components of
E_{\Sigma}
. Since
\mathfrak{L}_{1}\subset\widetilde{X}
is the unique codimension 2 leaf,
E_{\widetilde{X}}
is the closure of a fiber bundle over
\mathfrak{L}_{1}
, with fiber equal to
E_{\Sigma}
. Since the monodromy action of
\pi_{1}(\mathfrak{L}_{1})
on
\{V_{i}\}
is trivial, we have that
d=d^{\prime}
and
V_{i}=U_{i}\cap E_{\Sigma}
(up to reordering).

Lemma 7.7.2
.

Assume the conditions of Proposition
7.7.1
. Then the restriction map
\Pic(\widetilde{Y})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Pic(\mathfrak{S}_{1})
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

In the proof we can assume that
G
is semisimple and simply connected.

Consider the map
\mathbb{Z}^{d}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Cl(\widetilde{Y})
which takes the
i
th generator of
\mathbb{Z}^{d}
to
[U_{i}]\in\Cl(\widetilde{Z})
. This map induces a short exact sequence

(7.7.2)

\mathbb{Z}^{d}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Cl(\widetilde{Y})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Cl(\widetilde{Y}-\bigcup_{i=1}^{d}U_{i})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}0.

Note that
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
is an open subset of
\widetilde{Y}-\bigcup_{i=1}^{d}U_{i}
with complement of codimension
\geq 2
. Thus,
\Cl(\widetilde{Y}-\bigcup_{i=1}^{d}U_{i})\xrightarrow{\sim}\Cl(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})
. Since
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
is smooth,
\Cl(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})\simeq\Pic(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})
. And by condition (b2) of Proposition
7.7.1
,
\Pic(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})\simeq\mathfrak{X}(\Gamma_{1})
. Hence, (
7.7.2
) becomes

(7.7.3)

\mathbb{Z}^{d}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Cl(\widetilde{Y})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathfrak{X}(\Gamma_{1})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}0.

Now consider the map
\mathbb{Z}^{d}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Cl(\mathfrak{S}_{1})
which takes the
i
th generator of
\mathbb{Z}^{d}
to
[V_{i}]
. This map induces a short exact sequence

(7.7.4)

\mathbb{Z}^{d}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Cl(\mathfrak{S}_{1})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Cl(\Sigma_{1}^{\times})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}0,

where
\Sigma_{1}^{\times}:=\Sigma_{1}-\{0\}
. Since
\Sigma_{1}^{\times}
is smooth and isomorphic to
(\mathbb{C}^{2}-0)/\Gamma_{1}
, there are group isomorphisms
\Cl(\Sigma_{1}^{\times})\simeq\Pic(\Sigma_{1}^{\times})\simeq\mathfrak{X}(\Gamma_{1})
. Hence, (
7.7.4
) becomes

(7.7.5)

\mathbb{Z}^{d}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Cl(\mathfrak{S}_{1})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathfrak{X}(\Gamma_{1})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}0.

Since
U_{i}
intersects
\mathfrak{S}_{1}
transversely,
[U_{i}]
maps to
[V_{i}]
under the restriction map
\Cl(\widetilde{Y})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Cl(\mathfrak{S}_{1})
. Thus, sequences (
7.7.3
) and (
7.7.5
) form a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}^{d}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cl(\widetilde{Y})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{X}(\Gamma_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}^{d}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cl(\mathfrak{S}_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{X}(\Gamma_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces|_{\mathfrak{S}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu}

where
\nu
denotes the induced map on quotients. By Proposition
7.1.2
, there is a short exact sequence

0\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Pic(\widetilde{Y})\overset{\mathrm{div}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}}\Cl(\widetilde{Y})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Pic(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{L})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}0

and
\Pic(\widetilde{Y})\simeq\mathfrak{X}(L)
. By condition (b3) of Proposition
7.7.1
,
\Pic(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{L})=0
. So
\mathfrak{X}(L)\simeq\Pic(\widetilde{Y})\xrightarrow{\sim}\Cl(\widetilde{Y})
. Hence, the complexification of the restriction map
\Cl(\widetilde{Y})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Cl(\mathfrak{S}_{1})
coincides with the isomorphism
\eta:\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak{P}
. Any homomorphism of free abelian groups which is an isomorphism after complexification is injective. Hence,
\nu
is injective by the commutativity of the diagram and so an isomorphism, since
\mathfrak{X}(\Gamma_{1})
is finite. By the commutativity of the diagram, this implies that the restriction map
\Cl(\widetilde{Y})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Cl(\mathfrak{S}_{1})
is an isomorphism.
∎

Proof of
7.7.1
.

By Lemma
7.5.7
,
\eta
restricts to a group homomorphism
\eta:\mathfrak{X}(L)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Lambda_{1}
, and this homomorphism corresponds to the restriction map
\Pic(\widetilde{Y})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Pic(\mathfrak{S}_{1})
under the natural identifications
\mathfrak{X}(L)\simeq\Pic(\widetilde{Y})
and
\Lambda_{1}\simeq\Pic(\mathfrak{S}_{1})
. By Lemma
7.7.2
, this restriction map is an isomorphism. Hence,
\eta_{1}
restricts to an isomorphism of free abelian groups
\eta:\mathfrak{X}(L)\xrightarrow{\sim}\Lambda_{1}
.

By
Theorem

4.9.1
(iv),
\mathfrak{P}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\geq 0}
is the union of the ample cones of all
\mathbb{Q}
-terminalizations of
\widetilde{X}
. By
Lemma

7.5.1
(iii) and condition (b4),
\widetilde{Y}
is the unique such
\mathbb{Q}
-terminalization. Thus,
\eta:\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})_{\mathbb{R}}^{\geq 0}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak{P}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\geq 0}
, and hence
\eta
restricts to an isomorphism of free commutative monoids
\eta:\mathfrak{X}(L)^{\geq 0}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Lambda_{1}^{\geq 0}
. The source is generated by
\{\tau_{i}(1)\}
, and the target by
\{\omega_{i}(1)\}
. So
\eta
maps
\{\tau_{i}(1)\}
to
\{\omega_{i}(1)\}
, as asserted.
∎

7.7.2.
Case 1 for
G=\operatorname{SL}(n)

Let
G=\mathrm{SL}(n)
and let
\mathbb{O}
be a nilpotent
G
-orbit admitting a birationally rigid cover. By Proposition
7.6.7
,
\mathbb{O}
corresponds to a partition of the form
(d^{m})
of
n
, and by Proposition
7.6.9

L=\mathrm{S}(\mathrm{GL}(m)^{d}),\qquad\mathbb{O}_{L}=\{0\}.

Recall the barycenter parameter
\epsilon\in\mathfrak{P}^{X}
constructed in Example
5.3.2
, and let
\delta:=\eta^{-1}(\epsilon)\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})
. The following corollary describes
\delta
in standard coordinates on
\mathfrak{h}^{*}
. We can describe
\eta
completely (up to a diagram automorphism), but Corollary
7.7.3
is sufficient (and a bit easier to state).

Corollary 7.7.3
.

In standard coordinates on
\mathfrak{h}^{*}

\delta=(\underbrace{\frac{d-1}{2d},\ldots,\frac{d-1}{2d}}_{m},\underbrace{\frac{d-3}{2d},\ldots,\frac{d-3}{2d}}_{m},\ldots,\underbrace{\frac{1-d}{2d},\ldots,\frac{1-d}{2d}}_{m})\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}).

Proof.

We first verify that conditions of Proposition
7.7.1
are satisfied. (a1) is one of our assumptions on
\mathbb{O}
. By Proposition
7.6.9
,
X
contains a single codimension 2 leaf
\mathfrak{L}_{1}\subset X
and
\Sigma_{1}\simeq\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{d}
(this proves (b1)). Since
\Pic(\mathbb{O})
is finite, it is identified with the abelianization
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O})_{\mathrm{ab}}
, which is
\mathbb{Z}_{d}
by the results of Section
2.2
(this proves (b2)). (b3) is trivial since
\mathbb{O}_{M_{1}}=\mathbb{O}_{L}=\{0\}
. Note that

\dim(\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}))=d-1=\dim(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{sl}(d)}^{*})=\dim H^{2}(\mathfrak{S}_{1},\mathbb{C}),

Thus, the monodromy action of
\pi_{1}(\mathfrak{L}_{1})
on
\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{sl}(d)}^{*}
is trivial (this proves (a2)). Finally, note that
W^{\widetilde{X}}=W_{1}^{\widetilde{X}}\simeq S_{d}\simeq N_{G}(L)/L
. Note that the parabolic subgroups with Levi factor
L
are in bijection with elements of
S_{d}
. Thus,
P
is the unique parabolic with Levi factor
L
up to the action of
W^{\widetilde{X}}
(this proves (b4)).

Now Proposition
7.7.1
implies

\eta\{\tau_{i}(1)\mid 1\leq i\leq d-1\}=\{\omega_{i}(1)\mid 1\leq i\leq d-1\}.

By Proposition
7.6.10
,

\tau_{i}(1)=\frac{1}{d}(\underbrace{d-i,d-i,...,d-i}_{mi},\underbrace{-i,-i,...,-i}_{m(d-i)})\in\mathfrak{h}^{*},\qquad 1\leq i\leq d-1.

Hence

\delta=\eta^{-1}(\epsilon)=\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i=1}^{d-1}\tau_{i}(1)=(\underbrace{\frac{d-1}{2d},\ldots,\frac{d-1}{2d}}_{m},\underbrace{\frac{d-3}{2d},\ldots,\frac{d-3}{2d}}_{m},\ldots,\underbrace{\frac{1-d}{2d},\ldots,\frac{1-d}{2d}}_{m})

as asserted.
∎

7.7.3.
Case 2

By (ii) of Lemma
7.2.7
, there is an isomorphism
\Pic(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{C}\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{2}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}},\mathbb{C})
. Recall that condition (a1) implies that
H^{2}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}},\mathbb{C})=0
and hence that
\Pic(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})
is finite.

Proposition 7.7.4
.

Assume conditions (a1)-(a2) as well as

(c1)

\Sigma_{k}
is of type
A_{1}
.

Then
\Pic(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{M_{k}})
is finite,

(7.7.6)

c_{k}:=2|\Pic(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{M_{k}})||\Pic(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})|^{-1}

is an integer, and
\eta_{k}(\tau_{1}(k))=c_{k}\omega_{1}(k)
.

As in the proof of Proposition
7.7.1
, we will compute
\eta_{k}
using Lemma
7.5.7
. To apply this result, we must first fix a resolution datum
(P^{\prime},M_{k},Q)
for
L
. It may not be possible to arrange so that
P^{\prime}=P
, but we can always choose
P^{\prime}
so that the resulting identification
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak{P}
coincides with
\eta
, see Remark
7.5.4
. Thus, we can assume for convenience that
P=P^{\prime}
. As usual, write
\rho_{k}:\widetilde{Z}_{k}=G\times^{Q}(\widetilde{X}_{M_{k}}\times\mathfrak{q}^{\perp})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\widetilde{X}
for the partial resolution and
\rho_{k}^{\prime}:\widetilde{Y}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\widetilde{Z}_{k}
for the
\mathbb{Q}
-terminalization.

Since
\Sigma_{k}
is of type
A_{1}
, the monodromy action of
\pi_{1}(\mathfrak{L}_{k})
on
\mathfrak{h}_{k}^{*}
is trivial. Hence, by Lemma
7.5.7
(ii),
\eta_{k}
restricts to a group homomorphism
\mathfrak{X}(M_{k})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Lambda_{k}
. By Proposition
7.5.6
, the closed embedding
\Sigma_{k}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{X}
lifts to a closed embedding
\mathfrak{S}_{k}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{Z}_{k}
and by Lemma
7.5.7
the homomorphism
\mathfrak{X}(M_{k})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Lambda_{k}
corresponds to the restriction map
\Pic(\widetilde{Z}_{k})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Pic(\mathfrak{S}_{k})
under the natural identifications
\mathfrak{X}(M_{k})\simeq\Pic(\widetilde{Z}_{k})
and
\sigma:\Lambda_{k}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Pic(\mathfrak{S}_{k})
. Since
\mathfrak{P}_{k}
is one-dimensional, the Levi subgroup
M_{k}\subset G
has semisimple corank 1, see Lemma
7.5.10
. Hence, by Proposition
7.1.2
,
\mathbb{Z}\simeq\Pic(\widetilde{Z}_{k})
via
1\mapsto\pi_{k}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{G/Q}(\tau_{1})
. Also,
\mathbb{Z}\simeq\Pic(\mathfrak{S}_{k})
via
1\mapsto\sigma(\omega_{1})
. Let
\varphi:\mathbb{Z}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathbb{Z}
be the group homomorphism induced by the restriction map
\Pic(\widetilde{Z}_{k})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Pic(\mathfrak{S}_{k})
. To prove the proposition, it suffices to show that
\Pic(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{M_{k}})
is finite and
\varphi(1)=c_{k}
.

Let
E_{\widetilde{X}}
be the exceptional divisor of the partial resolution
\rho_{k}:\widetilde{Z}_{k}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\widetilde{X}
.

Lemma 7.7.5
.

Assume the conditions of Proposition
7.7.4
. The following are true:

(i)

\mathrm{rank}(\Cl(\widetilde{Z}_{k}))=1
.

(ii)

\Pic(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{M_{k}})
is finite.

(iii)

The map
a:\mathbb{Z}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Cl(\widetilde{Z}_{k})
defined by
a(1)=\mathrm{div}(\pi_{k}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{G/Q}(\tau_{1}))
gives rise to a short exact sequence

0\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathbb{Z}\overset{a}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}}\Cl(\widetilde{Z}_{k})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Pic(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{M_{k}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}0.

(iv)

The map
b:\mathbb{Z}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Cl(\widetilde{Z}_{k})
defined by
b(1)=[E_{X}]
gives rise to a short exact sequence

0\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathbb{Z}\overset{b}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}}\Cl(\widetilde{Z}_{k})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Pic(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}0.

Proof.

(iii) is a special case of Proposition
7.1.2
. We proceed to proving (i). Consider the restriction map
\Cl(\widetilde{Z}_{k})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Cl(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})
. Since
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
is an open subset of
\widetilde{Z}_{k}
, this map is surjective. Let
\widetilde{Z}_{k}^{2}\subset\widetilde{Z}_{k}
be the preimage under
\rho_{k}
of
\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{reg}}\cup\bigcup\mathfrak{L}_{j}\subset\widetilde{X}
. By Lemma
4.4.4
,
\codim(\widetilde{Z}_{k}-\widetilde{Z}_{k}^{2},\widetilde{Z}_{k})\geq 2
. Hence, there is a natural isomorphism
\Cl(\widetilde{Z}_{k})\simeq\Cl(\widetilde{Z}_{k}^{2})
.
By Proposition
7.5.6
,
\widetilde{Z}_{k}^{2}\cap E_{\widetilde{X}}
is a fiber bundle over
\mathfrak{L}_{k}
with fiber equal to
E_{\Sigma}
, and hence an irreducible variety. Thus, the kernel of the restriction map
\Cl(\widetilde{Z}_{k})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Cl(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})
is generated by the class
[E_{\widetilde{X}}]
and the following sequence is exact

(7.7.7)

\mathbb{Z}\overset{b}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}}\Cl(\widetilde{Z}_{k})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Cl(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}0.

Since
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
is smooth,
\Cl(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})\simeq\Pic(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})
. Hence, (
7.7.7
) becomes

(7.7.8)

\mathbb{Z}\overset{b}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}}\Cl(\widetilde{Z}_{k})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Pic(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}0.

Since
\Pic(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})
is finite, the exactness of (
7.7.8
) implies
\mathrm{rank}(\Cl(\widetilde{Z}_{k}))\leq 1
. The exactness of (iii) implies the opposite inequality. Hence,
\mathrm{rank}(\Cl(\widetilde{Z}_{k}))=1
, proving (i). Now, (i) and (iii) imply
\mathrm{rank}(\Pic(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{M_{k}}))=\mathrm{rank}(\Cl(\widetilde{Z}_{k}))-\mathrm{rank}(\mathbb{Z})=0
. Hence,
\Pic(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{M_{k}})
is finite, proving (ii). Similarly, (i) and (
7.7.8
) imply
\mathrm{rank}(\ker(b))=\mathrm{rank}(\mathbb{Z})-\mathrm{rank}(\Cl(\widetilde{Z}_{k}))+\mathrm{rank}(\Pic(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}))=0
. Hence,
\ker(b)=0
, proving (iv).
∎

Proof of
7.7.4
.

By Lemma
7.7.5
,
\Pic(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{M_{k}})
is finite. To show that
\eta_{k}(\tau_{1}(k))=c_{k}\omega_{1}(k)
, we must show that
\varphi(1)=c_{k}
. We will do so by comparing the injections
a,b:\mathbb{Z}\hookrightarrow\Cl(\widetilde{Z}_{k})
constructed in Lemma
7.7.5
.

Since
\widetilde{Z}_{k}
is normal, there is a natural isomorphism
\Cl(\widetilde{Z}_{k})\simeq\Pic(\widetilde{Z}_{k}^{\mathrm{reg}})
. Let
\phi
denote the composition

(7.7.9)

\phi:\Cl(\widetilde{Z}_{k})\simeq\Pic(\widetilde{Z}_{k}^{\mathrm{reg}})\overset{|_{\mathfrak{S}_{k}}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}}\Pic(\mathfrak{S}_{k})\simeq\mathbb{Z}.

Since the composition

\Pic(\widetilde{Z}_{k})\overset{\mathrm{div}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}}\Cl(\widetilde{Z}_{k})\simeq\Pic(\widetilde{Z}_{k}^{\mathrm{reg}})

coincides with the restriction map
\Pic(\widetilde{Z}_{k})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Pic(\widetilde{Z}_{k}^{\mathrm{reg}})
, we have

(\phi\circ a)(1)=\phi(\mathrm{div}(\pi_{k}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{G/Q}(\tau_{1})))=(\pi_{k}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{G/Q}(\tau_{1}))|_{\mathfrak{S}_{k}}.

Hence, by definition,
\phi\circ a=\varphi
.

On the other hand,
(\phi\circ b)(1)
corresponds to the line bundle
\mathcal{O}(E_{\Sigma})\in\Pic(\mathfrak{S}_{k})
under the canonical identification
\mathbb{Z}\simeq\Pic(\mathfrak{S}_{k})
. Since
\Sigma_{k}\simeq\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
and hence
\mathfrak{S}_{k}\simeq T^{*}\mathbb{P}^{1}
, this integer must be 2.

To complete the proof, we appeal to the following general fact: let
H
be a finitely-generated abelian group of rank 1 and let
\phi:H\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathbb{Z}
be an arbitrary homomorphism. Suppose there are exact sequences

0\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathbb{Z}\overset{a}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}}H\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}A\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}0,\qquad 0\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathbb{Z}\overset{b}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}}H\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}B\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}0.

Then

(\phi\circ a)(1)=|A||B|^{-1}(\phi\circ b)(1).

The proof of this fact is easy and is left to the reader. Setting
H=\Cl(\widetilde{Z}_{k})
,
A=\Pic(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{M_{k}})
, and
B=\Pic(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})
, we deduce

\varphi(1)=(\phi\circ a)(1)=|\Pic(\mathbb{O}_{M_{k}})||\Pic(\mathbb{O})|^{-1}(\phi\circ b)(1)=2|\Pic(\mathbb{O}_{M_{k}})||\Pic(\mathbb{O})|^{-1}=c_{k}.

This completes the proof.
∎

7.7.4.
Case 2 for linear classical groups

Let
G=\mathrm{Sp}(2n)
,
\mathrm{SO}(2n)
or
\mathrm{SO}(2n+1)
and let
\mathbb{O}
be a nilpotent
G
-orbit admitting a birationally rigid
G
-equivariant cover. Such orbits are described in Proposition
7.6.7
. Recall the barycenter parameter
\epsilon\in\mathfrak{P}^{X}
constructed in Example
5.3.2
, and let
\delta:=\eta^{-1}(\epsilon)\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})
. The following corollary describes
\delta
in standard coordinates on
\mathfrak{h}^{*}
.

Corollary 7.7.6
.

The following are true

(i)

Suppose
\mathbb{O}
is
not
of the form
\mathbb{O}_{(4^{2m},3,1)}
for
G=\mathrm{SO}(8m+4)
. Then

L=\prod_{k\in S_{2}(p)}\mathrm{GL}(k)\times G(n-|S_{2}(p)|),\qquad\mathbb{O}_{L}=\prod_{k\in S_{2}(p)}\{0\}\times\mathbb{O}_{p\#S_{2}(p)}.

In standard coordinates on
\mathfrak{h}^{*}

\delta=\frac{1}{2}(\underbrace{1,...,1}_{|S_{2}(p)|},\underbrace{0,...,0}_{n-|S_{2}(p)|}).

(ii)

Suppose
G=\mathrm{SO}(8m+4)
and
\mathbb{O}=\mathbb{O}_{(4^{2m},3,1)}
. Then

L=\mathrm{GL}(2m+1)\times\mathrm{GL}(2m+1),\qquad\mathbb{O}_{L}=\{0\}\times\{0\}.

In standard coordinates on
\mathfrak{h}^{*}

\delta=\frac{1}{2}(\underbrace{1,...,1}_{2m+1},\underbrace{0,...,0}_{2m+1}).

Proof.

First assume
G=\mathrm{Sp}(2n)
. By Proposition
7.6.7
,
\mathbb{O}
corresponds to a partition
p
of the following form

(7.7.10)

\text{For all }i,\ p_{i}\leq p_{i+1}+2\text{ and }p_{i}<p_{i+1}+2\text{ if }p_{i}\text{ is odd},

By Proposition
7.6.9
, the codimension 2 leaves
\mathfrak{L}_{k}\subset X

are parameterized by the set
S_{2}(p)
, and the corresponding singularities are of type
A_{1}
. Choose
k\in S_{2}(p)
. Then by Proposition
7.6.10
,

M_{k}\simeq^{G}\mathrm{GL}(k)\times\mathrm{Sp}(2n-2k),\qquad\mathbb{O}_{M_{k}}=\{0\}\times\mathbb{O}_{p\#(k)},

and

\tau_{1}(k)=(\underbrace{0,...,0}_{l(k)},\underbrace{1,...,1}_{k},\underbrace{0,...,0}_{n-l(k)-k})\in\mathfrak{h}^{*},\qquad l(k):=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in S_{2}(p)\\
j<k\end{subarray}}j.

By Proposition
7.7.4
,
\eta_{k}(\tau_{1}(k))=c_{k}\omega_{1}(k)
, where
c_{k}=2|\Pic(\mathbb{O}_{M_{k}})||\Pic(\mathbb{O})|^{-1}
. Since
\Pic(\mathbb{O})
is finite,
\Pic(\mathbb{O})\simeq\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O})_{\mathrm{ab}}
. Similarly,
\Pic(\mathbb{O}_{M_{k}})\simeq\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O}_{p\#(k)})_{\mathrm{ab}}
. Both
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O})
and
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O}_{p\#(k)})
are elementary abelian 2-groups of rank equal to the number of distinct even parts in the corresponding partition, see Section
2.2
. By the description of
p
(see (
7.7.10
)) and the definition of
p\#(k)
, it is clear that
p
contains one more distinct even part than
p\#(k)
. Hence,
c_{k}=1
and
\eta_{k}(\tau_{1}(k))=\omega_{1}(k)
. Now the computation of
\delta
is immediate:

\delta=\eta^{-1}(\epsilon)=\sum_{k\in S_{2}(p)}\eta_{k}^{-1}(\epsilon_{k})=\sum_{k\in S_{2}(p)}\frac{1}{2}\tau_{1}(k)=\frac{1}{2}(\underbrace{1,...,1}_{|S_{2}(p)|},\underbrace{0,...,0}_{n-|S_{2}(p)|})\in\mathfrak{h}^{*}.

If
G=\mathrm{SO}(2n+1)
or
\mathrm{SO}(2n)
and
\mathbb{O}
is not of the form
\mathbb{O}_{(4^{2m},3,1)}
for
G=\mathrm{SO}(8m+4)
, the computation is analogous. We leave the details to the reader.

Finally, assume
G=\mathrm{SO}(8m+4)
and
\mathbb{O}=\mathbb{O}_{(4^{2m},3,1)}
. By Proposition
7.6.9
, there are two codimension 2 leaves
\mathfrak{L}_{1},\mathfrak{L}_{2}\subset X
, and the corresponding singularities are both of type
A_{1}
. By Proposition
7.6.10
,

M_{1}\simeq^{G}\mathrm{GL}(4m+2)^{\mathrm{II}},\qquad\mathbb{O}_{M_{1}}=\mathbb{O}_{(2^{2m+1})},\qquad\tau_{1}(1)=\frac{1}{2}(\underbrace{1,...,1}_{2m+1},\underbrace{-1,...,-1}_{2m+1})\in\mathfrak{h}^{*},

and

M_{2}\simeq^{G}\mathrm{GL}(4m+2)^{\mathrm{I}},\qquad\mathbb{O}_{M_{2}}=\mathbb{O}_{(2^{2m+1})},\qquad\tau_{1}(2)=\frac{1}{2}(1,...,1)\in\mathfrak{h}^{*}.

For
k=1,2
, we have
|\Pic(\mathbb{O}_{M_{k}})|=|\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O}_{M_{k}})_{\mathrm{ab}}|=2
and
|\Pic(\mathbb{O})|=|\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O})_{\mathrm{ab}}|=4
(see Section
2.2
for the description of the fundamental groups). Hence
c_{k}=1
and by Proposition
7.7.4

\eta_{k}(\tau_{1}(k))=\omega_{1}(k)
. Thus

\delta=\eta^{-1}(\epsilon)=\frac{1}{2}\eta_{1}^{-1}(\omega_{1}(1))+\frac{1}{2}\eta_{2}^{-1}(\omega_{1}(2))=\frac{1}{2}\tau_{1}(1)+\frac{1}{2}\tau_{1}(2)=\frac{1}{2}(\underbrace{1,...,1}_{2m+1},\underbrace{0,...,0}_{2m+1})

as asserted.
∎

7.7.5.
Case 2 for Spin groups

For the next corollary, let
G=\mathrm{SO}(n)
. Suppose
\mathbb{O}
is a nilpotent
G
-orbit which admits a
\mathrm{Spin}(n)
-equivariant birationally rigid cover which is
not

\mathrm{SO}(n)
-equivariant. Hence,
\mathbb{O}
is one of the orbits described in Proposition
7.6.11
. Let
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathbb{O}
be the universal
G
-equivariant cover. Then the universal
\mathrm{Spin}(n)
-equivariant cover
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}
is birationally rigid (and a two-fold cover of
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
).

Let
\epsilon\in\mathfrak{P}^{\widetilde{X}}
be the barycenter parameter and let
\delta:=\eta^{-1}(\epsilon)
. By Proposition
7.6.12
,

L=\prod_{k\in S_{4}(p)}\mathrm{GL}(k)\times\mathrm{SO}(2n-2|S_{4}(p)|),\qquad\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{L}=\prod_{k\in S_{4}(p)}\{0\}\times\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{p\#S_{4}(p)},

where
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{p\#S_{4}(p)}
denotes the universal
\mathrm{SO}(2n-2|S_{4}(p)|)
-equivariant cover.

Corollary 7.7.7
.

In standard coordinates on
\mathfrak{h}^{*}

\delta=\frac{1}{4}(\underbrace{1,...,1}_{|S_{4}(p)|},\underbrace{0,...,0}_{n-|S_{4}(p)|})\in\mathfrak{h}^{*}.

Proof.

By Proposition
7.6.12
, the codimension 2 leaves
\mathfrak{L}_{k}\subset\widetilde{X}
are parameterized by
S_{4}(p)
and the corresponding singularities are of type
A_{1}
. If
k\in S_{4}(p)
, then by the same proposition

M_{k}\simeq^{G}\mathrm{GL}(k)\times\mathrm{SO}(n-2k),\qquad\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{M_{k}}=\{0\}\times\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{p\#(k)},

where
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{p\#(k)}
denotes the universal
\mathrm{SO}(n-2k)
-equivariant cover. Note that
p
is rather odd by Proposition
7.6.11
. Hence
p\#(k)
is rather odd by the construction of
p\#(k)
. So
\pi_{1}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})\simeq\pi_{1}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{M_{k}})\simeq\mathbb{Z}_{2}
, see Section
2.2
, and

c_{k}=2|\Pic(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{M_{k}})||\Pic(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})|^{-1}=2.

Hence by Proposition
7.7.4
,
\eta_{k}(\tau_{1}(k))=2\omega_{1}(k)
. By Proposition
7.6.10

\tau_{1}(k)=(\underbrace{0,...,0}_{l(k)},\underbrace{1,...,1}_{k},\underbrace{0,...,0}_{n-l(k)-k})\in\mathfrak{h}^{*},\qquad l(k):=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in S_{4}(p)\\
j<k\end{subarray}}j.

Hence,

\eta_{k}^{-1}(\epsilon_{k})=\eta_{k}^{-1}(\frac{1}{2}\omega_{1}(k))=\frac{1}{4}\tau_{1}(k)=\frac{1}{4}(\underbrace{0,...,0}_{l(k)},\underbrace{1,...,1}_{k},\underbrace{0,...,0}_{n-l(k)-k})\in\mathfrak{h}^{*}.

Summing over all
k\in S_{4}(p)
, we obtain the desired formula for
\delta
,

\delta=\eta^{-1}(\epsilon)=\sum_{k\in S_{4}(p)}\eta_{k}^{-1}(\epsilon_{k})=\frac{1}{4}(\underbrace{1,...,1}_{|S_{4}(p)|},\underbrace{0,...,0}_{n-|S_{4}(p)|})\in\mathfrak{h}^{*}.

∎

7.7.6.
Case 2 for exceptional groups

Example 7.7.8
.

Let
G
be the (unique) simple group of type
G_{2}
, and let
\mathbb{O}=G_{2}(a_{1})
. By
[
FJLS15
, Sec 13]
,
\overline{\mathbb{O}}
is normal in codimension 2, and there is a unique orbit
\mathbb{O}_{1}\subset\overline{\mathbb{O}}
of codimension 2, namely
\mathbb{O}_{1}=\widetilde{A}_{1}
. The corresponding singularity is of type
A_{1}
. In this example, we will show that the universal cover
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}
of
\mathbb{O}
is birationally rigid. We will also compute the Levi subgroup
M_{1}\subset G
adapted to the codimension 2 leaf
\mathfrak{L}_{1}\subset X=\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{O}])
as well as the identification
\eta_{1}:\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{m}_{1})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak{P}_{1}^{X}=\mathfrak{P}^{X}
.

Write
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}
for the simple roots for
\mathfrak{g}
, with
\alpha_{2}
the short root, and write
\varpi_{1},\varpi_{2}
for the corresponding fundamental weights. Since
\mathbb{O}
is distinguished, it is birationally induced from the
\{0\}
-orbit of its Jacobson-Morozov Levi
L_{\mathbb{O}}
, see Proposition
2.4.2
and Remark
2.6.3
. In this case,
L_{\mathbb{O}}
is the Levi subgroup (of type
A_{1}
) corresponding to the short simple root
\alpha_{2}
.

\widehat{\mathbb{O}}
is birationally rigid
. Since
\mathbb{O}
is distinguished, the reductive part
\mathfrak{r}
of the centralizer of
e\in\mathbb{O}
is
0
. Thus
H^{2}(\widehat{\mathbb{O}},\mathbb{C})\simeq\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{r})=0
by Lemma
7.2.7
. So by Corollary
7.6.1
, it suffices to show that
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}
is 2-leafless. Note that
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O})\simeq S_{3}
and
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O}_{1})=1
. By
[
DE09
, Sec 4]

\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{rig}}(\mathbb{O})=\{(A_{1},\{0\}),(\widetilde{A}_{1},\{0\})\}

In particular
m(\mathbb{O})=1
. We have

|\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O})||\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O}_{1})|^{-1}=6>1=m(\mathbb{O}),

Thus,
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}
is birationally rigid by Proposition
7.6.15
.

Computation of
M_{1}
. Since
\mathfrak{L}_{1}\subset X
is the unique codimension 2 leaf, it follows immediately from Lemma
7.5.8
that
L=M_{1}
.

Computation of
\eta_{1}
. Since
\mathbb{O}
admits a birationally rigid cover and
\Sigma_{1}\simeq A_{1}
, Proposition
7.7.4
is applicable. Note that
\mathbb{O}_{M_{1}}=\mathbb{O}_{L}=\{0\}
. Hence

c_{1}=2|\Pic(\mathbb{O}_{M_{1}})||\Pic(\mathbb{O})|^{-1}=2|\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O})_{\mathrm{ab}}|^{-1}=1,

and therefore by Proposition
7.7.4

(7.7.11)

\eta_{1}(\tau_{1}(1))=\omega_{1}(1).

By definition
\tau_{1}(1)
is the dominant generator of the free abelian group
\mathfrak{X}(M_{1})
, i.e.
\tau_{1}(1)=\varpi_{1}
. So (
7.7.11
) becomes

(7.7.12)

\eta_{1}(\varpi_{1})=\omega_{1}(1).

Example 7.7.9
.

Let
G
be the simply connected group of type
E_{7}
and let
\mathbb{O}=E_{7}(a_{4})
. By
[
FJLS15
, Sec 13]
,
\overline{\mathbb{O}}
is normal in codimension 2. The codimension 2 orbits
\mathbb{O}_{k}\subset\overline{\mathbb{O}}
as well as the singularities
\Sigma_{k}
and fundamental groups
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O}_{k})
are indicated below

k

\mathbb{O}_{k}

\Sigma_{k}

\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O}_{k})

1

A_{6}

A_{1}

1

2

D_{5}+A_{1}

A_{1}

\mathbb{Z}_{2}

3

D_{6}(a_{1})

A_{1}

\mathbb{Z}_{2}

In this example, we will show that the universal cover
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}
of
\mathbb{O}
is birationally rigid. We will also compute the Levi subgroup
M_{1}\subset G
adapted to the codimension 2 leaf
\mathfrak{L}_{1}\subset X=\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{O}])
as well as the identification
\eta_{1}:\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{m}_{1})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak{P}_{1}^{X}
.

Label the simple roots as follows

\alpha_{1}
\alpha_{2}
\alpha_{3}
\alpha_{4}
\alpha_{5}
\alpha_{6}
\alpha_{7}

and write
\varpi_{1},...,\varpi_{7}
for the fundamental weights. Arguing as in Example
7.7.8
, we see that
\mathbb{O}=\mathrm{Bind}^{G}_{L}\{0\}
, where
L
is the standard Levi subgroup (of Lie type
2A_{1}+A_{2}
) corresponding to the simple roots
\{\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3},\alpha_{4},\alpha_{6}\}
.

\widehat{\mathbb{O}}
is birationally rigid
. Since
\mathbb{O}
is distinguished, the reductive part
\mathfrak{r}
of the centralizer of
e\in\mathbb{O}
is
0
. Thus
H^{2}(\widehat{\mathbb{O}},\mathbb{C})\simeq\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{r})=0
by Lemma
7.2.7
. So by Corollary
7.6.1
, it suffices to show that
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}
is 2-leafless.

Note that
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O})\simeq S_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}
and by
[
DE09
, Sec 4]

(7.7.13)

\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{rig}}(\mathbb{O})=\{(A_{1}+D_{4},\{0\}\times(3,2^{2},1)),(2A_{1}+A_{2},\{0\})\}

In particular
m(\mathbb{O})=3
. Note that

|\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O})||\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O}_{1})|^{-1}=4>3=m(\mathbb{O})

Thus, by the argument given in the proof of
7.6.15
.,
\Sigma_{1}
is smoothened under
\widehat{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}X
.

For
\Sigma_{2}
and
\Sigma_{3}
, a separate argument is needed. We supply one for
\Sigma_{2}
(the argument for
\Sigma_{3}
is analogous). Suppose that
\Sigma_{2}
is
not
smoothened under
\widehat{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}X
. Since
\Sigma_{1}\simeq\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
, and
\widehat{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}X
is generically 4 to 1, the preimage of
\Sigma_{1}
under this map consists of two copies of
\mathbb{C}^{2}
. Write
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{1}
for the preimage of
\mathbb{O}_{1}
under
\widehat{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}X
. Since
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O}_{1})\simeq 1
,
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{1}
has two connected components. The action of
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O})
on
\widehat{X}
permutes the preimages of a point
e\in\mathbb{O}_{1}
. Consider the resulting map
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}S_{2}
, and let
K\simeq\mathbb{Z}_{2}
be its kernel. Let
\widetilde{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}X
be the cover corresponding to the subgroup
K\subset\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O})
. By construction, the preimage of
\mathbb{O}_{1}
under
\widetilde{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}X
has
2
connected components permuted by the action of
K
, and the corresponding singularities are of type
A_{1}
.
Lemma

7.6.6
implies that the singularity
\Sigma_{2}
is not smoothened under the map
\widetilde{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}X
. Therefore,
\dim\mathfrak{P}^{\widetilde{X}}\geq 3
. So by (
7.7.13
), we see that both
{\mathbb{O}}
and
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
are birationally induced from
(2A_{1}+A_{2},\{0\})
. This is a contradiction. Thus
\Sigma_{2}
is smoothened under
\widehat{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}X
.

Computation of
M_{1}
. Let
M
denote the standard Levi of Lie type
D_{6}
corresponding to the simple roots
\{\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3},\alpha_{4},\alpha_{5},\alpha_{6},\alpha_{7}\}
. Note that
L\subset M
and by Proposition
2.3.2

\mathbb{O}_{M}=\mathrm{Ind}^{M}_{L}\{0\}=\mathbb{O}_{(5,3^{2},1)}.

Using Lemma
7.5.10
, we will show that
M=M_{1}
. By
[
KP82
, Table 1]
,
\overline{\mathbb{O}}_{M}
is normal in codimension 2. There are two boundary orbits
\mathbb{O}_{M,2},\mathbb{O}_{M,3}\subset\overline{\mathbb{O}}_{M}
of codimension 2, namely

\mathbb{O}_{M,2}=\mathbb{O}_{(4^{2},3,1)},\qquad\mathbb{O}_{M,3}=\mathbb{O}_{(5,3,2^{2})},

and the corresponding singularities are of type
A_{1}
. Note that
\mathbb{O}_{M,2}=\Ind^{D_{6}}_{2A_{2}}\{0\}
and
\mathbb{O}_{M,3}=\Ind^{D_{6}}_{A_{3}}\{0\}
. Now by
[
DE09
, Sec 4]
, we have
\Ind^{G}_{M_{2}}\mathbb{O}_{M,2}=D_{5}+A_{1}=\mathbb{O}_{2}
and
\Ind^{G}_{M_{2}}\mathbb{O}_{M,3}=D_{6}(a_{1})=\mathbb{O}_{3}
. So
M=M_{1}
by Lemma
7.5.10
.

Computation of
\eta_{1}
. Since
\mathbb{O}
admits a birationally rigid cover and
\Sigma_{1}\simeq A_{1}
, the hypotheses of Proposition
7.7.4
are satisfied. Thus

\eta_{1}(\tau_{1}(1))=c_{1}\omega_{1}(1),\qquad c_{1}=2|\Pic(\mathbb{O}_{M_{1}})||\Pic(\mathbb{O})|^{-1}.

Note that
\Pic(\mathbb{O})\simeq\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O})_{\mathrm{ab}}\simeq S_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}
and
\Pic(\mathbb{O}_{M_{1}})\simeq\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O}_{M_{1}})_{\mathrm{ab}}\simeq\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}
. Thus,
c_{1}=2(4)/4=2
. By definition,
\tau_{1}(1)
is the dominant generator of the free abelian group
\mathfrak{X}(M_{1})
, i.e.
\tau_{1}(1)=\varpi_{1}
. It follows that

\eta_{1}(\varpi_{1})=2\omega_{1}(1)

Example 7.7.10
.

Let
G
be the (unique) simple group of type
E_{8}
, and let
\mathbb{O}=E_{8}(b_{6})
. The universal cover

of

is birationally rigid by Proposition

. By

, Sec 13]
,

is normal in codimension 2. The codimension 2 orbits
\mathbb{O}_{k}\subset\overline{\mathbb{O}}
and the singularities

are indicated below

k

\Sigma_{k}

\mathbb{O}_{k}

1

A_{1}

A_{7}

2

A_{2}

E_{6}(a_{1})+A_{1}

In this example, we will compute the Levi subgroups

adapted to the codimension 2 leaves
\mathfrak{L}_{1},\mathfrak{L}_{2}\subset\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{O}])=X
as well as the identifications

.

Label the simple roots as follows

\alpha_{1}
\alpha_{2}
\alpha_{3}
\alpha_{4}
\alpha_{5}
\alpha_{6}
\alpha_{7}
\alpha_{8}

and write
\varpi_{1},...,\varpi_{8}
for the fundamental weights. Arguing as in Example
7.7.8
, we see that
\mathbb{O}=\mathrm{Bind}^{G}_{L}\{0\}
, where

is the standard Levi subgroup (of Lie type

) corresponding to the simple roots
\{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3},\alpha_{5},\alpha_{6},\alpha_{7}\}
.

Computation of
M_{1}
. Let
M
denote the standard Levi subgroup (of Lie type
E_{7}
) corresponding to the simple roots
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3},\alpha_{4},\alpha_{5},\alpha_{6},\alpha_{7}
. Note that
L\subset M_{1}
and by

, Sec 4]

\mathbb{O}_{M}=\Ind^{M}_{L}\{0\}=A_{4}+A_{2}

Using Lemma
7.5.10
, we will show that

. By
[
FJLS15
, Sec 13]
,
\overline{\mathbb{O}}_{M}
is normal in codimension 2. There is one boundary orbit

, namely
\mathbb{O}_{M,2}=A_{4}+A_{1}
, and the corresponding singularity is of type
A_{2}
. By
