In Section
10.7
, we will explore the limits of this strategy for spin and exceptional groups.

10.1.
Unitarity of Harish-Chandra bimodules

In this section, we will recall what it means for a Harish-Chandra bimodule
\mathcal{B}\in\HC^{G}(U(\mathfrak{g}))
to be
unitary
.

Fix a maximal compact subgroup
K\subset G
. There is an anti-holomorphic involution
\sigma
of
G
such that
K=G^{\sigma}
. The differential of
\sigma
is a conjugate-linear Lie algebra involution of
\mathfrak{g}
(still denoted by
\sigma
). Let
\mathcal{B}\in\HC^{G}(U(\mathfrak{g}))
. A Hermitian form on
\mathcal{B}
is a sesquilinear pairing

\langle\ ,\ \rangle:\mathcal{B}\times\mathcal{B}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathbb{C}

such that

\langle v,w\rangle=\overline{\langle w,v\rangle},\qquad v,w\in\mathcal{B}.

This form is said to be
\sigma
-
invariant
if the following condition is satisfied

\langle XvY,w\rangle=-\langle v,\sigma_{c}(Y)w\sigma_{c}(X)\rangle,\qquad X,Y\in\mathfrak{g},\quad v,w\in\mathcal{B}.

We say that
\mathcal{B}
is
Hermitian
if it admits a non-degenerate
\sigma
-invariant Hermitian form. If
\mathcal{B}
is irreducible and Hermitian, then by a version of Schur’s lemma, this form is unique up to multiplication by
\mathbb{R}^{\times}
. On the level of Langlands parameters (cf. Theorem
3.4.6
) there is a simple criterion for deciding whether an irreducible bimodule is Hermitian. Recall from Theorem
3.4.6
that if
(\lambda_{\ell},\lambda_{r})\in\mathfrak{h}^{*}\times\mathfrak{h}^{*}
is a Langlands parameter, the induced bimodule
I^{G}_{H}(\lambda_{\ell},\lambda_{r}):=\Ind^{G}_{H}\mathbb{C}(\lambda_{\ell},\lambda_{r})
has a unique irreducible subquotient
\overline{I}(\lambda_{\ell},\lambda_{r})
containing the irreducible
G
-representation of extremal weight
\lambda_{\ell}-\lambda_{r}
.

Proposition 10.1.1

(
[
Duf79
]
, Sec 3, see also
[
Kna86
]
, Chp XVI)
.

Let
(\lambda_{\ell},\lambda_{r})
be a Langlands parameter for
G
. Then the following conditions are equivalent

(i)

I^{G}_{H}(\lambda_{\ell},\lambda_{r})
is Hermitian.

(ii)

\overline{I}^{G}_{H}(\lambda_{\ell},\lambda_{r})
is Hermitian.

(iii)

There is an element
w\in W
such that

w(\lambda_{\ell}-\lambda_{r})=\lambda_{\ell}-\lambda_{r},\qquad w(\lambda_{\ell}+\lambda_{r})=-\overline{(\lambda_{\ell}+\lambda_{r})}.

A
\sigma
-invariant Hermitian form is
positive-definite
if it satisfies the following additional condition

\langle v,v\rangle>0,\qquad 0\neq v\in\mathcal{B}.

We say that
\mathcal{B}
is
unitary
if it admits a positive-definite
\sigma
-invariant Hermitian form. There is no simple condition like Proposition
10.1.1
for deciding whether an irreducible bimodule is unitary. For classical groups, a classification of unitary Harish-Chandra bimodules was obtained in
[
Bar89
]
. For general groups, there is no known classification.

Finally, we explain the behavior of unitarity under parabolic induction. Let
M\subset G
be a Levi subgroup of
G
. Recall the functor
\Ind^{G}_{M}:\HC^{M}(U(\mathfrak{m}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\HC^{G}(U(\mathfrak{g}))
defined in Section
3.4
.

Proposition 10.1.2

(
[
GN57
]
, see also
[
Kna86
]
, Chp XVI)
.

Choose continuous functions

\lambda_{\ell},\lambda_{r}:[0,1]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{m})\text{ such that }\lambda_{\ell}(t)-\lambda_{r}(t)\in\mathfrak{X}(M),\quad\forall t\in[0,1],

and let
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\in\HC^{M}(U(\mathfrak{m}))
. Consider the one-parameter families of Harish-Chandra bimodules

\mathcal{B}^{\prime}(t):=\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\otimes\mathbb{C}(\lambda_{\ell}(t),\lambda_{r}(t))\in\HC^{M}(U(\mathfrak{m})),\qquad\mathcal{B}(t):=\Ind^{G}_{M}\mathcal{B}^{\prime}(t)\in\HC^{G}(U(\mathfrak{g})),\qquad t\in[0,1].

Suppose
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}(0)
is unitary. Then

(1)

\mathcal{B}(0)
is unitary.

(2)

If
\mathcal{B}(t)
is irreducible and Hermitian for every
t\in[0,1]
, then
\mathcal{B}(1)
is unitary.

In the setting of the proposition above, it is traditional to say that
\mathcal{B}(0)
is
unitarily induced
from
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}(0)
. Indeed, under the correspondence between Harish-Chandra bimodules and continuous representations of
G
, the functor
\Ind^{G}_{M}
corresponds to (normalized) parabolic induction and
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}(0)
corresponds to a unitary representation of
M
. If
\mathbb{C}(\lambda_{\ell}(1),\lambda_{r}(1))
is a non-unitary character (as it will be in applications), then
\mathcal{B}(1)
is
not
unitarily induced from
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}(1)
, but is unitary nonetheless. In this instance, it is traditional to say that
\mathcal{B}(1)
is obtained from
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}(0)
through a
complementary series construction
, see
[
Kna86
, Chp XVI]
.

10.2.
Parabolic induction of bimodules for Hamiltonian quantizations

In this section, we will define the notion of parabolic induction for Harish-Chandra bimodules for Hamiltonian quantizations of nilpotent covers. The goal is to construct
unipotent bimodules attached to covers of induced orbits via parabolic induction. We note that our construction is closely related to the classical notion of parabolic induction of Harish-Chandra bimodules, see Section

3.4
. In Appendix
A
we will show that, under suitable assumptions, these two constructions coincide. Our version has the advantage of making certain geometric properties of parabolic induction much more transparent, see e.g. Proposition
10.2.6
.

Choose a parabolic subgroup
P=LN\subset G
and a birationally rigid
L
-equivariant nilpotent cover
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{L}
such that
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}=\mathrm{Bind}^{G}_{L}\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{L}
. Choose also a parabolic subgroup
Q=MU\subset G
such that
P\subset Q
and
L\subset M
. Let
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{M}=\mathrm{Bind}^{M}_{L}\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{L}
,
\widetilde{X}_{M}=\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{M}])
, and form the partial resolutions

\rho:\widetilde{Y}:=G\times^{P}(\widetilde{X}_{L}\times\mathfrak{p}^{\perp})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\widetilde{X},\qquad\overline{\rho}:\widetilde{Z}:=G\times^{Q}(\widetilde{X}_{M}\times\mathfrak{q}^{\perp})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\widetilde{X},

and the projections

\pi:\widetilde{Y}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}G/P,\qquad\overline{\pi}:\widetilde{Z}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}G/Q.

Choose parameters
\beta_{1},\beta_{2}\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})
and let
\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{1}}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}}
,
\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{2}}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}}
be the corresponding Hamiltonian quantizations of
\widetilde{X}_{M}
. A
\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{1}}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}}
-
\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{2}}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}}
-bimodule
\mathcal{B}
is
Harish-Chandra
if it admits a good filtration, see the discussion preceding Definition
4.12.1
. Let
\HC(\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{1}}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}},\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{2}}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}})
denote the category of Harish-Chandra
\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{1}}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}}
-
\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{2}}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}}
-bimodules, and define the full subcategory
\HC^{M}(\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{1}}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}},\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{2}}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}})
of
M
-equivariant bimodules analogously to Definition
6.4.1
. There is a forgetful functor

\HC^{M}(\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{1}}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}},\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{2}}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\HC^{M}(U(\mathfrak{m}))

defined using the co-moment maps
\Phi^{\widetilde{X}_{M}}_{\beta_{1}}
and
\Phi^{\widetilde{X}_{M}}_{\beta_{2}}
.

Given a Harish-Chandra bimodule
\mathcal{B}\in\HC^{M}(\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{1}}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}},\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{2}}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}})
, we will produce an induced bimodule

\Ind^{G}_{M}\mathcal{B}\in\HC^{G}(\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{1}}^{\widetilde{X}},\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{2}}^{\widetilde{X}}),

thus defining a functor
\HC^{M}(\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{1}}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}},\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{2}}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\HC^{G}(\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{1}}^{\widetilde{X}},\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{2}}^{\widetilde{X}})
called
parabolic induction
.

The construction requires a bit of preparation. Let
\mathcal{D}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}}_{\beta_{i}}
denote the microlocalization of
\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{i}}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}}
over
\widetilde{X}_{M}
(for
i=1,2
), and let
\mathcal{E}
denote the microlocalization of
\mathcal{B}
over
\widetilde{X}_{M}
(the microlocalization of a filtered quantization was defined in Section
4.1
; the definition is analogous for Harish-Chandra bimodules). Note that
\mathcal{E}
is an
M
-equivariant Harish-Chandra
\mathcal{D}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}}_{\beta_{1}}
-
\mathcal{D}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}}_{\beta_{2}}
-bimodule (the sheaf-theoretic version of a Harish-Chandra bimodule was discussed in Remark

4.12.3
). As in Section
7.3
, form the completed tensor product

\mathcal{D}_{\beta_{i}}^{\prime}:=\mathfrak{D}_{G/U}\ \widehat{\otimes}\ \mathcal{D}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}}_{\beta_{i}}
.
Then let

\mathcal{E}^{\prime}:=\mathfrak{D}_{G/U}\ \widehat{\otimes}\ \mathcal{E}.

Note that
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
is an
M
-equivariant Harish-Chandra
\mathcal{D}_{\beta_{1}}^{\prime}
-
\mathcal{D}_{\beta_{2}}^{\prime}
-bimodule. A good filtration can be constructed as follows. Pick a good filtration
\mathcal{B}_{\leq i}
on
\mathcal{B}
, and consider the corresponding filtration
\mathcal{E}_{\leq i}
on
\mathcal{E}
. Consider the doubled order filtration
\mathfrak{D}_{G/U,\leq i}
(so that vector fields lie in degree
\mathfrak{D}_{G/U,\leq 2}
). Consider the tensor product filtration
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{\leqslant i}
on
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
. It is
G\times M
-stable and good. Then there is a natural identification

(10.2.1)

\gr\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\simeq\mathcal{O}_{T^{*}(G/U)}\boxtimes\gr\mathcal{E}.

Let
\mu:T^{*}(G/U)\times\widetilde{X}_{M}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathfrak{m}^{*}
be the moment map for the
M
-action on
T^{*}(G/U)\times\widetilde{X}_{M}
, and
\Phi_{\beta_{1}}^{\prime}
,
\Phi_{\beta_{2}}^{\prime}
be the quantum comoment maps for
\mathcal{D}_{\beta_{1}}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{D}_{\beta_{1}}^{\prime}
, respectively. Here we use the same shift as in Section

7.3
. The quotient
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}/\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\Phi_{\beta_{2}}(\mathfrak{m})
is a (weakly)
M
-equivariant
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\beta_{1}}
-module, set-theoretically supported on
\mu^{-1}(0)\subset T^{*}(G/U)\times\widetilde{X}_{M}
. Consider the quotient morphism
q:\mu^{-1}(0)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}G\times^{Q}(\widetilde{X}_{M}\times\mathfrak{q}^{\perp})=\widetilde{Z}
and set

\Ind^{G}_{M}\mathcal{E}:=\left(q_{*}[\mathcal{E}^{\prime}/\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\Phi_{\beta_{2}}(\mathfrak{m})]\right)^{M}.

By construction,
\Ind^{G}_{M}\mathcal{E}
has the structure of a right module for
\Ind^{G}_{M}\mathcal{D}_{\beta_{2}}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}}
. It is also a left module for
\Ind^{G}_{M}\mathcal{D}_{\beta_{1}}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}}
(this follows from the
M
-equivariance of
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
). These two actions commute, i.e.
\Ind^{G}_{M}\mathcal{E}
has the structure of an
\Ind^{G}_{M}\mathcal{D}_{\beta_{1}}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}}
-
\Ind^{G}_{M}\mathcal{D}_{\beta_{2}}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}}
bimodule. This bimodule has a
G
-action coming from the
G
-action on
\mathfrak{D}_{G/U}
. It also inherits a filtration from
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
, which is automatically complete and separated. Our next task is to describe its associated graded.

For any
M
-equivariant coherent sheaf
\mathcal{F}
on
\widetilde{X}_{M}
, we will construct a
G
-equivariant coherent sheaf
\Ind^{G}_{M}\mathcal{F}
on
\widetilde{Z}
. View
\mathcal{F}
as a
Q
-equivariant sheaf via the natural map
Q\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}M
and let
p_{1}
denote the projection
\widetilde{X}_{M}\times\mathfrak{q}^{\perp}\rightarrow\widetilde{X}_{M}
. Note that the pullback
p_{1}^{*}\mathcal{F}
is
Q
-equivariant. Restriction to the fiber over
1Q\in G/Q
induces a category equivalence
\Coh^{G}(\widetilde{Z})\xrightarrow{\sim}\Coh^{Q}(\widetilde{X}_{M}\times\mathfrak{q}^{\perp})
.
Let
\Ind^{G}_{M}\mathcal{F}
be the object in

\Coh^{G}(\widetilde{Z})
corresponding to
p_{1}^{*}\mathcal{F}
under this equivalence.

Remark 10.2.1
.

Note that the above is a special case of a more general construction. Let
Z_{M}
be a variety with
M
-action, and let
Z=G\times^{Q}(Z_{M}\times\mathfrak{q}^{\perp})
. Then for
\mathcal{F}\in\Coh^{M}(Z_{M})
, the construction above gives rise to a sheaf
\Ind_{M}^{G}\mathcal{F}\in\Coh^{G}(\widetilde{Z})
. Later in this section, we will apply this construction to
Z_{M}=\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{M}
and
Z=G\times^{Q}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{M}\times\mathfrak{q}^{\perp})
.

Lemma 10.2.2
.

There is a
G
and
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
-equivariant isomorphism of coherent sheaves on
\widetilde{Z}

\gr\Ind^{G}_{M}\mathcal{E}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Ind^{G}_{M}(\gr\mathcal{E}).

Proof.

The proof has three steps.

Step 1
. Recall that
\mu
stands for the moment map
T^{*}(G/U)\times\widetilde{X}_{M}\rightarrow\mathfrak{m}^{*}
. We claim that the natural epimorphism

(10.2.2)

[\mathcal{O}_{T^{*}(G/U)}\boxtimes\gr\mathcal{E}]/[\mathcal{O}_{T^{*}(G/U)}\boxtimes\gr\mathcal{E}]\mu^{*}(\mathfrak{m})\twoheadrightarrow\gr(\mathcal{E}^{\prime}/\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\Phi_{\beta_{2}}(\mathfrak{m}))

induced by (
10.2.1
) is an isomorphism.

Choose a basis
x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}\in\mathfrak{m}
.
The action of
M
on
T^{*}(G/U)
is free, and hence the elements
\mu_{T^{*}(G/U)}^{*}(x_{i})
form a regular sequence in the sheaf of algebras
\mathcal{O}_{T^{*}(G/U)}
. It follows that the elements
\mu^{*}(x_{i})
form a regular sequence for the sheaf of modules
\mathcal{O}_{T^{*}(G/U)}\boxtimes\gr\mathcal{E}
. Hence the Koszul complex associated to this sheaf of modules and the specified elements is exact (in positive degrees). The Chevalley-Eilenberg complex for the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{m}
acting on
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
via
\Phi_{\beta_{2}}
is a filtered deformation of the Koszul complex above. Since the filtration on
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
is complete and separated, the exactness of the Koszul complex implies the exactness of the Chevalley-Eilenberg complex and the claim that the associated graded of the 0th homology of the latter is the 0th homology of the former (the argument is by induction on the filtration degree, compare to the proof of Lemma
7.3.1
). So
(
10.2.2
) is an isomorphism.

Step 2
. Since
M
is reductive, taking
\gr
commutes with taking
M
-invariants. So

\gr\Ind^{G}_{M}\mathcal{E}=\gr\left(q_{*}[\mathcal{E}^{\prime}/\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\Phi_{\beta_{2}}(\mathfrak{m})]\right)^{M}=\left(\gr q_{*}[\mathcal{E}^{\prime}/\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\Phi_{\beta_{2}}(\mathfrak{m})]\right)^{M}

Since
q
is
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
-equivariant, taking
\gr
commutes with
q_{*}
. Thanks to
(
10.2.2
), we see that

(10.2.3)

\gr\Ind^{G}_{M}\mathcal{E}\xrightarrow{\sim}(q_{*}\left([\mathcal{O}_{T^{*}(G/U)}\boxtimes\gr\mathcal{E}]/[\mathcal{O}_{T^{*}(G/U)}\boxtimes\gr\mathcal{E}]\mu^{*}(\mathfrak{m})\right))^{M}.

Step 3
. It remains to identify the target of (
10.2.3
) with
\Ind^{G}_{M}(\gr\mathcal{E})
. First of all,

[\mathcal{O}_{T^{*}(G/U)}\boxtimes\gr\mathcal{E}]/[\mathcal{O}_{T^{*}(G/U)}\boxtimes\gr\mathcal{E}]\mu^{*}(\mathfrak{m})
is nothing but the restriction of
\mathcal{O}_{T^{*}(G/U)}\boxtimes\gr\mathcal{E}
to
\mu^{-1}(0)
. This restriction is identified with the pullback
of
\gr\mathcal{E}
to
\mu^{-1}(0)=G\times^{U}(\widetilde{X}_{M}\times\mathfrak{q}^{\perp})
under the natural projection
G\times^{U}(\widetilde{X}_{M}\times\mathfrak{q}^{\perp})\twoheadrightarrow\widetilde{X}_{M}
. This is a
G\times M
-equivariant sheaf. It is easy to see that it coincides with
q^{*}\Ind^{G}_{M}\gr\mathcal{E}
.
So the target of (
10.2.3
) is

\Ind^{G}_{M}\gr\mathcal{E}
. This completes the proof.
∎

To complete our construction of parabolic induction, define

\Ind^{G}_{M}\mathcal{B}:=\Gamma(\widetilde{Z},\Ind^{G}_{M}\mathcal{E}).

By Proposition
7.3.3
, there are isomorphisms
\Ind^{G}_{M}\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{i}}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}}\simeq\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{i}}^{\widetilde{X}}
for
i=1,2
. Hence,
\Ind^{G}_{M}\mathcal{B}
is an
\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{1}}^{\widetilde{X}}
-
\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{2}}^{\widetilde{X}}
bimodule. It inherits a filtration from
\Ind^{G}_{M}\mathcal{E}
. This filtration is compatible with the filtrations on
\mathcal{A}^{\widetilde{X}}_{\beta_{1}},\mathcal{A}^{\widetilde{X}}_{\beta_{2}}
. There is a
G
-action on
\Ind^{G}_{M}\mathcal{B}
which preserves this filtration, making
\Ind^{G}_{M}\mathcal{B}
a
G
-equivariant
\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{1}}^{\widetilde{X}}
-
\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{2}}^{\widetilde{X}}
bimodule.

By Lemma
10.2.2
, there is an injective homomorphism of
G
-equivariant
\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{X}]
-modules

(10.2.4)

\gr(\Ind^{G}_{M}\mathcal{B})\hookrightarrow\Gamma(\widetilde{Z},\gr(\Ind^{G}_{M}\mathcal{E}))\simeq\Gamma(\widetilde{Z},\Ind^{G}_{M}\gr(\mathcal{E})).

The latter module is finitely-generated over
\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{X}]
. Thus,
\Ind^{G}_{M}\mathcal{B}\in\HC^{G}(\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{1}}^{\widetilde{X}},\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{2}}^{\widetilde{X}})
.

Proposition 10.2.3
.

\Ind^{G}_{M}
defines a left exact functor

(10.2.5)

\Ind^{G}_{M}:\HC^{M}(\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{1}}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}},\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{2}}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\HC^{G}(\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{1}}^{\widetilde{X}},\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{2}}^{\widetilde{X}}).

This functor sends
\HC^{M}_{\partial}(\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{1}}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}},\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{2}}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}})
to
\HC^{G}_{\partial}(\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{1}}^{\widetilde{X}},\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{2}}^{\widetilde{X}})
and descends to a functor between the quotient categories

\Ind^{G}_{M}:\overline{\HC}^{M}(\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{1}}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}},\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{2}}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\overline{\HC}^{G}(\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{1}}^{\widetilde{X}},\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{2}}^{\widetilde{X}}).

Proof.

That
\Ind_{M}^{G}
defines a functor is immediate from the construction. Next we show that this functor is left exact.
Let

0\rightarrow\mathcal{B}_{1}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}_{2}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}_{3}\rightarrow 0

be an exact sequence in
\HC^{M}(\mathcal{A}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}}_{\beta_{1}},\mathcal{A}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}}_{\beta_{2}})
. Equip the terms with good filtrations so that
there is an exact sequence of graded modules

0\rightarrow\gr\mathcal{B}_{1}\rightarrow\gr\mathcal{B}_{2}\rightarrow\gr\mathcal{B}_{3}\rightarrow 0.

The induction functor for coherent sheaves is manifestly exact. Thus, there is an exact sequence of Harish-Chandra
\Ind^{G}_{M}\mathcal{D}^{\widetilde{X}}_{\beta_{1}}
-

\Ind^{G}_{M}\mathcal{D}^{\widetilde{X}}_{\beta_{2}}
-
bimodules

0\rightarrow\Ind^{G}_{M}\mathcal{E}_{1}\rightarrow\Ind^{G}_{M}\mathcal{E}_{2}\rightarrow\Ind^{G}_{M}\mathcal{E}_{3}\rightarrow 0.

Since
\Gamma
is left exact so is

\Ind^{G}_{M}:\HC^{M}(\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{1}}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}},\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{2}}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\HC^{G}(\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{1}}^{\widetilde{X}},\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{2}}^{\widetilde{X}}).

Next we show that

(10.2.6)

\Ind^{G}_{M}\left(\HC^{M}_{\partial}(\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{1}}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}},\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{2}}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}})\right)\subset\HC^{G}_{\partial}(\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{1}}^{\widetilde{X}},\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{2}}^{\widetilde{X}})

Since
\overline{\rho}
is proper,
\mathcal{V}(\Ind^{G}_{M}\mathcal{B})\subseteq\overline{\rho}\mathcal{V}(\Ind^{G}_{M}\mathcal{E})
(cf.
[
BL21
, Lemma 2.18]
). By (
10.2.4
), there is an inclusion

\mathcal{V}(\Ind^{G}_{M}\mathcal{E})\subseteq\overline{\rho}[\mathrm{Supp}(\Ind^{G}_{M}\gr(\mathcal{E}))]\subseteq\overline{\rho}(G\times^{Q}(\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{E})\times\mathfrak{q}^{\perp}))=\overline{\rho}(G\times^{Q}(\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{B})\times\mathfrak{q}^{\perp})).

Since
\overline{\rho}:\widetilde{Z}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\widetilde{X}
maps
\widetilde{Z}-\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
to
\widetilde{X}-\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
, the condition
\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{B})\subseteq\widetilde{X}_{M}-\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{M}
implies
\mathcal{V}(\Ind^{G}_{M}\mathcal{B})\subseteq\widetilde{X}-\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
.
This proves (
10.2.6
).

Finally, we show that
\Ind^{G}_{M}
descends to the quotient categories. Let
\theta_{M}
denote the quotient functor

\HC^{M}(\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{1}}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}},\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{2}}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}})\twoheadrightarrow\overline{\HC}^{M}(\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{1}}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}},\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{2}}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}})
(and define
\theta_{G}
similarly). Note that
\theta_{M}
has a right adjoint (and left inverse)
\theta_{M}^{*}
: first microlocalize to
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{M}
and then take global sections. Define the functor

(10.2.7)

\Ind^{G}_{M}:\overline{\HC}^{M}(\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{1}}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}},\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{2}}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\overline{\HC}^{G}(\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{1}}^{\widetilde{X}},\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{2}}^{\widetilde{X}})

as the compositio
\theta_{G}\circ\Ind^{G}_{M}\circ\theta_{M}^{*}
.
Since the cokernel of the adjunction unit
\operatorname{id}\rightarrow\theta_{M}^{*}\circ\theta_{M}
lies in
\HC_{\partial}^{M}(\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{1}}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}},\mathcal{A}_{\beta_{2}}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}})
, we see that
\theta_{G}\circ\Ind^{G}_{M}\simeq\Ind^{G}_{M}\circ\theta_{M}
. Hence, (
10.2.5
)
descends to (
10.2.7
).
∎

Let
\chi\in\mathfrak{X}(M)
and consider the one-dimensional bimodule
\mathbb{C}(\chi,0)
, see (
3.4.1
). If
\mathcal{B}\in\HC^{M}(\mathcal{A}_{\beta}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}})
, then
\mathcal{B}\otimes\mathbb{C}(\chi,0)\in\HC^{M}(\mathcal{A}_{\beta+\chi}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}},\mathcal{A}_{\beta}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}})
. This defines an equivalence

\otimes\ \mathbb{C}(\chi,0):\HC^{M}(\mathcal{A}_{\beta}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\HC^{M}(\mathcal{A}_{\beta+\chi}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}},\mathcal{A}_{\beta}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}})

with inverse
\otimes\ \mathbb{C}(-\chi,0)
.

For the remainder of this subsection, we will impose the following condition on
\chi
:

(10.2.8)

\beta\text{ and }\beta+\chi\text{ are conjugate under }W^{\widetilde{X}}

Under this condition,
\mathcal{A}_{\beta}^{\widetilde{X}}\simeq\mathcal{A}_{\beta+\chi}^{\widetilde{X}}
as Hamiltonian quantizations and thus
\Ind^{G}_{M}
defines a functor

\Ind^{G}_{M}:\HC^{M}(\mathcal{A}_{\beta+\chi}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}},\mathcal{A}_{\beta}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\HC^{G}(\mathcal{A}_{\beta}^{\widetilde{X}})

Consider the composition

\Ind^{G}_{M}[\chi]:\HC^{M}(\mathcal{A}_{\beta}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}})\overset{\otimes\mathbb{C}(\chi,0)}{\xrightarrow{\sim}}\HC^{M}(\mathcal{A}_{\beta+\chi}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}},\mathcal{A}_{\beta}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}})\overset{\Ind^{G}_{M}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}}\HC^{G}(\mathcal{A}_{\beta}^{\widetilde{X}}).

By Proposition
10.2.3
, this functor descends to a functor (still denoted by
\Ind^{G}_{M}[\chi]
)

\Ind^{G}_{M}[\chi]:\overline{\HC}^{M}(\mathcal{A}_{\beta}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\overline{\HC}^{G}(\mathcal{A}_{\beta}^{\widetilde{X}})

These functors should be viewed as ‘twisted’ versions of parabolic induction. We will now give
\Ind^{G}_{M}[\chi]
an alternative description involving restriction functors. Our main result in this direction, Proposition
10.2.6
, requires a bit of preparation.

Choose
x\in\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
and let
\Omega=\pi_{1}^{G}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})\simeq G_{x}/G_{x}^{\circ}
. By Proposition
6.4.2
, there is a full monoidal embedding

\bullet_{\dagger}:\overline{\HC}^{G}(\mathcal{A}_{\beta}^{\widetilde{X}})\hookrightarrow\Omega\modd.

Consider the group homomorphisms
\mathcal{L}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}}:\mathfrak{X}(G_{x})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Pic(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})
and
\mathcal{L}_{G/Q}:\mathfrak{X}(M)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Pic(G/Q)
, as well as the embedding
\mathfrak{X}(\Omega)\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{X}(G_{x})
induced from the quotient homomorphism
G_{x}\twoheadrightarrow\Omega
. Let
\widetilde{\psi}
denote the composition
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{Z}=G\times^{Q}(\widetilde{X}_{M}\times\mathfrak{q}^{\perp})\twoheadrightarrow G/Q
.

Lemma 10.2.4
.

Assume
\chi
satisfies (
10.2.8
). There is a uniquely defined character
\varphi_{M}^{G}(\chi)\in\mathfrak{X}(\Omega)
such that

\widetilde{\psi}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{G/Q}(\chi)\simeq\mathcal{L}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}}(\varphi_{M}^{G}(\chi))

as
G
-equivariant line bundles on
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
.

Proof.

The proof has two steps. First, we show that the line bundle
\widetilde{\psi}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{G/Q}(\chi)
has trivial first Chern class. Then we use this to deduce the statement of the lemma.

Step 1.
We can assume that
G
is semisimple. The projection
\overline{\pi}:\widetilde{Z}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}G/Q
induces a homomorphism

\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{m})\simeq H^{2}(G/Q,\mathbb{C})\overset{\overline{\pi}^{*}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}}H^{2}(\widetilde{Z},\mathbb{C}).

Since taking Chern classes commutes with pullbacks, the Chern class of
\overline{\pi}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{G/Q}(\chi)
corresponds to
\chi
under the isomorphism above. Thus, the Chern class of
\widetilde{\psi}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{G/Q}(\chi)=[\overline{\pi}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{G/Q}(\chi)]|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}}
corresponds to the image of
\chi
under the composition
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{m})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}H^{2}(\widetilde{Z},\mathbb{C})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}H^{2}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}},\mathbb{C})
. We claim that this map coincides with the composition

(10.2.9)

\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{m})\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak{P}^{\widetilde{X}}\twoheadrightarrow H^{2}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}},\mathbb{C}),

where middle map is the isomorphism
\eta
, see (
7.2.1
), and the final map is projection onto
\mathfrak{P}_{0}^{\widetilde{X}}\simeq H^{2}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}},\mathbb{C})
(the latter identification is by Lemma
7.2.7
). Recall from Proposition
7.2.5
that
\eta
is well-defined defined up to the
W^{\widetilde{X}}
-action on
\mathfrak{P}^{\widetilde{X}}
. Since
W^{\widetilde{X}}
acts trivially on
\mathfrak{P}_{0}^{\widetilde{X}}
, we see that
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})\twoheadrightarrow H^{2}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}},\mathbb{C})
is independent of
P
.

To show that the composition
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{m})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}H^{2}(\widetilde{Z},\mathbb{C})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}H^{2}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}},\mathbb{C})
coincides with (
10.2.9
), pick a
\mathbb{Q}
-terminalization
\widetilde{Y}_{M}
of
\widetilde{X}_{M}
. It has the form
M\times^{P\cap M}(\widetilde{X}_{L}\times(\mathfrak{m}^{*}\cap\mathfrak{p}^{\perp}))
for some choice of a parabolic subgroup
P
contained in
Q
.
Consider the
\mathbb{Q}
-terminalization
\widetilde{Y}:=G\times^{Q}(\widetilde{Y}_{M}\times\mathfrak{q}^{\perp})\simeq G\times^{P}(\widetilde{X}_{L}\times\mathfrak{p}^{\perp})
of
\widetilde{X}
(the isomorphism was established in Lemma
7.4.1
). Let
\pi^{\prime}:\widetilde{Y}\twoheadrightarrow G/Q

denote the projection. The composition
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{m})\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak{P}^{\widetilde{X}}
coincides with the map
\pi^{\prime*}:H^{2}(G/Q,\mathbb{C})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}H^{2}(\widetilde{Y},\mathbb{C})
. Since the natural map
\widetilde{Y}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\widetilde{Z}
is an isomorphism over
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
, the restrictions of
\overline{\pi}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{G/Q}(\chi)
and
\pi^{\prime*}\mathcal{L}_{G/Q}(\chi)
to
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
are isomorphic. Thus the composition
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{m})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}H^{2}(\widetilde{Z},\mathbb{C})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}H^{2}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}},\mathbb{C})
indeed coincides with (
10.2.9
).

Since
\beta+\chi
and
\beta
are conjugate under
W^{\widetilde{X}}
, and
W^{\widetilde{X}}
acts trivially on
H^{2}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}},\mathbb{C})\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{P}^{\widetilde{X}}\simeq\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})
, the image of
\chi
under (
10.2.9
) is
0
. Thus, the Chern class of the line bundle
\widetilde{\psi}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{G/Q}(\chi)
is trivial, as asserted.

Step 2.
By Step 1,

(10.2.10)

c_{1}(\widetilde{\psi}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{G/Q}(\chi))=0.

We will use this to deduce the statement of the lemma. For any algebraic group
H
, the torsion subgroup
\mathfrak{X}(H)_{\mathrm{tor}}\subset\mathfrak{X}(H)
coincides with
\mathfrak{X}(H/H^{\circ})
. In particular,
\mathfrak{X}(G_{x})_{\mathrm{tor}}=\mathfrak{X}(G_{x}/G_{x}^{\circ})\simeq\mathfrak{X}(\Omega)
. Thus,
\mathcal{L}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}}:\mathfrak{X}(G_{x})\simeq\Pic^{G}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})
restricts to an isomorphism

(10.2.11)

\mathcal{L}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}}:\mathfrak{X}(\Omega)\simeq\Pic^{G}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})_{\mathrm{tor}}.

On the other hand, the Chern class map
c_{1}:\Pic(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}H^{2}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}},\mathbb{C})
induces a linear isomorphism
\Pic(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{C}\simeq H^{2}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}},\mathbb{C})
, see Lemma
7.2.7
. Hence,
\ker{c_{1}}=\Pic(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})_{\mathrm{tor}}
. By (
10.2.10
), the line bundle
\overline{\pi}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{G/Q}(\chi)|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}}
belongs to
\ker{c_{1}}
, and is
G
-equivariant by construction. Thus by (
10.2.11
), there is a unique character
\varphi_{M}^{G}(\chi)\in\mathfrak{X}(\Omega)
such that
\widetilde{\psi}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{G/Q}(\chi)\simeq\mathcal{L}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}}(\varphi_{M}^{G}(\chi))
.
∎

For the next lemma, let
P_{M}:=P\cap M
, a parabolic in
M
. Consider the partial resolution
\widetilde{Y}_{M}:=M\times^{P_{M}}(\widetilde{X}_{L}\times\mathfrak{p}_{M}^{\perp})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\widetilde{X}_{M}
and the projection map
{\pi}_{M}:\widetilde{Y}_{M}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}M/P_{M}
. There is a
G
-equivariant identification
\widetilde{Y}=G\times^{Q}(\widetilde{Y}_{M}\times\mathfrak{q}^{\perp})
, see Lemma
7.4.1
.

Set
\Omega^{\prime}=\pi_{1}^{M}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{M})
. Recall the surjective homomorphism
f:\Omega\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Omega^{\prime}
defined in Lemma
2.5.1
, and let
\Omega_{0}=\ker{f}
. Write
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{M}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{M}
for the universal
M
-equivariant cover—its Galois group is
\Omega^{\prime}
. Set

\widecheck{\mathbb{O}}:=\mathrm{Bind}^{G}_{M}\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{M},\qquad\widecheck{Z}=G\times^{Q}(\widecheck{\mathbb{O}}\times\mathfrak{q}^{\perp}).

Observe that
\widecheck{\mathbb{O}}
is a Galois cover of
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
with Galois group
\Omega^{\prime}
.
So by the construction of
f:\Omega\twoheadrightarrow\Omega^{\prime}
in Section
2.5
, we have
\widecheck{\mathbb{O}}\simeq\widehat{\mathbb{O}}/\Omega_{0}
, where
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}
is the universal
G
-equivariant cover of
\mathbb{O}
.
Finally, let
\widecheck{\psi}
denote the composition
\widecheck{\mathbb{O}}\hookrightarrow\widecheck{Z}\twoheadrightarrow G/Q
.

Lemma 10.2.5
.

The following are true:

(i)

Let
\chi\in\mathfrak{X}(L)
. Then there is an isomorphism of
G
-equivariant lines bundles on
\widetilde{Y}

\pi^{*}\mathcal{L}_{G/P}(\chi)\simeq\Ind_{M}^{G}[{\pi}_{M}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{M/P_{M}}(\chi)].

(ii)

Let
\chi^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{X}(\Omega^{\prime})
. Then there is an isomorphism of
G
-equivariant line bundles on
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}

(10.2.12)

\mathcal{L}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}}(f^{*}\chi^{\prime})\simeq\Ind_{M}^{G}[\mathcal{L}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{M}}(\chi^{\prime})]|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}},

and also an isomorphism of
G\times\Omega^{\prime}
-equivariant line bundles on
\widecheck{\mathbb{O}}

(10.2.13)

\chi^{\prime}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\widecheck{\mathbb{O}}}\simeq\Ind_{M}^{G}[\mathcal{L}_{\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{M}}(\chi^{\prime})]|_{\widecheck{\mathbb{O}}}.

Proof.

We first prove (i). We can assume that
G
is semisimple and simply connected.
Note that both sides are line bundles on
\widetilde{Y}
so it suffices to show that their classes in
\operatorname{Pic}(\widetilde{Y})
coincide. Recall, Proposition
7.1.2
, that
\pi^{*}:\Pic(G/P)\xrightarrow{\sim}\Pic(\widetilde{Y})
is an isomorphism. Its inverse is the restriction to the zero section
G/P\subset\widetilde{Y}
. So it is enough to show that

\pi^{*}\mathcal{L}_{G/P}(\chi)|_{G/P}\simeq\Ind_{M}^{G}[{\pi}_{M}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{M/P_{M}}(\chi)]|_{G/P}.

The left hand side is
\mathcal{L}_{G/P}(\chi)
.
It follows easily from the construction of
\operatorname{Ind}^{G}_{M}
that the right hand side is the same.

We proceed to proving (ii). By construction, the left (resp. right) side of (
10.2.12
)
is obtained from the left (resp. right) side of (
10.2.13
) by equivariant descent for the action of
\Omega^{\prime}
. So (
10.2.12
) and (
10.2.13
) are equivalent. To prove (
10.2.13
) we note

\Ind_{M}^{G}[\mathcal{L}_{\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{M}}(\chi^{\prime})]|_{\widecheck{\mathbb{O}}}\simeq\chi^{\prime}\otimes\Ind_{M}^{G}[\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{M}}]|_{\widecheck{\mathbb{O}}}=\chi^{\prime}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\widecheck{\mathbb{O}}}.

This completes the proof of (ii).
∎

The next proposition is the main result of this section. In it, we describe parabolic induction of Harish-Chandra bimodules in terms of the equivalence of
6.4.2
.

Proposition 10.2.6
.

Recall that
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
is birationally induced from
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{M}
, and we write
\Omega^{\prime},\Omega
for
\pi_{1}^{M}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{M}),\pi_{1}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})
, respectively.
Assume
\chi
satisfies (
10.2.8
), and define
\varphi_{M}^{G}(\chi)\in\mathfrak{X}(\Omega)
as in Lemma
10.2.4
. Then the following diagram of functors commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\HC}^{M}(\mathcal{A}_{\beta}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\HC}^{G}(\mathcal{A}_{\beta}^{\widetilde{X}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{\prime}\modd}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega\modd}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet_{\dagger}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Ind^{G}_{M}[\chi]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet_{\dagger}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{M}^{G}(\chi)\otimes f^{*}(\bullet)}

Proof.

Since all categories in question are semisimple, it is enough to show that

(10.2.14)

\varphi_{M}^{G}(\chi)\otimes f^{*}(\mathcal{B}_{\dagger})\simeq\left(\Ind^{G}_{M}[\chi](\mathcal{B})\right)_{\dagger}

for all
\mathcal{B}\in\HC(\mathcal{A}_{\beta}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}})
. Set
V:=\mathcal{B}_{\dagger}\in\Omega^{\prime}\modd
. By
Lemma

6.4.3
, it suffices to show that

(10.2.15)

(\gr\Ind^{G}_{M}[\mathcal{B}\otimes\mathbb{C}(\chi,0)])|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}}\simeq(\varphi^{G}_{M}(\chi)\otimes f^{*}V\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{\mathbb{O}}})^{\Omega}

as
G
-equivariant coherent sheaves, where
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
denotes the universal
G
-equivariant cover.

Using
Lemma

10.2.2
, we have

(\gr\Ind^{G}_{M}[\mathcal{B}\otimes\mathbb{C}(\chi,0)])|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}}\simeq(\Ind^{G}_{M}[\gr\mathcal{B}\otimes\mathbb{C}(\chi,0)])|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}}.

Let
i:\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{M}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\widetilde{X}_{M}
be the inclusion. Since
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}\subset G\times^{Q}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{M}\times\mathfrak{q}^{\perp})
, there is an isomorphism of
G
-equivariant coherent sheaves on
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}

(\Ind^{G}_{M}[\gr\mathcal{B}\otimes\mathbb{C}(\chi,0)])|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}}\simeq(\Ind^{G}_{M}i^{*}[\gr\mathcal{B}\otimes\mathbb{C}(\chi,0)])|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}}.

By (
6.4.3
),
i^{*}\gr\mathcal{B}\simeq(V\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{M}})^{\Omega^{\prime}}
. Thus, we get

(10.2.16)

(\gr\Ind^{G}_{M}[\mathcal{B}\otimes\mathbb{C}(\chi,0)])|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}}\simeq\Ind^{G}_{M}(V\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{M}}\otimes\mathbb{C}(\chi,0)^{\Omega^{\prime}})|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}}=\Ind^{G}_{M}([\chi\otimes V\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{M}}]^{\Omega^{\prime}})|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}}.

We proceed to computing
\Ind^{G}_{M}(\chi\otimes V\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{M}})
.
We claim that for any representation
V
of
\Omega^{\prime}
we have an isomorphism

(10.2.17)

V\otimes\widecheck{\psi}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{G/Q}(\chi)\simeq\Ind^{G}_{M}(\chi\otimes V\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{M}})|_{\widecheck{\mathbb{O}}}

of
G\times\Omega^{\prime}
-equivariant coherent sheaves on
\widecheck{\mathbb{O}}
(recall that
\widecheck{\psi}
is the morphism
\widecheck{\mathbb{O}}\hookrightarrow\widecheck{Z}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}G/Q
). Note that
\Ind^{G}_{M}(\chi\otimes V\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{M}})\simeq V\otimes\Ind^{G}_{M}(\chi\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{M}})
, so we can assume that
V
is trivial. Let
\overline{\pi}

denote the projection
\widetilde{Z}\twoheadrightarrow G/Q
. Now (
10.2.17
)
follows from
\overline{\pi}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{G/Q}(\chi)\simeq\Ind^{G}_{M}(\chi\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{M}})
(a tautological special case of (i) of Lemma
10.2.5
): to get
(
10.2.17
) from the latter isomorphism we pull back along
\widecheck{\mathbb{O}}\rightarrow\widetilde{Z}
.

Let
\mathcal{L}_{\widecheck{\mathbb{O}}}(\varphi^{G}_{M}(\chi))
denote the
G\times\Omega^{\prime}
-equivariant line bundle on
\widecheck{\mathbb{O}}
corresponding to
\varphi^{G}_{M}(\chi)
, this is the pullback of
\mathcal{L}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}}(\varphi^{G}_{M}(\chi))
. By Lemma
10.2.4
, we have an isomorphism
\mathcal{L}_{\widecheck{\mathbb{O}}}(\varphi^{G}_{M}(\chi))\simeq\widecheck{\psi}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{G/Q}(\chi)
of
G\times\Omega^{\prime}
-equivariant line bundles. Combining this with (
10.2.17
) we see that

\Ind^{G}_{M}(\chi\otimes V\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{M}})|_{\widecheck{\mathbb{O}}}\simeq V\otimes\mathcal{L}_{\widecheck{\mathbb{O}}}(\varphi^{G}_{M}(\chi)),

equivalently, by
\Omega^{\prime}
-equivariant descent,

\Ind^{G}_{M}([\chi\otimes V\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{M}}]^{\Omega^{\prime}})|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}}\simeq[V\otimes\mathcal{L}_{\widecheck{\mathbb{O}}}(\varphi^{G}_{M}(\chi))]^{\Omega^{\prime}}\simeq[f^{*}(V)\otimes\varphi^{G}_{M}(\chi)\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{\mathbb{O}}}]^{\Omega}.

Combining the composed isomorphism with
(
10.2.16
) we arrive at
(
10.2.15
).

∎

Corollary 10.2.7
.

Assume
\chi
satisfies (
10.2.8
). Then the functor

\Ind^{G}_{M}[\chi]:\overline{\HC}^{M}(\mathcal{A}_{\beta}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\overline{\HC}^{G}(\mathcal{A}_{\beta}^{\widetilde{X}})

enjoys the following properties:

(i)

Suppose
L\subset M
is a Levi subgroup and choose a character
\chi^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{X}(L)
such that

\bullet

\beta
and
\beta+\chi^{\prime}
are conjugate under
W^{\widetilde{X}_{M}}
,

\bullet

\beta
and
\beta+\chi+\chi^{\prime}
are conjugate under
W^{\widetilde{X}}
.

Then there is a natural isomorphism

\Ind^{G}_{L}[\chi+\chi^{\prime}]\simeq\Ind^{G}_{M}[\chi]\circ\Ind^{M}_{L}[\chi^{\prime}].

(ii)

\Ind^{G}_{M}[\chi]
is conservative, i.e. takes non-isomorphic objects to non-isomorphic objects.

(iii)

\Ind^{G}_{M}[\chi]
takes irreducibles to irreducibles.

Proof.

By Proposition
10.2.6
, it suffices to establish the analogous properties for the functor
\varphi^{G}_{M}(\chi)\otimes f^{*}(\bullet):\Omega^{\prime}\modd\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Omega\modd

(and the similar functors in (i)). Properties (ii) and (iii) follow from the surjectivity of
f
.

For (i), we need some notation. Let
\Omega^{\prime\prime}=\pi_{1}^{L}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{L})
and let

g:\Omega^{\prime}\twoheadrightarrow\Omega^{\prime\prime}
denote the analog of
f:\Omega\twoheadrightarrow\Omega^{\prime}
for
L\subset M
. Then
g\circ f
is the analog of
f
for
L\subset G
. For (i), it suffices to show that

(10.2.18)

\varphi_{L}^{G}(\chi+\chi^{\prime})\otimes(g\circ f)^{*}(\mathcal{B}_{\dagger})=\varphi_{M}^{G}(\chi)\otimes f^{*}[\varphi_{L}^{M}(\chi^{\prime})\otimes g^{*}(\mathcal{B}_{\dagger})],

We will need the following facts about the maps
\varphi_{\bullet}^{\bullet}
:

(1)

\varphi_{L}^{G}(\chi)=\varphi_{M}^{G}(\chi)
.

(2)

\varphi_{L}^{G}(\chi+\chi^{\prime})=\varphi_{L}^{G}(\chi)\otimes\varphi_{L}^{G}(\chi^{\prime})
.

(3)

\varphi_{L}^{G}(\chi^{\prime})=f^{*}\varphi_{L}^{M}(\chi^{\prime})
.

(1) follows from the isomorphism
\pi^{*}\mathcal{L}_{G/P}(\chi)\simeq\overline{\pi}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{G/Q}(\chi)
. (2) follows from the fact that
\pi^{*}\mathcal{L}_{G/P}^{*}(\bullet)|_{\overline{\mathbb{O}}}
is a homomorphism
\mathfrak{X}(L)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Pic^{G}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})
. For (3) we argue as follows. By Lemma
10.2.5
(i) (using the notation therein), there is an isomorphism

\Ind^{G}_{M}(\pi_{M}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{M/P_{M}}(\chi^{\prime}))\simeq\pi^{*}\mathcal{L}_{G/P}(\chi^{\prime}).

Restricting to
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
we get a
G
-equivariant isomorphism

\mathcal{L}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}}(\varphi_{L}^{G}(\chi^{\prime}))\simeq\Ind^{G}_{M}[\mathcal{L}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{M}}(\varphi_{L}^{M}(\chi^{\prime})]|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}}.

Now (3) follows from Lemma
10.2.5
(ii). Equation (
10.2.18
) is an immediate consequence of facts (1)-(3).
∎

10.3.
Unitarity of bimodules for Hamiltonian quantizations

Let
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
be a
G
-equivariant nilpotent cover and choose a Hamiltonian quantization
\mathcal{A}
of
\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}]
. As explained in Section
6.6
, there is a faithful embedding

\Phi^{*}:\HC^{G}(\mathcal{A})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\HC^{G}(U(\mathfrak{g})).

A bimodule
\mathcal{B}\in\HC^{G}(\mathcal{A})
is said to be unitary if
\Phi^{*}(\mathcal{B})
is unitary. In this section, we will translate the content of Proposition
10.1.2
into the setting of
\HC^{G}(\mathcal{A})
.

Choose Levi subgroups
L\subset M\subset G
and a birationally rigid
L
-equivariant nilpotent cover
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{L}
such that
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}=\mathrm{Bind}^{G}_{L}\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{L}
. Let
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{M}=\mathrm{Bind}^{M}_{L}\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{L}
. Let
\beta\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})
and form the Hamiltonian quantizations
\mathcal{A}_{\beta}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}}
and
\mathcal{A}_{\beta}^{\widetilde{X}}
. Choose a character
\chi\in\mathfrak{X}(M)
such that
\beta+\frac{\chi}{2}
and
\beta-\frac{\chi}{2}
are conjugate under
W^{\widetilde{X}}
. If
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\in\HC^{M}(\mathcal{A}_{\beta}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}})
, then
\Ind^{G}_{M}[\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\otimes\mathbb{C}(-\frac{\chi}{2},\frac{\chi}{2})]\in\HC^{G}(\mathcal{A}_{\beta+\frac{\chi}{2}}^{\widetilde{X}})
.

Proposition 10.3.1
.

Suppose
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\in\HC^{M}(\mathcal{A}_{\beta}^{\widetilde{X}_{M}})
is unitary, and
I_{\beta+\frac{\chi}{2}}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})=I_{\beta-\frac{\chi}{2}}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})
as well as
I_{\beta}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{M})
are maximal ideals. Then
\Ind^{G}_{M}[\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\otimes\mathbb{C}(-\frac{\chi}{2},\frac{\chi}{2})]
is unitary.

Proof.

Since
I_{\beta+\frac{\chi}{2}}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})=I_{\beta-\frac{\chi}{2}}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})
and
I_{\beta}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{M})
are maximal ideals, Proposition
A.0.1
is applicable. So there is an isomorphism in
\HC^{G}(U(\mathfrak{g}))

\Phi^{*}\Ind^{G}_{M}[\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\otimes\mathbb{C}(-\frac{\chi}{2},\frac{\chi}{2})]\simeq\Ind^{G}_{M}[\Phi^{*}(\mathcal{B}^{\prime})\otimes\mathbb{C}(-\frac{\chi}{2},\frac{\chi}{2})].

By assumption,
\Phi^{*}(\mathcal{B}^{\prime})
is unitary. Hence
\Phi^{*}(\mathcal{B}^{\prime})\otimes\mathbb{C}(-\frac{\chi}{2},\frac{\chi}{2})
is unitary, since
\mathbb{C}(-\frac{\chi}{2},\frac{\chi}{2})
corresponds to an algebraic (thus, unitary) character of
L
. Now apply Proposition
10.1.2
(1).
∎

For the next proposition, we will assume
M
decomposes as a direct product of reductive subgroups
M_{1},M_{2}\subset M
. Let
\mathbb{O}_{1}
be a nilpotent
M_{1}
-orbit, and
\mathbb{O}_{M}=\mathbb{O}_{1}\times\{0\}
. Let
W_{1}
and
W_{2}
denote the Weyl groups of
M_{1},M_{2}
, respectively. Choose
\nu\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{m}_{2})
and
\chi\in\mathfrak{X}(M_{2})
such that
\beta+\nu+\chi
and
\beta+\nu
are conjugate under
W^{\widetilde{X}}
. If
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\in\HC^{M}(\mathcal{A}_{\beta}^{X_{M}})
, then,
\Ind^{G}_{M}[\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\otimes\mathbb{C}(\nu+\chi,\nu)]\in\HC^{G}(\mathcal{A}_{\beta+\nu}^{X})
.

To simplify notation, let

(10.3.1)

\lambda_{\ell}(t):=\nu+\chi+\frac{1}{2}(t-1)(\chi+2\nu),\qquad\lambda_{r}(t):=\nu+\frac{1}{2}(t-1)(\chi+2\nu),\qquad t\in[0,1].

Proposition 10.3.2
.

Suppose
\mathbb{O}
is birationally induced from
\mathbb{O}_{M}
.
Let
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\in\HC^{M}(\mathcal{A}_{\beta}^{X_{M}})
be an irreducible unitary Harish-Chandra bimodule. Suppose, further, that

(i)

The following ideals are maximal

I_{\beta}(\mathbb{O}_{M}),I_{\beta+\lambda_{\ell}(t)}(\mathbb{O}),I_{\beta+\lambda_{r}(t)}(\mathbb{O}),\qquad\forall\ t\in[0,1].

(ii)

There is an element
w\in W_{2}
such that

w\chi=\chi,\qquad w(\chi+2\nu)=-\overline{(\chi+2\nu)}.

Then
\Ind^{G}_{M}[\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\otimes\mathbb{C}(\nu+\chi,\nu)]
is unitary.

Proof.

Consider the one-parameter family of Harish-Chandra bimodules

\mathcal{B}(t):=\Phi^{*}\Ind^{G}_{M}[\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\otimes\mathbb{C}(\lambda_{\ell}(t),\lambda_{r}(t))],\qquad t\in[0,1].

Note that
\mathcal{B}(0)=\Phi^{*}\Ind^{G}_{M}[\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\otimes\mathbb{C}(\frac{\chi}{2},-\frac{\chi}{2})]
and
\mathcal{B}(1)=\Phi^{*}\Ind^{G}_{M}[\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\otimes\mathbb{C}(\nu+\chi,\nu)]
. The first module is unitary by Proposition
10.3.1
. Thus by Proposition
10.1.2
it suffices to show that
\mathcal{B}(t)
is irreducible and Hermitian for every
t\in[0,1]
.

\underline{\mathcal{B}(t)\text{ is irreducible}}
: let
\mathcal{W}
be the
W
-algebra associated to
\mathbb{O}
and let
R
be the reductive part of the centralizer of
e\in\mathbb{O}
. Recall the functors constructed in Section
3.5

\bullet_{\dagger}:\HC^{G}_{\overline{\mathbb{O}}}(U(\mathfrak{g}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\HC^{R}_{fin}(\mathcal{W}),\qquad\bullet^{\dagger}:\HC^{R}_{\mathrm{fin}}(\mathcal{W})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\HC^{G}_{\overline{\mathbb{O}}}(U(\mathfrak{g})).

For every
t
, there is the adjunction unit homomorphism
\mathcal{B}(t)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}(\mathcal{B}(t)_{\dagger})^{\dagger}
. Since
I_{\beta+\lambda_{\ell}(t)}(\mathbb{O})
is maximal, this map is an isomorphism, see
Lemma

3.5.2
. From this, we will deduce the following implication

(10.3.2)

\mathcal{B}(t)_{\dagger}\text{ irreducible}\implies\mathcal{B}(t)\text{ irreducible}.

Indeed, suppose
\mathcal{B}(t)_{\dagger}
is irreducible and choose a nonzero subbimodule
C\subseteq\mathcal{B}(t)
. There are inclusions
I_{\beta+\lambda_{\ell}(t)}(\mathbb{O})\subseteq\mathrm{LAnn}_{U(\mathfrak{g})}(\mathcal{B}(t))\subseteq\mathrm{LAnn}_{U(\mathfrak{g})}(C)
. Since
I_{\beta+\lambda_{\ell}(t)}(\mathbb{O})
is maximal, these inclusion are equalities. In particular,
\mathcal{V}(C)=V(I_{\beta+\lambda_{\ell}(t)}(\mathbb{O}))=\overline{\mathbb{O}}
and therefore
C_{\dagger}\neq 0
by Proposition
3.5.1
(ii). Since
B(t)_{\dagger}
is irreducible, we must have
C_{\dagger}=B(t)_{\dagger}
. Applying Lemma
3.5.2
to
C
, we see that
C
coincides with
(C_{\dagger})^{\dagger}=(\mathcal{B}(t)_{\dagger})^{\dagger}
under the natural isomorphism
\mathcal{B}(t)\simeq(\mathcal{B}(t)_{\dagger})^{\dagger}
. Hence,
C=\mathcal{B}(t)
and
\mathcal{B}(t)
is irreducible.

In view of (
10.3.2
), it is enough to show that
\mathcal{B}(t)_{\dagger}
is irreducible, for every
t\in[0,1]
. In fact, we will show that
F\mathcal{B}(t)_{\dagger}
is irreducible, for every
t\in[0,1]
, where
F:\HC^{R}_{fin}(\mathcal{W})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}R\modd
is the forgetful functor.

First, we will show that
F\mathcal{B}(t)_{\dagger}
is independent of
t
. If we choose a good filtration on
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
, we get a good filtration on
(\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\otimes\mathbb{C}(\lambda_{\ell}(t),\lambda_{r}(t)))_{\dagger}
, and the associated graded sheaf
\gr(\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\otimes\mathbb{C}(\lambda_{\ell}(t),\lambda_{r}(t)))_{\dagger}
is independent of
t
. Let
\mathcal{F}:=\gr(\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\otimes\mathbb{C}(\lambda_{\ell}(t),\lambda_{r}(t)))_{\dagger}
. By the construction of
\Ind_{M}^{G}
in Section
10.2
, the bimodule
\mathcal{B}(t)
comes with a good filtration
such that
\gr\mathcal{B}(t)|_{\mathbb{O}}\simeq\Ind^{G}_{M}\mathcal{F}|_{\mathbb{O}}
. By (ii) of Proposition

3.5.1
,
\gr([\Phi^{*}\mathcal{B}(t)]_{\dagger})
is
R
-equivariantly isomorphic to the pullback to
S
of
\Ind^{G}_{M}\mathcal{F}
. Since
\gr([\Phi^{*}\mathcal{B}(t)]_{\dagger})
and
[\Phi^{*}\mathcal{B}(t)]_{\dagger}
are isomorphic as
R
-modules,
F\mathcal{B}(t)_{\dagger}
is independent of
t
, as asserted above. Thus, it is enough to prove the irreducibility of
F\mathcal{B}(1)_{\dagger}
.

Since
\mathbb{O}
is birationally induced from
\mathbb{O}_{M}
,
\mathcal{A}_{\beta+\nu}
is a quantization of
\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{O}]
.
By Proposition
3.5.1
,
\underline{J}:=I_{\beta+\nu}(\mathbb{O})_{\dagger}
is an
R
-stable codimension
1
ideal in
\mathcal{W}
.
Recall the functor
\mathsf{B}_{\bullet}:\Omega\modd\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\HC^{R}_{\mathrm{fin}}(\mathcal{W}/\underline{J})
from
Lemma

6.4.5
. In our case,
\Omega=\pi_{1}^{G}(\mathbb{O})=R/R^{\circ}
. Hence
\mathsf{B}_{\bullet}
is a category equivalence.
By (iii) of Corollary
10.2.7
,

\mathcal{B}(1)
is an irreducible object in

\overline{\HC}(\mathcal{A}_{\beta+\nu}^{X})
. Hence by Proposition
6.4.2
,

\mathcal{B}(1)_{\dagger}
is an irreducible object in
\Omega\operatorname{-mod}
. The
R
-module
F\mathcal{B}(1)_{\dagger}
is obtained from
\mathcal{B}(1)_{\dagger}
by pullback along the surjective homomorphism

R\twoheadrightarrow R/R^{\circ}=\Omega
. We conclude that
F\mathcal{B}(1)_{\dagger}
is irreducible. This finishes the proof of the claim that
\mathcal{B}(t)
is irreducible for all
t\in[0,1]
.

\underline{\mathcal{B}(t)\text{ is Hermitian}}
: thanks to condition (i) and Proposition
A.0.1
, there is an isomorphism in
\HC^{G}(U(\mathfrak{g}))

\mathcal{B}(t)=\Phi^{*}\Ind^{G}_{M}[\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\otimes\mathbb{C}(\lambda_{\ell}(t),\lambda_{r}(t))]\simeq\Ind^{G}_{M}[\Phi^{*}\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\otimes\mathbb{C}(\lambda_{\ell}(t),\lambda_{r}(t))]

Let
(\lambda_{\ell}^{\prime},\lambda_{r}^{\prime})\in\mathfrak{h}^{*}\times\mathfrak{h}^{*}
denote the Langlands parameters for
\Phi^{*}\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\in\HC^{M}(U(\mathfrak{m}))
and define

\lambda_{\ell}(t)=\lambda^{\prime}_{\ell}+\lambda_{\ell}(t)\in\mathfrak{h}^{*},\qquad\lambda_{r}(t)=\lambda_{r}^{\prime}+\lambda_{r}(t)\in\mathfrak{h}^{*},\qquad\forall t\in[0,1].

Then
\Phi^{*}\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\simeq\overline{I}^{M}_{H}(\lambda^{\prime}_{\ell},\lambda_{r}^{\prime})
and

\Phi^{*}\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\otimes\mathbb{C}(\lambda_{\ell}(t),\lambda_{r}(t))\simeq\overline{I}^{M}_{H}(\lambda_{\ell}(t),\lambda_{r}(t)),\qquad\forall t\in[0,1].

By the previous step,
\mathcal{B}(t)\simeq\Ind^{G}_{M}\overline{I}^{M}_{H}(\lambda_{\ell}(t),\lambda_{r}(t))
is irreducible for all
t\in[0,1]
. Thus by Lemma
3.4.7

\mathcal{B}(t)\simeq\overline{I}^{G}_{H}(\lambda_{\ell}(t),\lambda_{r}(t)),\qquad\forall t\in[0,1].

To show that
\mathcal{B}(t)
is Hermitian, we use Proposition
10.1.1
. Since
\Phi^{*}\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\simeq\overline{I}^{M}_{H}(\lambda^{\prime}_{\ell},\lambda_{r}^{\prime})
is Hermitian, there is an element
w^{\prime}\in W_{1}
such that

w^{\prime}(\lambda^{\prime}_{\ell}-\lambda_{r}^{\prime})=\lambda^{\prime}_{\ell}-\lambda_{r}^{\prime},\qquad w^{\prime}(\lambda^{\prime}_{\ell}+\lambda_{r}^{\prime})=-\overline{(\lambda_{\ell}^{\prime}+\lambda_{r}^{\prime})}.

Take
w\in W_{2}
as described in the statement of the proposition. Then

ww^{\prime}(\lambda_{\ell}(t)-\lambda_{r}(t))=ww^{\prime}(\lambda_{\ell}^{\prime}-\lambda_{r}^{\prime})+ww^{\prime}(\chi)=\lambda_{\ell}^{\prime}-\lambda_{r}^{\prime}+\chi=\lambda_{\ell}(t)-\lambda_{r}(t),

and

ww^{\prime}(\lambda_{\ell}(t)+\lambda_{r}(t))=ww^{\prime}(\lambda_{\ell}^{\prime}+\lambda_{r}^{\prime})+tww^{\prime}(\chi+2\nu)=-\overline{(\lambda_{\ell}^{\prime}+\lambda_{r}^{\prime})}-t\overline{(\chi+2\nu)}=-\overline{(\lambda_{\ell}(t)+\lambda_{r}(t))}.

Hence,
\mathcal{B}(t)
is Hermitian by Proposition
10.1.1
.
∎

10.4.
Construction of unipotent bimodules: some general results

In Section
10.5
, we will construct all unipotent bimodules for linear classical groups via parabolic induction. More precisely, for each nilpotent cover
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
, we will construct a Levi subgroup
L\subset G
and a rigid orbit
\mathbb{O}_{L}
such that all bimodules in
\mathrm{Unip}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})
are constructed from bimodules in
\mathrm{Unip}_{\mathbb{O}_{L}}(L)
through several unitarity-preserving operations: unitary induction, complementary series, and extraction of direct summands. The proof of this result is essentially an induction on the number of codimension 2 leaves in
X=\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{O}])
. In this section, we will work out the induction step.

Suppose for convenience that
G
is semisimple, and let
\mathbb{O}
be a nilpotent
G
-orbit. Choose a Levi subgroup
L\subset G
and a birationally rigid
L
-orbit
\mathbb{O}_{L}
such that
\mathbb{O}=\mathrm{Bind}^{G}_{L}\mathbb{O}_{L}
. For the remainder of this subsection, we will assume

(a1)

\mathbb{O}
admits a birationally rigid cover.

Choose a codimension 2 leaf
\mathfrak{L}_{k}\subset X
and let
\Sigma_{k}=\mathbb{C}^{2}/\Gamma_{k}
be the corresponding singularity. Assumption (a1) guarantees that
H^{2}(\mathbb{O},\mathbb{C})=0
, see Corollary
7.6.1
. Let
M_{k}\subset G
be the (unique) Levi subgroup adapted to
\mathfrak{L}_{k}
, see (
7.5.1
). Then
L\subset M_{k}
and
\eta
restricts to an isomorphism
\eta_{k}:\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{m}_{k})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak{P}_{k}^{X}
. As usual, let
\mathbb{O}_{M_{k}}=\mathrm{Bind}^{M_{k}}_{L}\mathbb{O}_{L}
. To simplify the arguments, we will impose some additional conditions on
\mathbb{O}
and
\mathfrak{L}_{k}
, which are satisfied in many cases:

(a2)

\pi_{1}(\mathfrak{L}_{k})
acts trivially on
H^{2}(\mathfrak{S}_{k},\mathbb{C})
.

(a3)

\mathbb{O}_{M_{k}}
admits a 2-leafless cover.

(a4)

\pi_{1}^{G}(\mathbb{O})
and
\pi_{1}^{M_{k}}(\mathbb{O}_{M_{k}})
are abelian.

Here
\mathfrak{S}_{k}
denotes, as usual, the minimal resolution of
\Sigma_{k}
.

Consider the barycenter parameters
\epsilon\in\mathfrak{P}^{X}
and
\epsilon^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{P}^{X_{M_{k}}}
, cf. Example
5.3.2
. Define the elements

\delta:=\eta^{-1}(\epsilon)\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}),\qquad\delta^{\prime}:=\eta_{M_{k}}^{-1}(\epsilon^{\prime})\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}\cap[\mathfrak{m}_{k},\mathfrak{m}_{k}]),\qquad\delta_{k}:=\eta_{k}^{-1}(\epsilon_{k})\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{m}_{k}).

Lemma 10.4.1
.

\delta=\delta^{\prime}+\delta_{k}
.

Proof.

The space
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})
inherits a nondegenerate symmetric bilinear form
B
from the dual Cartan subalgebra
\mathfrak{h}^{*}
. Note that
B
induces a form
B^{\eta}
on
\mathfrak{P}^{X}
via the identification
\eta:\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak{P}^{X}
. We claim that the decomposition
\mathfrak{P}^{X}=\bigoplus_{k}\mathfrak{P}_{k}^{X}
is orthogonal with respect to
B^{\eta}
. Note that
B
is invariant under the natural action of
N_{G}(L)
. By (ii) of Proposition
7.2.2

W^{X}=N_{G}(L,\mathbb{O}_{L})/L
. Hence,
B^{\eta}
is invariant under
W^{X}
. The decomposition
\mathfrak{P}^{X}=\bigoplus_{k}\mathfrak{P}_{k}^{X}
is orthogonal with respect to any nondegenerate
W^{X}
-invariant symmetric form, and, in particular, with respect to
B^{\eta}
, as asserted. Under
\eta
,
\mathfrak{P}^{X}_{k}
corresponds to
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{m}_{k})
, see
(
7.5.2
). Since

\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}\cap[\mathfrak{m}_{k},\mathfrak{m}_{k}])\subset\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})
is the orthogonal complement of
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{m}_{k})\subset\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})
, it follows that
\eta\left(\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}\cap[\mathfrak{m}_{k},\mathfrak{m}_{k}])\right)\subset\mathfrak{P}^{X}
is the orthogonal complement of
\mathfrak{P}_{k}^{X}
. By the discussion above,
\bigoplus_{i\neq k}\mathfrak{P}_{i}^{X}
is the orthogonal complement of
\mathfrak{P}_{k}^{X}
. So
\eta(\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}\cap[\mathfrak{m}_{k},\mathfrak{m}_{k}]))=\bigoplus_{i\neq k}\mathfrak{P}_{i}^{X}
.

Now it suffices to show that the orthogonal projections of
\delta
to
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{m}_{k})
and
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}\cap[\mathfrak{m}_{k},\mathfrak{m}_{k}])
coincide with
\delta_{k}
and
\delta^{\prime}
, respectively. Note that the projection of
\epsilon

to
\mathfrak{P}^{X}_{k}
is determined by the isomorphism type of
\Sigma_{k}
. By Proposition
7.5.6
, the codimension
2
leaves in
Z_{k}:=G\times^{Q_{k}}(X_{M_{k}}\times\mathfrak{q}_{k}^{\perp})
are in bijection with the leaves
\mathfrak{L}_{i},i\neq k
, via the partial resolution
Z_{k}\twoheadrightarrow X
.

We can describe these symplectic leaves explicitly using the geometry of
X_{M_{k}}
, Namely, let
\mathfrak{L}_{i}^{\prime}\subset X_{M_{k}}
be a codimension
2
leaf. Then
\mathfrak{L}_{i}=G\times^{Q_{k}}(\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}_{i}\times\mathfrak{q}_{k}^{\perp})
is a symplectic leaf of
Z_{k}
. Pick a point
x\in\mathfrak{L}_{i}^{\prime}\subset\mathfrak{L}_{i}
, and let
\Sigma_{i}\subset X_{M_{k}}
be the corresponding Kleinian singularity. There is a following commutative diagram.
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{S}_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Y}_{M_{k}}^{\mathrm{reg}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Y}^{\mathrm{reg}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Sigma}_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{M_{k}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}

It implies that for every
i\neq k
the pullback map
H^{2}({Y}^{\mathrm{reg}},\mathbb{C})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}H^{2}(\mathfrak{S}_{i},\mathbb{C})
factors through the pullback map
H^{2}(Y^{\mathrm{reg}},\mathbb{C})\twoheadrightarrow H^{2}(Y_{M_{k}}^{\mathrm{reg}},\mathbb{C})
. Therefore, the projection
\mathfrak{P}^{X}\twoheadrightarrow\bigoplus_{i\neq k}\mathfrak{P}^{X}_{i}
coincides with the pullback map
H^{2}(Y^{\mathrm{reg}},\mathbb{C})\twoheadrightarrow H^{2}(Y_{M_{k}}^{\mathrm{reg}},\mathbb{C})
. By Proposition
7.4.2
, the latter coincides with the projection
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})\twoheadrightarrow\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}\cap[\mathfrak{m}_{k},\mathfrak{m}_{k}])
. We conclude that the projection of
\delta
to
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}\cap[\mathfrak{m}_{k},\mathfrak{m}_{k}])
coincides with
\delta^{\prime}
.

It remains to show that the orthogonal projection of
\delta
to
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{m}_{k})
coincides with
\delta_{k}
. This is equivalent to the claim that the unique
W^{X}
-equivariant projection
\mathfrak{P}^{X}\twoheadrightarrow\mathfrak{P}^{X}_{k}
sends
\epsilon
to
\epsilon_{k}
. This follows directly from the definition of
\epsilon
.
∎

Below, we will construct the irreducible objects in
\overline{\HC}^{G}(\mathcal{A}_{\delta}^{X})
from the irreducible objects in
\overline{\HC}^{M}(\mathcal{A}_{\delta^{\prime}}^{X_{M}})
using parabolic induction. Mirroring Section
7.5
, we will consider separately two (overlapping) cases.

10.4.1.
Case 1

Assume the conditions of Proposition
7.7.1
:

(b1)

\mathfrak{L}_{k}=\mathfrak{L}_{1}
is the unique codimension 2 leaf in
X
.

(b2)

\Pic(\mathbb{O})\simeq\mathfrak{X}(\Gamma_{1})
.

(b3)

\Pic(\mathbb{O}_{M})\simeq 0
(where we write
M
for
M_{1}
).

(b4)

There is a unique parabolic subgroup
P\subset G
(up to conjugation by
W^{X}
) with Levi subgroup
L
.

Fix the notation of Section
7.7.1
, i.e. let
\{\tau_{i}\}\subset\mathfrak{h}^{*}
denote the dominant generators for the free abelian group
\mathfrak{X}(L)
and let
\{\omega_{i}\}
denote the fundamental weights in
\mathfrak{P}_{1}^{X}\simeq\mathfrak{h}_{1}^{*}
. Under the conditions above, we have

M_{1}=L,\qquad\mathbb{O}_{M_{1}}=\mathbb{O}_{L},\qquad\eta_{1}=\eta,\qquad\delta^{\prime}=0,

and, by Proposition
7.7.1
,

\eta\{\tau_{i}\}=\{\omega_{i}\}.

In this setting, our main result is the following.

Proposition 10.4.2
.

Assume conditions (a1)-(a4), (b1)-(b4), as well as:

(b5)

\Sigma
is of type
A_{d-1}
.

(b6)

|\pi_{1}^{G}(\mathbb{O})|=d|\pi_{1}^{L}(\mathbb{O}_{L})|
.

Then

(1)

If
\mathcal{B}\in\overline{\HC}^{L}(\mathcal{A}_{0}^{X_{L}})
, then the induced bimodules

\Ind^{G}_{L}[\mathcal{B}\otimes\mathbb{C}(\delta,\delta)]\quad\text{and}\quad\Ind^{G}_{L}[\mathcal{B}\otimes\mathbb{C}(\delta-\tau_{i},\delta)],\quad 1\leq i\leq d-1,

are in
\overline{\HC}^{G}(\mathcal{A}_{\delta}^{X})
.

(2)

If
\mathcal{B}
is irreducible, then so are the objects in (1).

(3)

Every irreducible object in
\overline{\HC}^{G}(\mathcal{A}_{\delta}^{X})
is isomorphic to exactly one bimodule of the form described in (1) (for a unique irreducible
\mathcal{B}
).

Proof.

For (1), we must show that
\delta
and
\delta-\tau_{i}
are conjugate under
W^{X}
, for
1\leq i\leq d-1
. Note that
\eta(\delta)=\epsilon
and
\eta(\delta-\tau_{i})=\epsilon-\omega_{i}
. By condition (b1),
W^{X}\simeq W_{1}
and by condition (b5),
W_{1}\simeq S_{d}
with its standard action on
\mathfrak{h}_{1}^{*}
. Thus, it suffices to show that
\epsilon
and
\epsilon-\omega_{i}
, when written in standard coordinates on
\mathfrak{h}_{1}^{*}
, differ by permutation of entries. In standard coordinates

\epsilon=\frac{1}{d}(\omega_{1}+...+\omega_{d-1})=\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i=1}^{d-1}(\underbrace{d-i,...,d-i}_{i},\underbrace{-i,...,-i}_{d-i})=\frac{1}{2d}(d-1,d-3,...,3-d,1-d)

and

\displaystyle\epsilon-\omega_{i}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2d}(d-1,d-3,...,3-d,1-d)-\frac{1}{d}(\underbrace{d-i,...,d-i}_{i},\underbrace{-i,...,-i}_{d-i})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2d}(\underbrace{2i-1-d,2i-3-d,...,1-d}_{i},\underbrace{d-1,d-3,...,2i+1-d}_{d-i})

Let
\sigma_{i}\in S_{d}
denote the permutation given by
\sigma_{i}(j)=d-i+j
for
j\leqslant i
and
\sigma_{i}(j)=j-i
for
j>i
. Then clearly
\epsilon-\omega_{i}=\sigma_{i}(\epsilon)
. This completes the proof of (1).

Since
\mathcal{B}\in\overline{\HC}^{L}(\mathcal{A}_{\delta}^{X_{L}})
is irreducible, the induced bimodules
\Ind^{G}_{L}[\mathcal{B}\otimes\mathbb{C}(\delta,\delta)],\Ind^{G}_{L}[\mathcal{B}\otimes\mathbb{C}(\delta-\tau_{i},\delta)]\in\overline{\HC}^{G}(\mathcal{A}_{\delta}^{X})
are irreducible by Corollary
10.2.7
(iii). This completes the proof of (2).

By condition (a1), the universal
G
-equivariant cover
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}
of
\mathbb{O}
is 2-leafless. By Proposition
5.3.1
,
\mathcal{A}_{\delta}^{X}
is identified with the
\pi_{1}^{G}(\mathbb{O})
-invariants in
\mathcal{A}^{\widehat{X}}_{0}
, where, as usual
\widehat{X}:=\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\widehat{\mathbb{O}}])
.
Similarly, by Proposition
5.3.1
and (a3),
\mathcal{A}_{0}^{X_{L}}
is the
\pi_{1}^{L}(\mathbb{O}_{L})
-invariants in
\mathcal{A}_{0}^{\widehat{X}_{L}}
. Hence by Corollary
6.4.4
, there are equivalences

\overline{\HC}^{G}(\mathcal{A}_{\delta}^{X})\simeq\pi_{1}^{G}(\mathbb{O})\modd,\qquad\overline{\HC}^{L}(\mathcal{A}_{0}^{X_{L}})\simeq\pi_{1}^{L}(\mathbb{O}_{L})\modd.

Thus, by condition (a4),

|\mathrm{Irr}[\overline{\HC}^{G}(\mathcal{A}_{\delta}^{X})]|=|\pi_{1}^{G}(\mathbb{O})|,\ |\mathrm{Irr}[\overline{\HC}^{L}(\mathcal{A}_{0}^{X_{L}})]|=|\pi_{1}^{L}(\mathbb{O}_{L})|.

And, by (b6),

|\mathrm{Irr}[\overline{\HC}^{G}(\mathcal{A}_{\delta}^{X})]|=d\ |\mathrm{Irr}[\overline{\HC}^{L}(\mathcal{A}_{0}^{X_{L}})]|.

To prove (3) it suffices to show that the induced bimodules

\{\Ind^{G}_{L}[\mathcal{B}\otimes\mathbb{C}(\delta,\delta)]\mid\mathcal{B}\text{ irreducible}\}\cup\{\Ind^{G}_{L}[\mathcal{B}\otimes\mathbb{C}(\delta,\delta-\tau_{i})]\mid\mathcal{B}\text{ irreducible},\ 1\leq i\leq d-1\}

are pairwise non-isomorphic. Recall that
\tau_{i}
determines a character
\varphi^{G}_{L}(\tau_{i})
of
\pi_{1}^{G}(\mathbb{O})
, see Lemma
10.2.4
. By
10.2.6
we have

\left(\Ind^{G}_{L}[\mathcal{B}\otimes\mathbb{C}(\delta,\delta)]\right)_{\dagger}\simeq f^{*}(\mathcal{B}_{\dagger}),\quad\left(\Ind^{G}_{L}[\mathcal{B}\otimes\mathbb{C}(\delta,\delta-\tau_{i})]\right)_{\dagger}\simeq(\varphi^{G}_{L}\tau_{i})^{-1}\otimes f^{*}(\mathcal{B}_{\dagger}),\quad 1\leq i\leq d-1.

Thus, it is enough to show that the characters
\varphi^{G}_{L}(\tau_{i})
are nontrivial and distinct. Let
\Sigma^{\times}=\Sigma-\{0\}
. By Lemma
10.2.4
, there is an isomorphism
\mathcal{L}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}}(\varphi^{G}_{L}(\tau_{i}))|_{\Sigma^{\times}}\simeq\pi^{*}\mathcal{L}_{G/P}(\tau_{i})|_{\Sigma^{\times}}
of line bundles on
\Sigma^{\times}
. By
7.7.4
,
\pi^{*}\mathcal{L}_{G/P}(\tau_{i})|_{\mathfrak{S}}\simeq\sigma(\omega_{i})
, where
\sigma(\omega_{i})
is the line bundle on
\mathfrak{S}
corresponding to
\omega_{i}
via the isomorphism
\sigma:\Lambda\simeq\Pic(\mathfrak{S})
, see Proposition

4.2.3
. Thus, it suffices to show that
\sigma(\omega_{i})|_{\Sigma^{\times}}
are distinct (and nontrivial) for
1\leq i\leq d-1
.

The restriction map
\Pic(\mathfrak{S})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Pic(\Sigma^{\times})
corresponds to the restriction map
\Lambda\twoheadrightarrow\mathfrak{X}(Z(\mathrm{SL}(d)))
, under the identifications
\Lambda\simeq\Pic(\mathfrak{S})
and
\mathfrak{X}(Z(\mathrm{SL}(d)))\simeq\Pic(\Sigma^{\times})
, see Section
4.2
. It is trivial to check that the fundamental weights
\{\omega_{i}\}
restrict to distinct nontrivial characters of
Z(\mathrm{SL}(d))
.
∎

10.4.2.
Case 2

Assume the condition of Proposition
7.7.4
:

(c1)

\Sigma_{k}
is of type
A_{1}
for all
k
.

By Proposition
7.7.4
,

\eta_{k}(\tau_{1}(k))=c_{k}\omega_{1}(k),\qquad c_{k}=2|\Pic(\mathbb{O}_{M_{k}})||\Pic(\mathbb{O})|^{-1}.

Our main result in this setting is as follows. The proof, which is almost identical to that of Proposition
10.4.2
, is omitted.

Proposition 10.4.3
.

Assume conditions (a1)-(a4), (c1), as well as:

(c2)

c_{k}=1
for all
k
.

(c3)

|\pi_{1}^{G}(\mathbb{O})|=2|\pi_{1}^{M_{k}}(\mathbb{O}_{M_{k}})|
for all
k
.

Then

(1)

If
\mathcal{B}\in\overline{\HC}^{M_{k}}(\mathcal{A}_{\delta^{\prime}}^{X_{M_{k}}})
, then the induced bimodules

\Ind^{G}_{M_{k}}[\mathcal{B}\otimes\mathbb{C}(\frac{1}{2}\tau_{1}(k),\frac{1}{2}\tau_{1}(k))]\quad\text{and}\quad\Ind^{G}_{M_{k}}[\mathcal{B}\otimes\mathbb{C}(-\frac{1}{2}\tau_{1}(k),\frac{1}{2}\tau_{1}(k))]

are in
\overline{\HC}^{G}(\mathcal{A}_{\delta}^{X})
.

(2)

If
\mathcal{B}
is irreducible, then so are both objects in (1).

(3)

Every irreducible object in
\overline{\HC}^{G}(\mathcal{A}_{\delta}^{X})
is isomorphic to exactly one bimodule of the form described in (1) (for a unique irreducible
\mathcal{B}
).

10.5.
Unipotent bimodules attached to birationally rigid covers: classical case

10.5.1.
Type A

Let
G=\mathrm{SL}(n)
and let
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
be a birationally rigid
G
-equivariant nilpotent cover. By Proposition
7.6.4
,
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
is the universal cover of an orbit
\mathbb{O}
corresponding to a partition of the form
(d^{m})
of
n
. Let

L=\mathrm{S}(\mathrm{GL}(m)^{d}),\qquad\mathbb{O}_{L}=\{0\},

so that
\mathbb{O}=\mathrm{Bind}^{G}_{L}\mathbb{O}_{L}
, and fix
\tau_{1}(1),\tau_{2}(1),...,\tau_{d-1}(1),\delta\in\mathfrak{h}^{*}
as in Corollary
7.7.3
(and its proof). Thus,
\mathcal{A}_{\delta}^{X}
is isomorphic to the
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O})
-invariants in
\mathcal{A}_{0}^{\widetilde{X}}
and
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O})\simeq\mathbb{Z}_{d}
.

Proposition 10.5.1
.

The set
\mathrm{Unip}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}}(G)
consists of the following
d
bimodules

\{\Ind^{G}_{L}\mathbb{C}(\delta,\delta)\}\cup\{\Ind^{G}_{L}\mathbb{C}(\delta-\tau_{i}(1),\delta)\mid 1\leq i\leq d-1\}.

All are unitary.

Proof.

Let
I=I_{0}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})=I_{\delta}(\mathbb{O})
. By Proposition
B.1.1
,
I
is maximal, so the algebra
\mathcal{A}_{\delta}^{X}
is simple, see Proposition
6.3.3
. Thus by Lemma
4.12.2
,

\HC^{G}(\mathcal{A}_{\delta}^{X})\xrightarrow{\sim}\overline{\HC}^{G}(\mathcal{A}_{\delta}^{X}),

By Corollary
6.5.6
,
\Phi:U(\mathfrak{g})/I\xrightarrow{\sim}(\mathcal{A}_{\delta}^{X})^{\Pi}
. In particular,

\Phi^{*}:\HC^{G}(\mathcal{A}_{\delta}^{X})\xrightarrow{\sim}\HC^{G}(U(\mathfrak{g})/I).

By Proposition
7.6.9
, there is a unique codimension 2 leaf
\mathfrak{L}_{1}\subset X
and, by Proposition
7.6.10
,

M_{1}=L,\qquad\mathbb{O}_{M_{1}}=\{0\}.

Conditions (a1)-(a2) as well as (b1)-(b4) were verified in the proof of Corollary
7.7.3
. To apply Proposition
10.4.2
it remains to check (a3)-(a4) and (b5)-(b6). Since
\mathbb{O}_{M_{1}}=\{0\}
, condition (a3) is trivial. Condition (a4) and (b6) follow from
\pi_{1}^{G}(\mathbb{O})\simeq\mathbb{Z}_{d}
. (b5) was established in Proposition
7.6.9
(i). If
\Phi^{*}\mathcal{B}\in\mathrm{Unip}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}}(G)
, then, by Proposition
10.4.2
,

\mathcal{B}\simeq\Ind^{G}_{L}\mathbb{C}(\delta,\delta)\quad\text{or}\quad\mathcal{B}\simeq\Ind^{G}_{L}\mathbb{C}(\delta-\tau_{i}(1),\delta),\quad 1\leq i\leq d-1.

First, assume
\mathcal{B}\simeq\Ind^{G}_{L}\mathbb{C}(\delta,\delta)
. We will show that
\mathcal{B}
is unitary using Proposition
10.3.2
. Set

M_{1}=1,\qquad M_{2}=L,\qquad\mathbb{O}_{M}=\{0\},\qquad\beta=0,\qquad\nu=\delta,\qquad\chi=0.

Condition (i) of Proposition
10.3.2
follows from Lemma
B.1.2
. It remains to exhibit an element
w\in S_{n}
such that
w(\delta)=-\delta
. For this, we choose
w=w_{0}
(the longest element of
S_{n}
).

Next, assume
\mathcal{B}\simeq\Ind^{G}_{L}\mathbb{C}(\delta-\tau_{i}(1),\delta)
. We will show that
\mathcal{B}
is unitary in the same way. Set

M_{1}=L,\qquad M_{2}=\{1\},\qquad\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{M}=\{0\},\qquad\beta=0,\qquad\nu=\delta,\qquad\chi=-\tau_{i}(1).

Condition (i) of Proposition
10.3.2
follows from Lemma
B.1.3
. It remains to exhibit an element
w\in S_{n}
such that

(10.5.1)

w\tau_{i}(1)=\tau_{i}(1),\qquad w(2\delta-\tau_{i}(1))=\tau_{i}(1)-2\delta.

Note that

\displaystyle\tau_{i}(1)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d}(\underbrace{d-i,d-i,...,d-i}_{mi},\underbrace{-i,-i,...,-i}_{m(d-i)}),

\displaystyle 2\delta-\tau_{i}(1)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d}(\underbrace{i-1,...}_{m},\underbrace{i-3,...}_{m},...,\underbrace{1-i,...}_{m},\underbrace{d-i-1}_{m},...,\underbrace{1+i-d}_{m}).

Write
w^{\prime}_{0}\in S_{mi}
and
w_{0}^{\prime\prime}\in S_{m(d-i)}
for the longest elements, and take
w
to be the image of
(w^{\prime}_{0},w^{\prime\prime}_{0})\in S_{mi}\times S_{m(d-i)}
under the natural embedding
S_{mi}\times S_{m(d-i)}\subset S_{n}
. Then an easy calculation shows that
w
satisfies (
10.5.1
).
∎

10.5.2.
Types B,C,and D

Let
G=\mathrm{Sp}(2n)
,
\mathrm{SO}(2n)
, or
\mathrm{SO}(2n+1)
, and let

\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
be a birationally rigid
G
-equivariant nilpotent cover. We will first assume that
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
covers an orbit
\mathbb{O}
which is
not
of the form
\mathbb{O}_{(4^{2m},3,1)}
for
G=\mathrm{SO}(2n)
. The orbits
\mathbb{O}
which can appear are described in Proposition
7.6.7
.

Let

L=\prod_{k\in S_{2}(p)}\mathrm{GL}(k)\times G(n-|S_{2}(p)|),\qquad\mathbb{O}_{L}=\prod_{k\in S_{2}(p)}\{0\}\times\mathbb{O}_{p\#S_{2}(p)}

so that
\mathbb{O}=\mathrm{Bind}^{G}_{L}\mathbb{O}_{L}
, and fix
\{\tau_{1}(k)\mid k\in S_{2}(p)\}\subset\mathfrak{h}^{*}
and
\delta\in\mathfrak{h}^{*}
as in Corollary
7.7.6
(i) (and its proof). Thus,
\mathcal{A}_{\delta}^{X}
is identified with the
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O})
-invariants in
\mathcal{A}_{0}^{\widetilde{X}}
.

Proposition 10.5.2
.

For every bimodule
\mathcal{B}\in\mathrm{Unip}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}}(G)
there is a unique subset
S\subseteq S_{2}(p)
and a unique bimodule
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Unip}_{\mathbb{O}_{L}}(L)
such that

\Ind^{G}_{L}[\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\otimes\mathbb{C}(\delta-\sum_{k\in S}\tau_{1}(k),\delta)].

If
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
is unitary, then
\mathcal{B}
is unitary.

Proof.

Let
I=I_{0}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})=I_{\delta}(\mathbb{O})
. By Proposition
B.2.1
,
I
is maximal, so the algebra
\mathcal{A}_{\delta}^{X}
is simple, see Proposition
6.3.3
. Thus by Lemma
4.12.2
,

\HC^{G}(\mathcal{A}_{\delta}^{X})\xrightarrow{\sim}\overline{\HC}^{G}(\mathcal{A}_{\delta}^{X}),

By Corollary
6.5.6
,
\Phi:U(\mathfrak{g})/I\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{A}_{\delta}^{X}
. In particular

\Phi^{*}:\HC^{G}(\mathcal{A}_{\delta}^{X})\xrightarrow{\sim}\HC^{G}(U(\mathfrak{g})/I)).

Hence,
\mathcal{B}
is the image under
\Phi^{*}
of a (unique) irreducible object in
\HC^{G}(\mathcal{A}_{\delta}^{X})
(which we will continue to denote by
\mathcal{B}
).

We will prove the proposition by induction on
|S_{2}(p)|
. If
|S_{2}(p)|=0
, then
\mathbb{O}
is birationally rigid by Proposition
7.6.3
and the assertion is vacuous.

Now suppose
|S_{2}(p)|\geq 1
, and choose
k\in S_{2}(p)
. Recall, see Proposition
7.6.9
, that the set
S_{2}(p)
parameterizes codimension 2 leaves
\mathfrak{L}\subset X
.
Let
M_{k}\subset G
be the Levi subgroup adapted to the leaf
\mathfrak{L}_{k}
, see (
7.5.1
), and let
\mathbb{O}_{M_{k}}=\mathrm{Bind}^{M_{k}}_{L}\mathbb{O}_{L}
. We claim that conditions (a1)-(a4) and (c1)-(c3) hold for
\mathbb{O}
and
\mathfrak{L}_{k}
. Of course, (a1) holds by assumption. By Propositions
7.6.9
and
7.6.10
,
\Sigma_{k}\simeq\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
and

M_{k}\simeq GL(k)\times G(n-k),\qquad\mathbb{O}_{M_{k}}=\{0\}\times\mathbb{O}_{p\#(k)}.

Conditions (a2) and (c1) follow immediately. Condition (a3) follows from Proposition
7.6.7
. Condition (a4) is automatic since
G
is linear classical. Finally, conditions (c2)-(c3) were verified in the proof of Corollary
7.7.6
. Now Proposition
10.4.3
implies

\text{either }\mathcal{B}\simeq\Ind^{G}_{M_{k}}[\mathcal{B}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{C}(\frac{1}{2}\tau_{1}(k),\frac{1}{2}\tau_{1}(k))]\quad\text{or}\quad\mathcal{B}\simeq\Ind^{G}_{M_{k}}[\mathcal{B}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{C}(-\frac{1}{2}\tau_{1}(k),\frac{1}{2}\tau_{1}(k))]

for a uniquely determined irreducible object
\mathcal{B}^{1}\in\overline{\HC}^{M_{k}}(\mathcal{A}_{\delta-\frac{1}{2}\tau_{1}(k)}^{X_{M_{k}}})
. By the induction hypothesis,

\mathcal{B}^{1}\simeq\Ind^{M_{k}}_{L}[\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\otimes\mathbb{C}(\delta-\frac{1}{2}\tau_{1}(k)-\sum_{j\in S^{\prime}}\tau_{1}(j),\delta-\frac{1}{2}\tau_{1}(k))]

for a uniquely determined irreducible object
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\in\overline{\HC}^{L}(\mathcal{A}_{0}^{X_{L}})
and a uniquely determined subset
S^{\prime}\subset S_{2}(p)
not containing
k
. By Corollary
10.2.7
(ii),

\mathcal{B}\simeq\Ind^{G}_{L}[\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\otimes\mathbb{C}(\delta-\sum_{k\in S}\tau_{1}(k),\delta)].

It remains to show that
\mathcal{B}
is unitary if
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
is unitary. If
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
is unitary, then
\mathcal{B}^{1}
is unitary by the induction hypothesis. To show that that
\mathcal{B}
is unitary, we apply Propositions
10.3.1
and
10.3.2
.

First, suppose
\mathcal{B}\simeq\Ind^{G}_{M_{k}}[\mathcal{B}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{C}(\frac{1}{2}\tau_{1}(k),\frac{1}{2}\tau_{1}(k))]
. Condition (i) of Proposition
10.3.2
follows from Lemma
B.2.5
. It remains to exhibit an element
w\in W
such that
w\delta=-\delta
. For this, we choose
w=w_{0}
(the longest element of
W
).

Next, suppose
\mathcal{B}\simeq\Ind^{G}_{M_{k}}[\mathcal{B}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{C}(-\frac{1}{2}\tau_{1}(k),\frac{1}{2}\tau_{1}(k))]
. By Lemmas
B.2.2
,
B.3.1
, and
B.4.1
, the ideal
I_{\delta}(\mathbb{O})\subset U(\mathfrak{g})
is maximal. Thus,
\mathcal{B}
is unitary by Proposition
10.3.1
.
∎

Finally, suppose
G=\mathrm{SO}(8m+4)
,
\mathbb{O}=\mathbb{O}_{(4^{2m},3,1)}
, and
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathbb{O}
is the universal
G
-equivariant cover. Let

L=\mathrm{GL}(2m+1)\times\mathrm{GL}(2m+1),\qquad\mathbb{O}_{L}=\{0\},

so that
\mathbb{O}=\mathrm{Bind}^{G}_{L}\mathbb{O}_{L}
. Fix
\tau_{1}(1),\tau_{1}(2),\delta\in\mathfrak{h}^{*}
as in Corollary
7.7.6
(ii). Thus,
\mathcal{A}_{\delta}^{X}
is identified with the
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O})
-invariants in
\mathcal{A}_{0}^{\widetilde{X}}
and
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{O})\simeq\mathbb{Z}_{2}
.

Proposition 10.5.3
.

The set
\mathrm{Unip}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}}(G)
consists of the following 2 bimodules

\{\Ind^{G}_{L}\mathbb{C}(\delta,\delta),\ \Ind^{G}_{L}\mathbb{C}(\delta-\tau_{1}(1),\delta)\}.

Both are unitary.

Proof.

Let
I=I_{0}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})=I_{\delta}(\mathbb{O})
. By Proposition
B.2.1
,
I
is maximal, so the algebra
\mathcal{A}_{\delta}^{X}
is simple, see Proposition
6.3.3
. Thus by Lemma
4.12.2
,

\HC^{G}(\mathcal{A}_{\delta}^{X})\xrightarrow{\sim}\overline{\HC}^{G}(\mathcal{A}_{\delta}^{X}).

By Corollary
6.5.6
,
\Phi:U(\mathfrak{g})/I\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{A}_{\delta}^{X}
. In particular

\Phi^{*}:\HC^{G}(\mathcal{A}_{\delta}^{X})\xrightarrow{\sim}\HC^{G}(U(\mathfrak{g})/I).

Since
\pi_{1}^{G}(\mathbb{O})\simeq\mathbb{Z}_{2}
, the category
\overline{\HC}^{G}(\mathcal{A}_{\delta}^{X})\simeq\HC(U(\mathfrak{g})/I)
contains two irreducible objects, see Theorem
6.6.2
. So for the first claim it suffices to show that the induced bimodules
\mathcal{B}:=\Ind^{G}_{L}\mathbb{C}(\delta,\delta)
,
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}:=\Ind^{G}_{L}\mathbb{C}(\delta-\tau_{1}(1),\delta)
are irreducible and distinct. Irreducibility is immediate from Corollary
10.2.7
(iii). By Proposition
10.2.6
,

\mathcal{B}_{\dagger}\simeq\operatorname{triv},\qquad\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{\dagger}\simeq\varphi^{G}_{L}(\tau_{1}(1)).

Thus in order to prove that
\mathcal{B}\not\simeq\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
, it is enough to show that
\varphi^{G}_{L}(\tau_{1}(1))
is a nontrivial character of
\pi_{1}^{G}(\mathbb{O})
. Let
\Sigma_{1}^{\times}=\Sigma_{1}-\{0\}
, and let
\mathfrak{S}_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Sigma_{1}
be the minimal resolution. By Lemma
10.2.4
, there is an isomorphism
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{O}}\left(\varphi^{G}_{L}(\tau_{1}(1))\right)|_{\Sigma_{1}^{\times}}\simeq\pi^{*}\mathcal{L}_{G/P}(\tau_{1}(1))|_{\Sigma^{\times}_{1}}
of line bundles on
\Sigma_{1}^{\times}
. And by
7.7.4
,
\pi^{*}\mathcal{L}_{G/P}(\tau_{1}(1))|_{\mathfrak{S}_{1}}\simeq\sigma_{1}(\omega_{1}(1))
, where
\sigma_{1}(\omega_{1}(1))
is the line bundle on
\mathfrak{S}_{1}
corresponding to
\omega_{1}(1)
via the natural identification
\sigma_{1}:\Lambda_{1}\simeq\Pic(\mathfrak{S}_{1})
, see Proposition

4.2.3
. Thus, it suffices to show that
\sigma_{1}(\omega_{1}(1))
restricts to a nontrivial line bundle on
\Sigma^{\times}
. This follows as a special case from the final paragraph of the proof of
10.4.2
(setting
d=2
).

It remains to show that
\mathcal{B}
and
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
are unitary. To prove
\mathcal{B}
is unitary, we use Proposition
10.3.2
. Condition (i) of that proposition follows from Lemma
B.2.5
. It remains to exhibit an element
w\in W
such that
w\delta=-\delta
. For this, we choose
w=w_{0}
(the longest element of
W
). The unitarity of
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
is proved by a similar argument.
∎

10.6.
Unitarity of unipotent bimodules: classical case

Let
G
be a linear classical group, and let
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
be a
G
-equivariant nilpotent cover, maximal in its equivalence class. In this section, we will prove the following result.

Theorem 10.6.1
.

The set
\mathrm{Unip}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}}(G)
consists of unitary bimodules.

The proof will require several preliminary results. The first is an easy consequence of Barbasch’s classification.

Lemma 10.6.2
.

Suppose
\mathbb{O}
is rigid. Then the set
\mathrm{Unip}_{\mathbb{O}}(G)
consists of unitary bimodules.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{B}\in\mathrm{Unip}_{\mathbb{O}}(G)
. By Proposition
6.1.2
,
V(\mathcal{B})=\overline{\mathbb{O}}
, and the ideal
\mathrm{LAnn}(\mathcal{B})=\mathrm{RAnn}(\mathcal{B})=I_{0}(\mathbb{O})\subset U(\mathfrak{g})
is maximal by Lemmas
B.2.2
,
B.3.1
, and
B.4.1
. Thus,
\mathcal{B}
is unitary by
[
Bar89
, Prop 10.6]
and Remark
8.2.4
.
∎

The next two lemmas hold in complete generality. For the first lemma, choose a Levi subgroup
M\subset G
and an
M
-equivariant nilpotent cover
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{M}
such that
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}=\mathrm{Bind}^{G}_{M}\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{M}
.

Lemma 10.6.3
.

Suppose the ideals
I_{0}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{M})
,
I_{0}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})
are maximal and
\mathrm{Unip}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{M}}(M)
